% BEGIN SIGNED_STATE_EVALUATION
\section{The eight-state evaluation and its exact sign decomposition}
The eight inverse states from GF7 give a second, intrinsic four-vector
frame in the original conductor target. This construction uses the actual
opposite-sign pairs in that fibre, rather than selecting four unrelated
vectors. Write $A=u_0$, $B=u_2$, $C=u_3$, $D=u_4$ and
$h(r)=Ar^4+r^3+Br^2+Cr+D$, retaining $A\ne0$.
For each of its four distinct roots $r_j$ choose one of the two values
$a_j^2 h'(r_j)=1$, and write $q_{j,+}=q(r_j,a_j)$ and
$q_{j,-}=q(r_j,-a_j)$ using the full inverse GF7. Put $d_j=h'(r_j)$.
Direct addition and subtraction of GF7 give
\[
 e_j:=\frac{q_{j,+}+q_{j,-}}2=
 \begin{pmatrix}0\\-r_j\\3i d_j\\
 (B-17r_j+Ar_j^2)d_j-2A d_j^2\end{pmatrix},\qquad
 o_j:=\frac{q_{j,+}-q_{j,-}}2=
 a_j\begin{pmatrix}1\\-i d_j\\A d_j^2+2r_jd_j\\
 7ir_j^2d_j-13i d_j^2\end{pmatrix}.
 \tag{SE1}
\]
For example $a_j^{-1}=a_jd_j$, $a_j^{-2}=d_j$,
$a_j^{-3}=a_jd_j^2$, and $a_j^{-4}=d_j^2$; these identities
retain every constant and sign in that addition. Thus SE1 is an exact
reconstruction $q_{j,\pm}=e_j\pm o_j$.

Let $\mathcal F_u=\C^{\{(j,+),(j,-):1\le j\le4\}}$ with its
eight separately marked basis vectors $f_{j,\pm}$, and let
$\mathsf S f_{j,\pm}=f_{j,\mp}$. The two explicit embeddings are
\[
 \iota_+e_j^{\rm coord}=(f_{j,+}+f_{j,-})/2,\qquad
 \iota_-e_j^{\rm coord}=(f_{j,+}-f_{j,-})/2.
 \tag{SE2}
\]
Their images are the $+1$ and $-1$ eigenspaces of $\mathsf S$;
every vector of $\mathcal F_u$ has a unique expression
$\iota_+\alpha+\iota_-\beta$. The factors $1/2$ in SE2 are the
explicit coefficients of these maps, not an unrecorded metric change.
Define the source-coordinate evaluation map by
$\mathcal E f_{j,\pm}=q_{j,\pm}$ and matrices
$E=(e_1\ e_2\ e_3\ e_4)$, $O=(o_1\ o_2\ o_3\ o_4)$.
Then the whole eight-state map is exactly
\[
 \mathcal E(\iota_+\alpha+\iota_-\beta)=E\alpha+O\beta.
 \tag{SE3}
\]

\section{The sign differences are a basis on the literal quartet}
The rank of these two pieces can be proved in the original arithmetic
quartet, without dropping the phases in $a_j$. For this purpose form
the four polynomials
\[
 v(r)=(1,-ih'(r),A h'(r)^2+2rh'(r),
                         7ir^2h'(r)-13ih'(r)^2)^T.
\]
Reduce each polynomial modulo the original $h$ by polynomial division;
this changes no evaluation at a root. Write the remainders as
$v(r)=V(1,r,r^2,r^3)^T$ modulo $h$. Their full coefficient matrix is
\[
\begin{aligned}
 V_{1,*}&=(1,0,0,0),\\
 V_{2,*}&=(-iC,-2iB,-3i,-4iA),\\
 V_{3,*}&=(AC^2-9D,\ 4ABC-8AD-7C,\
             -16A^2D+4AB^2-2AC-5B,\ -8A^2C+4AB-3),\\
 V_{4,*}&=(-13iC^2+20iD/A,\ -52iBC+76iD+20iC/A,\
             208iAD-52iB^2+5iC+20iB/A,\
             104iAC-66iB+20i/A).
\end{aligned}\tag{SE4}
\]
This is polynomial division by a quartic with its retained leading
coefficient $A$; the denominators in the last row have not been removed.
For the literal $u_\rho$ in GF20, substitution and expansion of the
three-by-three determinant remaining after the first row give
\[
 \det V=448\delta^2\gamma^2
                   (2\delta^2\gamma^2-\delta^2+\gamma^2)>0.
 \tag{SE5}
\]
The inequality follows since the last factor is
$\gamma^2+\delta^2(2\gamma^2-1)>0$ for $\gamma>2$.
The exact polynomial remainder and determinant calculations in SE4--5
are reproduced by \texttt{derive\_signed\_evaluation.py}; they use
rational function arithmetic, not a sampled determinant sign.

Write $\Delta=\det(r_j^{n})_{0\le n\le3,1\le j\le4}$ in the
same original marking, and retain
\[
 \chi=A^2\Delta\prod_{j=1}^4a_j\in\{1,-1\}.
 \tag{SE6}
\]
Indeed $\prod_jh'(r_j)=A^4\Delta^2$ by multiplying all pair
differences; the six sign reversals have positive product. Squaring
SE6 and using $a_j^2=1/h'(r_j)$ proves $\chi^2=1$. No particular
choice of its sign has been imposed. Evaluating SE4 at the roots gives
$O=V(r_j^n)_{n,j}\diag(a_1,a_2,a_3,a_4)$, hence
\[
 \det O=\frac{\chi}{A^2}\det V
   =1792\chi\delta^2\gamma^2
                   (2\delta^2\gamma^2-\delta^2+\gamma^2)\ne0
 \tag{SE7}
\]
on the literal quartet, where $A=-1/2$ is retained. Thus the four
opposite-sign differences are an actual basis in the original source
coordinates for every admitted quartet, for either choice of each sign.

The pair means have rank exactly three. Their first coordinate is zero.
For a direct lower bound on rank, let $E_{\mathrm{pol}}$ be the coefficient
matrix of the four polynomials in the first column expression of SE1,
again reduced modulo the original $h$. The minor with rows $y,z,w$
and columns corresponding to $r,r^2,r^3$ is
\[
 6i\bigl(64A^3D-16A^2B^2-22A^2C+89AB-30\bigr)
 =6i\bigl(16\delta^2\gamma^2-35\delta^2+35\gamma^2\bigr)\ne0.
 \tag{SE8}
\]
The equality follows by substituting GF20. Positivity of the last
real factor follows from
$16\delta^2\gamma^2+35(\gamma^2-\delta^2)>0$ under the original
bounds $0<\delta<1/2$, $\gamma>2$. Evaluation at four distinct
roots multiplies $E_{\mathrm{pol}}$ by the invertible Vandermonde,
so its rank and the rank of $E$ are both exactly three.

The remaining even kernel line is also explicit. The full coefficient
matrix of the means has rows
\[
 (0,0,0,0),\quad(0,-1,0,0),\quad(3iC,6iB,9i,12iA),\quad
 (w_0,w_1,w_2,w_3),
\]
where
\[
\begin{aligned}
 w_0&=-2AC^2+BC+71D,&w_1&=-8ABC+12AD+2B^2+54C,\\
 w_2&=32A^2D-8AB^2+AC+40B,&w_3&=16A^2C-6AB+20.
\end{aligned}\tag{SE8a}
\]
For $W=(r_j^n)_{n,j}$ define
\[
 t=(3w_3-4Aw_2,\ 0,\ 4Aw_0-Cw_3,\ Cw_2-3w_0)^T,
 \qquad \lambda=W^{-1}t.
 \tag{SE8b}
\]
The first coordinate of $t$ is nonzero by SE8. Substitution in
the three nonzero rows of SE8a gives zero: the last two equations
are the cross-product cancellation between $(C,3,4A)$ and
$(w_0,w_2,w_3)$; the second coordinate of $t$ is zero. Consequently
\[
 \ker E=\C\lambda,\qquad
 \lambda_j=
 \frac{t_0(C+Br_j+r_j^2+Ar_j^3)+t_2(1+Ar_j)+t_3A}{h'(r_j)}.
 \tag{SE8c}
\]
To verify the last formula, the $j$th row of $W^{-1}$ is the
coefficient row of $h(r)/((r-r_j)h'(r_j))$. Polynomial division
gives the four coefficients
$h'(r_j)^{-1}(C+Br_j+r_j^2+Ar_j^3,B+r_j+Ar_j^2,1+Ar_j,A)$.
Pairing with $t$, whose $r$ coefficient is zero, gives SE8c.
Thus the one even direction lost by evaluation is calculated
in the original labelled coordinates, not inferred to be the
constant direction from the separate tetrahedral altitude map.

\section{The entire eight-state kernel and the original-metric return}
Because $O$ is invertible on the literal quartet, SE3 gives the complete
kernel, with no unspecified missing sign or root state:
\[
 \ker\mathcal E=
 \{\iota_+\alpha-\iota_-O^{-1}E\alpha:\alpha\in\C^4\},
 \qquad
 \mathcal E\,\iota_-O^{-1}=I_{\C^4}.
 \tag{SE9}
\]
The inverse here is exactly $\operatorname{adj}(O)/\det O$, with
SE1 and SE7 specifying numerator and nonzero denominator. The kernel
therefore has dimension four. Its intersection with the even subspace
has dimension one by SE8; its intersection with the odd subspace is zero
by SE7. These assertions follow by solving $E\alpha+O\beta=0$;
they do not identify the even kernel with a different tetrahedral
constant direction by analogy.

The receiving map to the actual conductor target is
$\mathcal E_W=\Psi\mathcal E$, and its source partner is
$\mathcal E_U=\Phi\mathcal E$, with the exact original maps FC32.
Thus
\[
 T_A\mathcal E_U=\mathcal E_W,\quad
 \ker\mathcal E_U=\ker\mathcal E_W=\ker\mathcal E,\quad
 \mathcal E_W\,\iota_-O^{-1}\Psi^{-1}=I_{\mathcal W}.
 \tag{SE10}
\]
The last identity constructs a complete section into the sign-odd
four-plane of the actual eight-state space. Changing which partner
is named $+$ changes the relevant column of $O$ and of $\iota_-$
by the same sign; it leaves this section unchanged. Permuting the
four labels likewise permutes these two maps inversely, so no root
label is implicitly forgotten.

The four original target vectors $\Psi o_j$ have the full Gram
$O^*GO$, with $G$ from FC56--56a. Consequently
\[
 \det(O^*GO)=1792^2\delta^4\gamma^4
       (2\delta^2\gamma^2-\delta^2+\gamma^2)^2\det G>0.
 \tag{SE11}
\]
This retains the entire original Gamma measure and all its cross
terms. Their source Gram is $O^*G_UO$; therefore the exact ratio is
\[
 \frac{\det(O^*GO)}{\det(O^*G_UO)}
 =\frac{\det G}{\det G_U}=|\det A_{3,s}|^2.
 \tag{SE12}
\]
The first equality cancels the same nonzero explicitly calculated
$|\det O|^2$ on both sides, while the second is FC30 with the
unchanged $K$ factors. This is a basis constructed from the actual
eight signed inverse states and transported through the original
linear conductor. Its full sign-odd part is invertible; the rank-three
loss in the pair means and the four-dimensional graph kernel SE9
record exactly what the sign-forgetting evaluation removes.

\section{An invertible total state map retaining both labels and evaluation}
The graph kernel can be resolved by retaining the original root-label
vector, rather than discarding the nonlinear evaluation. Define
\[
 \mathcal J_U(\iota_+\alpha+\iota_-\beta)
       =(X\alpha,\ \Phi(E\alpha+O\beta))\in\mathcal U\oplus\mathcal U,
 \qquad
 \mathcal J_W(\iota_+\alpha+\iota_-\beta)
       =(Y\alpha,\ \Psi(E\alpha+O\beta))\in\mathcal W\oplus\mathcal W.
 \tag{SE13}
\]
Both are isomorphisms of the complete eight-dimensional state space.
For the target, the entire inverse is
\[
 \mathcal J_W^{-1}(p_1,p_2)=
 \iota_+Y^{-1}p_1+
 \iota_-O^{-1}(\Psi^{-1}p_2-EY^{-1}p_1).
 \tag{SE14}
\]
Substitution in SE13 proves each inverse composition. Replacing
$Y,\Psi$ by $X,\Phi$ proves the source inverse. Moreover the
original conductor itself supplies the exact map between these
total state spaces:
\[
 (T_A|_{\mathcal U}\oplus T_A|_{\mathcal U})\,\mathcal J_U
      =\mathcal J_W.
 \tag{SE15}
\]
This is direct substitution of $T_AX=Y$ and $T_A\Phi=\Psi$.
In particular, the two original sign states of root $j$ become
$(y_j,\Psi q_{j,+})$ and $(y_j,\Psi q_{j,-})$ in the same
target direct sum. The root coordinate and the nonlinear input
are both retained.

The exact action of signed root permutations on this object includes
the terms that evaluation alone would lose. For a permutation $\pi$
and signs $\sigma_j\in\{1,-1\}$, let
$g f_{j,t}=f_{\pi(j),\sigma_jt}$, let $\Pi e_j=e_{\pi(j)}$,
and let $R e_j=\sigma_j e_{\pi(j)}$. Thus
$g\iota_+=\iota_+\Pi$ and $g\iota_-=\iota_-R$.
In coordinates $(\alpha,z)$ for the target pair $(Y\alpha,\Psi z)$,
SE13--14 give the full transported action
\[
 \widehat g=
 \begin{pmatrix}
  \Pi&0\\ E\Pi-ORO^{-1}E&ORO^{-1}
 \end{pmatrix}.
 \tag{SE16}
\]
Indeed $z=E\alpha+O\beta$ gives
$z'=E\Pi\alpha+OR\beta
=ORO^{-1}z+(E\Pi-ORO^{-1}E)\alpha$.
The off-diagonal block is therefore evaluated exactly, with all
coefficients given in SE1 and SE7. Since SE16 is
$\mathcal J_W g\mathcal J_W^{-1}$ in the displayed coordinates,
it obeys the group law for every pair of signed permutations.
The actual monodromy subgroup is determined in the signed-root
continuation; this formula retains its full action as well.
The source uses the identical block matrix in coordinates
$(X\alpha,\Phi z)$, so SE15 intertwines every such action.

Every nontrivial even sign change has a nonzero lower-left block,
and its rank is exact. Put $F=O^{-1}E$. The image of $E$ is the
entire first-coordinate-zero hyperplane by SE8, and the first row
of $O$ is $(a_1,a_2,a_3,a_4)$. Hence
$\operatorname{im}F=\{x:\sum_j a_jx_j=0\}$.
Each $a_j$ is nonzero. Projection of this hyperplane onto any
proper subset of coordinates is surjective: choose one omitted
coordinate to solve the single displayed equation. For a diagonal
sign action $D_J$ negating the subset $J$, the lower-left block
of SE16 is $O(I-D_J)F$. Therefore
\[
 \operatorname{rank}\bigl(E-OD_JO^{-1}E\bigr)
 =\begin{cases}2,&|J|=2,\\3,&|J|=4.\end{cases}
 \tag{SE18}
\]
For two coordinates this is the just-proved surjective projection;
for all four it is twice a rank-three matrix. These are all
nonidentity elements of the even-sign kernel. Thus the extra
label coordinate in SE13 retains a necessary, quantitatively
specified mixing term. SE16 supplies the full action including it.

Put the original orthogonal direct-sum Gamma norms on the two target
copies and the original quotient direct-sum norms on the two source
copies. In the coordinates just used their Grams are
$\diag(G_y,G)$ and $\diag(G_x,G_U)$, respectively. This choice
retains each original mass and cross Gram within its copy; it does
not identify either $\mathcal J$ with an isometry from a previously
assigned norm on the abstract finite state space. For $L=T_A|_U$
and every $0\le r\le8$, the actual inverse-exterior norm is
\[
 \left\|\wedge^r(L\oplus L)^{-1}\right\|
 =\max_{\substack{p+q=r\\0\le p,q\le4}}
          \|\wedge^pL^{-1}\|\,\|\wedge^qL^{-1}\|.
 \tag{SE17}
\]
To prove it, the orthogonal direct sum decomposes its $r$th exterior
space into the orthogonal summands
$\wedge^p\mathcal W\otimes\wedge^q\mathcal W$, $p+q=r$.
The map on each summand is the tensor product of the displayed
two exterior inverses; in singular bases its norm is the product
of their largest singular values. The norm of a block diagonal
map on an orthogonal direct sum is the maximum of block norms,
proving SE17. Each factor is the original four-plane $A_{3,s}$
inverse appearing in audit (11). This proves the full eight-state
return to that bound without deleting the rank-three mean part,
the four root labels, either factor sign, or the mixing block.

% BEGIN SIGNED_MONODROMY
\providecommand{\Disc}{\operatorname{Disc}}
\providecommand{\sgn}{\operatorname{sgn}}
\providecommand{\Aut}{\operatorname{Aut}}
% Root-reviewed complete independent signed-root monodromy proof.
The next calculation determines the symmetry of the actual cover GF16,
with the original ES map and four marked labels retained \cite{ES488}.

\section{The actual cover and its eight labelled states}
For $u=(u_0,u_2,u_3,u_4)$ put
\[
 H_u(U,V)=u_0U^4+U^3V+u_2U^2V^2+u_3UV^3+u_4V^4,
 \qquad h_u(x)=H_u(x,1),\qquad A=u_0.
\]
The original cubic coefficient remains one throughout. Let
\[
 \mathcal B=\{u:A\Disc(H_u)\ne0\},\qquad
 R=\C[u_0,u_2,u_3,u_4,(A\Disc H_u)^{-1}].
\]
Over this domain the original source is isomorphic to the finite
cover with coordinate algebra
\begin{equation}
 S=R[r,a]/(h_u(r),\ a^2h_u'(r)-1).\tag{SM1}
\end{equation}
In the unchanged original source coordinates its inverse is
\begin{align}
 y&=-r-i/a,\nonumber\\
 z&=A/a^3+2r/a+3i/a^2,\nonumber\\
 w&=7ir^2/a+(u_2-17r+Ar^2)/a^2-13i/a^3-2A/a^4.
 \tag{SM2}
\end{align}
These formulas are obtained by factoring
$H_u=(aU+bV)(cU^3+dU^2V+eUV^2+fV^3)$, setting $r=-b/a$,
and retaining the resultant equation
$C(-b,a)=a^2h_u'(r)=1$. Thus SM1 describes the actual inverse
states; its two signs are two distinct original source points.

For completeness, $R[r]/(h_u)$ is free of rank four because $A$
is a unit and $A^{-1}h_u$ is monic. The discriminant condition
and the Sylvester Bezout identity make $h_u'(r)$ a unit. The
second equation of SM1 is therefore the monic relation
$a^2=h_u'(r)^{-1}$, and $S$ is free of rank eight over $R$.
The Jacobian of the two equations with respect to $(r,a)$ has
determinant $2a h_u'(r)^2$, a unit. In a sufficiently small target
neighbourhood the four simple roots and their two nonzero square
roots therefore give eight separate holomorphic inverse charts.
This provides the local path-lifting property used below.

Both the base and the actual cover are path connected. The base
is the complement in $\C^4$ of the zero set of the nonzero
polynomial $A\Disc H_u$. The actual source is the complement
of the zero set of its polynomial pullback under the original map
$P$. This pullback is nonzero, since the eight states of any point
in $\mathcal B$ exist by SM1--2. To connect two points in either
complement, restrict its defining polynomial to the complex line
through them. Its value at the endpoints is nonzero, so the
restricted polynomial is not identically zero and has only
finitely many zeros. A path in the complex line parameter from
zero to one, with small detours around these finitely many points,
connects the endpoints within the complement. This proves the
asserted connectedness without an unproved topological
classification of the base.

\section{The exact upper bound from the retained product invariant}
At a base point order the four roots as $r_1,r_2,r_3,r_4$ and
choose one of the two actual values $a_j$ at each root. Denote
the eight states by $(j,+)$ and $(j,-)$, corresponding respectively
to $a_j$ and $-a_j$ in SM2. During continuation the four roots
remain distinct, and their scalings remain nonzero. Put
\[
 \Delta=\prod_{j<k}(r_k-r_j),\qquad
 \chi=A^2\Delta\prod_{j=1}^4a_j.
\]
The complete derivative product is
\[
 \prod_{j=1}^4h_u'(r_j)
 =A^4\prod_{j=1}^4\prod_{k\ne j}(r_j-r_k)
 =A^4\Delta^2.
\]
There are six pairs, so the sign from reversing each pair is
$(-1)^6=1$. Consequently
\begin{equation}
 \chi^2=1.\tag{SM3}
\end{equation}
The continuous value $\chi$ is therefore constant along every
ordered lift of a path. Its initial value may be $1$ or $-1$;
neither value has been discarded.

A loop in $\mathcal B$ induces a root permutation $\pi\in S_4$
and signs $\sigma_j\in\{1,-1\}$ such that
$r_j\mapsto r_{\pi(j)}$ and $a_j\mapsto\sigma_j a_{\pi(j)}$.
The negative state continues as the negative of the positive one,
by uniqueness in the equation $a^2h'(r)=1$. The exact action is
therefore
\begin{equation}
 (j,\epsilon)\longmapsto(\pi(j),\sigma_j\epsilon),
 \qquad \epsilon\in\{1,-1\}.\tag{SM4}
\end{equation}
The final Vandermonde is $\sgn(\pi)\Delta$. Applying the
constancy of SM3 gives the necessary relation
\begin{equation}
 \prod_{j=1}^4\sigma_j=\sgn(\pi).\tag{SM5}
\end{equation}
Thus the monodromy is contained in the explicitly defined group
\begin{equation}
 G=\{(\sigma,\pi)\in\{\pm1\}^4\rtimes S_4:
                       \prod_j\sigma_j=\sgn(\pi)\}.
 \tag{SM6}
\end{equation}
For each permutation exactly eight sign choices satisfy SM5, so
$|G|=8\cdot24=192$. As signed permutation matrices its elements
have determinant $(\prod_j\sigma_j)\sgn(\pi)=1$.

\section{Explicit local exchanges realizing the full group}
Use the real roots
\[
 (r_{\mathrm T},r_{\mathrm E},r_{\mathrm M},r_{\mathrm N})
 =(7,9,11,13),\qquad A=-1/40.
\]
Their sum is forty; hence the cubic coefficient of
$A\prod(x-r_j)$ is exactly one. The base target is
\begin{equation}
 u_*^{\rm real}=(-1/40,-59/4,95,-9009/40)\in\mathcal B.
 \tag{SM7}
\end{equation}
For the next calculation index the real order as
$s_1=7,s_2=9,s_3=11,s_4=13$; these indices refer respectively
to the unchanged labels $\mathrm T,\mathrm E,\mathrm M,\mathrm N$.
The four derivatives are
\[
 (h'(s_1),h'(s_2),h'(s_3),h'(s_4))
       =(6/5,-2/5,2/5,-6/5).
\]
Choose the exact base scalings
\begin{equation}
 (a_{\mathrm T},a_{\mathrm E},a_{\mathrm M},a_{\mathrm N})
 =\left(\sqrt{5/6},-i\sqrt{5/2},-\sqrt{5/2},i\sqrt{5/6}\right),
 \tag{SM8}
\end{equation}
where the real square roots displayed are positive. Each satisfies
the original equation in SM1.

For each adjacent pair $(s_j,s_{j+1})$, let
$c=(s_j+s_{j+1})/2$ and $d=1$. Keeping the other two roots fixed,
take the explicit path
\begin{equation}
 s_j(\theta)=c-d e^{i\theta},\qquad
 s_{j+1}(\theta)=c+d e^{i\theta},\qquad0\le\theta\le\pi.
 \tag{SM9}
\end{equation}
The pair sum remains $2c$, so the total root sum and the original
coefficient $A=-1/40$ are unchanged. The two moving roots remain
distinct. The closed disk of radius one about $c$ contains no
other root, so no collision with a fixed root occurs. At the
endpoint the pair is interchanged. The unordered roots and all
polynomial coefficients consequently return to the same target;
SM9 is a loop in the actual base $\mathcal B$.

We compute its signs without assuming a braid-group presentation.
For a fixed root $s_k$ outside the pair disk, the moving contribution
to $h'(s_k)$ is
\[
 (s_k-s_j(\theta))(s_k-s_{j+1}(\theta))
       =(s_k-c)^2-d^2e^{2i\theta}.
\]
Its values lie in the disk with positive real centre $(s_k-c)^2$
and strictly smaller radius $d^2$. This disk is in the right
half-plane and has a single continuous logarithm. After the loop
that logarithm returns to its initial value. Therefore neither
scaling at a fixed root changes sign.

For a moving root, its difference from the other moving root turns
by exactly $\pi$. Each of its two differences from an outside
root lies in a disk not containing zero, with both endpoint values
real and of the same sign. A logarithm on the corresponding left
or right half-plane shows that the net imaginary part of each of
those logarithm differences is zero. The argument change of the
complete moving derivative is thus exactly $\pi$. Its inverse
square root changes phase by $-\pi/2$, with its magnitude taking
the new root's required magnitude. By the explicit choices SM8,
the resulting permutation is the four-cycle
\begin{equation}
 \beta_j=(j+\quad(j+1)+\quad j-\quad(j+1)-).
 \tag{SM10}
\end{equation}
Equivalently, in the four retained Time labels the three generators
are
\begin{equation}
 \begin{split}
 \beta_1&=(\mathrm T+\ \mathrm E+\ \mathrm T-\ \mathrm E-),\\
 \beta_2&=(\mathrm E+\ \mathrm M+\ \mathrm E-\ \mathrm M-),\\
 \beta_3&=(\mathrm M+\ \mathrm N+\ \mathrm M-\ \mathrm N-).
 \end{split}\tag{SM11}
\end{equation}
Every sign and every fixed state has been determined by the path.

The square $\beta_j^2$ flips exactly the two adjacent root signs.
These three pair flips generate all even sign patterns: as vectors
over $\mathbb F_2$ they are
$(1,1,0,0)$, $(0,1,1,0)$ and $(0,0,1,1)$, which are independent
and span the dimension-three subspace with coordinate sum zero.
The projections of the three $\beta_j$ are the three adjacent
transpositions, and they generate all of $S_4$ by successively
moving entries to any prescribed positions. Thus the group
generated by these actual loops contains all eight elements in
the sign kernel and projects onto all 24 permutations. It has
at least $8\cdot24=192$ elements. The universal upper bound SM6
has exactly 192 elements, proving equality:
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\operatorname{Mon}(P^{-1}(\mathcal B)/\mathcal B)=G.}
 \tag{SM12}
\end{equation}
This proof establishes the entire group without an unproved claim
that every base loop is a braid word.

\section{Transport from the literal original quartet}
Start with the original marking
\[
 (\rho_{\mathrm M},\rho_{\mathrm N},\rho_{\mathrm T},\rho_{\mathrm E})
 =\left(\tfrac12+\delta+i\gamma,\tfrac12+\delta-i\gamma,
        \tfrac12-\delta+i\gamma,\tfrac12-\delta-i\gamma\right),
 \qquad0<\delta<\tfrac12,\quad\gamma>2.
\]
Its target is $H_\rho=-\frac12\prod(U-\rho_jV)$, with the
original cubic coefficient one. The following two paths give a
fully explicit base transport to SM7. First, for $0\le t\le1$,
put
\begin{equation}
 \begin{array}{ll}
 r_{\mathrm M}(t)=\tfrac12+(1-t)(\delta+i\gamma)+t,
 &r_{\mathrm N}(t)=\tfrac12+(1-t)(\delta-i\gamma)+3t,\\
 r_{\mathrm T}(t)=\tfrac12+(1-t)(-\delta+i\gamma)-3t,
 &r_{\mathrm E}(t)=\tfrac12+(1-t)(-\delta-i\gamma)-t.
 \end{array}\tag{SM13}
\end{equation}
Their sum is always two, so $A=-1/2$ throughout this segment.
Roots with different original imaginary signs have different
imaginary parts for $t<1$. Roots with the same original imaginary
sign have real difference $2\delta(1-t)+4t$, which is positive.
At $t=1$ the four real roots are $(3/2,7/2,-5/2,-1/2)$ and
are distinct. Thus the entire segment remains in $\mathcal B$.

Next translate all four roots by $19t/2$, $0\le t\le1$, and
put $A(t)=-1/(2+38t)$. Their differences remain fixed, their sum
is $2+38t\ne0$, and the retained original cubic coefficient is
again one. At its endpoint the marked roots are
$(11,13,7,9)$, namely SM7 in the original Time order. This
second segment also lies entirely in $\mathcal B$.

The actual signs transport by the original equations, not by
discarding a branch. An exact formula along either differentiable
segment is
\begin{equation}
 a_j(t)=a_j(0)\exp\left[-\frac12\int_0^t
 \left(\frac{A'(s)}{A(s)}+
 \sum_{k\ne j}\frac{r_j'(s)-r_k'(s)}{r_j(s)-r_k(s)}\right)ds\right].
 \tag{SM14}
\end{equation}
The integrand has no singularity on these paths. Differentiating
shows that $a_j(t)^2A(t)\prod_{k\ne j}(r_j(t)-r_k(t))$ is
constant, and its initial value is one. Hence SM14 is precisely
the lifted inverse branch. Both initial signs lift separately.
Using SM8 as the signs at the real endpoint defines their exact
transported signs at the original quartet; any other initial
sign convention conjugates SM11 by a diagonal sign permutation.
It does not change SM12, because the determinant-one subgroup
is invariant under that conjugation.

Conjugating each loop SM9 by the explicitly constructed path
SM13 and its translation segment gives based loops at the literal
quartet with the same marked-root action. This transport concerns
the auxiliary quartic targets of the fixed nonlinear map. It does
not assert that intermediate roots are arithmetic zeta zeros or
that an original conductor period is moved along these paths.

\section{Why this group is not the Weyl group of type $D_4$}
With composition acting first by the rightmost generator, the
product $\beta_1\beta_2\beta_3$ is the eight-cycle
\begin{equation}
 (\mathrm T+\ \mathrm E+\ \mathrm M+\ \mathrm N+\
   \mathrm T-\ \mathrm E-\ \mathrm M-\ \mathrm N-).
 \tag{SM15}
\end{equation}
This follows by applying the three four-cycles SM11 to each
state in order. It has order eight.

For comparison, the reflections in the roots
$\{\pm e_j\pm e_k:j\ne k\}$ generate precisely the signed
permutations with an even total number of sign changes. Indeed
the reflection in $e_j-e_k$ swaps those coordinates, and the
reflection in $e_j+e_k$ swaps them with both signs changed.
Their compositions generate every permutation and every pair
sign flip, and each such reflection has even total sign product.
This proves the usual signed-permutation description of the
Weyl group $W(D_4)$ directly:
\[
 W(D_4)=\{(\sigma,\pi):\prod_j\sigma_j=1\}.
\]
This group has no element of order eight. An underlying
permutation of four coordinates can have cycle lengths only
$1,2,3,4$. A signed cycle of length $m$ has order $m$ if its
sign product is positive and $2m$ if its sign product is
negative, as follows by applying the cycle $m$ times to each
coordinate. With no length-four cycle, the available orders
combine to divisors of four or six. With a length-four cycle,
the even total sign condition makes its sign product positive,
so its order is four. Thus order eight never occurs in $W(D_4)$,
whereas SM15 occurs in $G$. The groups are therefore not even
abstractly isomorphic, despite their equal order 192.

\section{Exact deck groups}
Let $\tau$ denote simultaneous reversal of all four root signs,
$\tau(j,\epsilon)=(j,-\epsilon)$. The centralizer of $G$ in
the full permutation group of the eight inverse states is
\begin{equation}
 C_{S_8}(G)=\{1,\tau\}.\tag{SM16}
\end{equation}
Here is a complete proof. Fix one state, say $(1,+)$. Its
stabilizer in $G$ also fixes $(1,-)$. No state at another root
is fixed by that stabilizer: a pair sign flip involving that
root and one of the other two roots different from root 1
belongs to the stabilizer and moves that state. Hence the
stabilizer has exactly the two fixed states $(1,+),(1,-)$.
Any permutation $c$ commuting with $G$ must send $(1,+)$ to
one of these fixed states. The group $G$ is transitive on all
eight states (permutations move root labels and pair flips
change either chosen sign), so the image of this one state
determines $c$ everywhere through $c(gx)=g c(x)$. The first
choice gives the identity and the second gives $\tau$.
Both commute with every signed permutation, proving SM16.

A deck transformation of the actual connected cover restricts
on the base fibre to a permutation commuting with monodromy:
apply the deck transformation to a lifted loop and use uniqueness
of path lifting in the local charts of SM1. Its restriction is
injective as a function of the deck transformation. Indeed a
transformation fixing one fibre point fixes the lift of every
path starting there; connectedness, or the explicit path-lifting
description, reaches the entire cover. Thus SM16 is an upper
bound of two for the actual deck group.

Both transformations are realized. The nontrivial one is the
original rational factor-sign involution, in unchanged source
coordinates,
\begin{equation}
 \Sigma(a,y,z,w)=\left(-a,\ y+\frac{2i}{a},\
 \frac{6i}{a^2}-z,\
 w-\frac{14iy^2}{a}+\frac{28y}{a^2}+\frac{40i}{a^3}\right).
 \tag{SM17}
\end{equation}
To check it directly in the inverse model, keep the same $r,u$
in SM2 and replace $a$ by $-a$. Its new $y$ is $y+2i/a$ and
its new $z$ is $6i/a^2-z$. Expanding the two expressions for
$w$ gives exactly SM17. It follows from this same operation
that $\Sigma^2=1$ and $P\Sigma=P$ on $a\ne0$. Since $u_0=ac$
is nonzero on $\mathcal B$, every point of its preimage has
$a\ne0$. SM17 is regular on the entire cover and realizes
$\tau$. The exact cover deck group is consequently
\begin{equation}
 \Aut_{\mathcal B}(P^{-1}(\mathcal B))=\{1,\Sigma\}\cong C_2.
 \tag{SM18}
\end{equation}

The same two elements are all rational birational deck
transformations of the original polynomial map. To justify the
passage from rational maps, write $K_0=\C(u_0,u_2,u_3,u_4)$
and $L_0=\C(a,y,z,w)$ with the embedding defined by $P$.
SM1 makes $[L_0:K_0]=8$. The actual ring $S$ is also the
localization
\[
 \C[a,y,z,w,(P_0\Disc(H_{P(q)}))^{-1}],
\]
so it is integrally closed: a localization of a polynomial
unique-factorization ring has this property, since a reduced
fraction integral over it has denominator dividing a power of
its relatively prime numerator and therefore has unit denominator.
Since $S$ is finite integral over $R$, it is the full integral
closure of $R$ in $L_0$. In fact any element of $L_0$ integral
over $R$ is integral over $S$ by the same monic polynomial,
and integrally closedness puts it in $S$.
Every $K_0$-automorphism of $L_0$ preserves this integral closure,
and hence extends to a regular deck transformation on the actual
cover. SM18 therefore bounds it by those same two elements;
SM17 realizes both. Thus
\begin{equation}
 \Aut_{K_0}(L_0)=\{1,\Sigma\}.\tag{SM19}
\end{equation}
This statement is about birational automorphisms fixing the
target field, not arbitrary rational maps of the source.

Finally the only global polynomial deck automorphism $D$ of $\C^4$
satisfying $P\circ D=P$ is the identity. Such an automorphism would
restrict to SM19. The nonidentity element cannot extend
polynomially across $a=0$, because its second coordinate is
$(ay+2i)/a$, whose numerator is not divisible by $a$.
Equality on the dense open set $a\ne0$ forces equality as
rational functions, so there is no alternative polynomial
extension. This proves the assertion for the whole original
source, while retaining the nontrivial regular deck
transformation on the precise eight-sheet domain. This does not
classify polynomial automorphisms satisfying the different equation
$P\circ D=D\circ P$; that centralizer is not asserted to be trivial.

\section{Receiving the group in the fixed original conductor target}
For the fixed original receiving isomorphism
$\Psi:\C^4\to W=\cP_3$, with all original moments and Gamma
norms retained, set $P_W=\Psi P\Psi^{-1}$. Its eight-sheet
base is exactly $\Psi(\mathcal B)$. Sending a target path
$u(t)$ to $\Psi u(t)$ and each source state to its image under
$\Psi$ gives a bijection of all path lifts, with inverse
$\Psi^{-1}$. Thus its monodromy is exactly SM12 on the eight
transported marked states, and its nontrivial rational deck
transformation is exactly $\Psi\Sigma\Psi^{-1}$. The cover
deck group is $C_2$ and its global polynomial deck group is
trivial by the same conjugacy.

These are statements about the constructed nonlinear cover on
the original target. They do not enlarge the symmetry group
of a fixed Segre pairing or assert that the original conductor
itself is a nonlinear map. The original linear conductor
remains the exact invertible intertwiner proved in
FC26--35 above. All four Time labels and both signs over each
are present in the monodromy action just calculated.
% END SIGNED_MONODROMY

% BEGIN SIGNED_FRAME_CONNECTION
The four signed inverse states are those of the original ES--Fable
map \cite{ES488}, with their exact inverse coordinates proved in
GF7 and GF19--28 above. This note differentiates their actual odd frame in
the unchanged coordinates $(a,y,z,w)$. It then transports the
result through one fixed original conductor receiving map. No
moving conductor, diagonal replacement of the frame, or change
of its original Gamma norm is used.

\section{The original odd frame and its derivative}
Fix $\gamma>2$ and let $0<\delta<1/2$. Order the labels as
$(\mathrm M,\mathrm N,\mathrm T,\mathrm E)$, with corresponding
$(\varepsilon,\eta)=(1,1),(1,-1),(-1,1),(-1,-1)$. Retain
\begin{equation}
 r_j=\tfrac12+\varepsilon_j\delta+i\eta_j\gamma,
 \qquad A=-\tfrac12,\qquad
 d_j=h_\rho'(r_j)=4\delta\gamma(\varepsilon_j\gamma-i\eta_j\delta),
 \qquad a_j^2=d_j^{-1}.\tag{SF1}
\end{equation}
Each of the two sign choices is retained; take either continuous
choice on the displayed positive interval when differentiating.
The exact half-difference of its two original source points is
\begin{equation}
 o_j=a_j\,v_j,\qquad
 v_j=\begin{pmatrix}
 1\\-i d_j\\-d_j^2/2+2r_jd_j\\7ir_j^2d_j-13id_j^2
 \end{pmatrix},\qquad
 N=(v_1\ v_2\ v_3\ v_4),\quad
 O=N\diag(a_1,a_2,a_3,a_4).\tag{SF2}
\end{equation}
For example these expressions follow from the complete inverse
by using $a^{-1}=ad$, $a^{-2}=d$, $a^{-3}=ad^2$,
$a^{-4}=d^2$ and subtracting the values at $a$ and $-a$.
All constants, including $A=-1/2$, remain in SF2.

The determinant and branch logarithmic derivatives are
\begin{align}
 \det N&=-28672\delta^4\gamma^4(\delta^2+\gamma^2)
                         (\gamma^2+(2\gamma^2-1)\delta^2),\nonumber\\
 \frac{a_j'}{a_j}&=-\frac1{2\delta}
       +\frac{i\eta_j}{2(\varepsilon_j\gamma-i\eta_j\delta)}.
 \tag{SF3}
\end{align}
The determinant is obtained from the four explicit columns SF2
by expansion; the independent coefficient identity and exact
polynomial numerator certificate are supplied below. Every factor
displayed in this determinant is nonzero on the original interval.
The second equation follows by differentiating $a_j^2d_j=1$;
it is independent of the retained choice of sign.
Writing $L=\diag(a_j'/a_j)$, ordinary product differentiation gives
the exact original-coordinate connection
\begin{equation}
 \Omega_\delta=O'O^{-1}=(N'+NL)N^{-1}.\tag{SF4}
\end{equation}
In particular the square roots and their signs cancel from the
connection. This is an identity of the original matrices, not an
identification of the signed source points with each other.

\section{Every entry of the rational matrix}
For readable formulas only, abbreviate
$t=\delta^2$, $g=\gamma^2$, $Q=t+g$ and $F=g+(2g-1)t$.
These abbreviations do not change any coordinate of the four-vector.
The complete matrix is
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\quad\Omega_\delta=
             \frac{B(\delta^2,\gamma^2)}{448\delta QF}.\quad}
 \tag{SF5}
\end{equation}
Here all sixteen entries of the polynomial matrix $B(t,g)$ are
as follows, in the original row and column order $(a,y,z,w)$:
\begin{align*}
 B_{11}&=-224(5t^2g-2t^2+tg^2+3tg+g^2),&
 B_{12}&=-28i,&B_{13}&=28,&B_{14}&=0,\\
 B_{21}&=2880it gQ^2,&&&&\\
 B_{22}&=4(280t^2g-140t^2+56tg^2-82tg+7t+28g^2-7g),&&&&\\
 B_{23}&=-8i(104tg-7t+7g),& B_{24}&=16F,&&&\\
 B_{31}&=64t gQ^2(84tg-56Q+39),&&&&\\
 B_{32}&=-4i(224t^2g-84t^2+448tg^2-86tg-7t+28g^2+7g),&&&&\\
 B_{33}&=8(252t^2g-112t^2+252tg^2+20tg-7t+28g^2+7g),&&&&\\
 B_{34}&=16iF,&&&&\\
 B_{41}&=112it gQ^2(1248tg-1012t+68g+123),&&&&\\
 B_{42}&=-7\bigl(1120t^3g-560t^3+2240t^2g^2-2336t^2g+112t^2&&&&\\
 &\hspace{15mm}+1120tg^3-1440tg^2+254tg+7t+560g^3+112g^2-7g\bigr),&&&&\\
 B_{43}&=14i(832t^2g-28t^2+832tg^2-64tg-7t-140g^2+7g),&&&&\\
 B_{44}&=28F(28Q-1).&&&&
\end{align*}
These expressions include the complete polynomial numerators;
no coefficient has been fitted at a sample quartet.

To verify SF5 from the original definition, insert the four
columns SF2 and the four entries of $L$ from SF3. Direct
multiplication yields the exact matrix identity
\begin{equation}
 B(\delta^2,\gamma^2)N
 =448\delta(\delta^2+\gamma^2)
       (\gamma^2+(2\gamma^2-1)\delta^2)(N'+NL).\tag{SF6}
\end{equation}
Every entry is a rational expression with a nonzero denominator
on the original domain. Multiplication by those denominators
reduces SF6 to sixteen polynomial identities over $\mathbb Q(i)$.
The supplied certificate constructs these polynomials from the
displayed expressions and verifies that every coefficient is zero.
The nonzero determinant SF3 then implies SF5. This verification
also proves the determinant in SF3 directly from SF2, so the
calculation does not require an inferred frame rank.

The exact trace provides a separate scalar check:
\begin{equation}
 \tr\Omega_\delta=\frac2\delta+
        \frac{2\delta(2\gamma^2-1)}{\gamma^2+(2\gamma^2-1)\delta^2}.
 \tag{SF7}
\end{equation}
It follows either by adding the diagonal entries SF5 or by
differentiating the actual determinant. In fact, with
$\Delta_\rho=-64\delta^2\gamma^2(\delta^2+\gamma^2)$,
the preserved sign invariant
$\chi=A^2\Delta_\rho\prod_ja_j\in\{1,-1\}$ gives
\begin{equation}
 \det O=1792\chi\delta^2\gamma^2
                    (\gamma^2+(2\gamma^2-1)\delta^2).
 \tag{SF8}
\end{equation}
Indeed $A^2=1/4$, so multiplication of SF3 by
$\prod a_j=4\chi/\Delta_\rho$ gives SF8 with its full sign.
Its logarithmic derivative is precisely SF7.

\section{The exact pole and retained Laurent remainder}
The pole at $\delta=0$ is simple. Its residue, still in the
unchanged original coordinates and with $g=\gamma^2$, is
\begin{equation}
 R_\gamma=\lim_{\delta\to0}\delta\Omega_\delta
 =\begin{pmatrix}
 -\tfrac12&-\dfrac{i}{16g^2}&\dfrac1{16g^2}&0\\[5pt]
 0&\dfrac{4g-1}{16g}&-\dfrac{i}{8g}&\dfrac1{28g}\\[5pt]
 0&-\dfrac{i(4g+1)}{16g}&\dfrac{4g+1}{8g}&\dfrac{i}{28g}\\[5pt]
 0&-\dfrac{7(80g^2+16g-1)}{64g}&\dfrac{7i(1-20g)}{32g}
                                   &\dfrac{28g-1}{16g}
 \end{pmatrix}.
 \tag{SF9}
\end{equation}
It is $B(0,g)/(448g^2)$ by SF5. Define the polynomial matrix
\[
 C(t,g)=\frac{g^2B(t,g)-(t+g)(g+(2g-1)t)B(0,g)}{t}.
\]
The numerator vanishes at $t=0$, entry by entry, so this is
exact polynomial division. The complete remainder identity is
\begin{equation}
 \Omega_\delta=\frac{R_\gamma}{\delta}
       +\delta\,\frac{C(\delta^2,\gamma^2)}
        {448\gamma^4(\delta^2+\gamma^2)
                        (\gamma^2+(2\gamma^2-1)\delta^2)}.
 \tag{SF10}
\end{equation}
The second term is analytic and of order $\delta$ near zero.
No higher terms have been suppressed: its exact rational
numerator and denominator remain specified by SF5 and SF10.
The entry $R_{11}=-1/2$ proves that this pole does not cancel.

For completeness the entire finite complex-$\delta$ pole set
of SF5 is
\begin{equation}
 0,\quad\pm i\gamma,\quad
 \pm\frac{i\gamma}{\sqrt{2\gamma^2-1}}.\tag{SF11}
\end{equation}
All five poles are simple when $\gamma>2$. The denominator in
SF5 has these distinct simple zeros, and its $(1,2)$ numerator
is the nonzero constant $-28i$, so none can be removed from the
matrix. At the pair with $Q=0$ the common residue is
$B(-g,g)/(1792g^2(g-1))$; at the pair with $F=0$ it is
$-(2g-1)B(-g/(2g-1),g)/(1792g^2(g-1))$.
These formulas follow by differentiating the scalar denominator
$448\delta QF$ at its simple zeros. The original positive
interval contains none of these poles except its excluded
endpoint zero.

As a rational matrix function, SF5 has pole order three at
infinity, with exact leading term
\[
 \lim_{\delta\to\infty}\delta^{-3}\Omega_\delta
 =\frac{g}{2g-1}\bigl(4(3g-2)E_{31}
                         +i(312g-253)E_{41}\bigr).
\]
Here $E_{jk}$ is the matrix with its only nonzero entry, equal
to one, in position $(j,k)$. The limit follows from the highest
powers in SF5; its $(3,1)$ entry is nonzero for $g>4$.
If the connection is regarded as the one-form
$\Omega_\delta\,d\delta$, its order at infinity is five,
because $d\delta=-\zeta^{-2}d\zeta$ for $\zeta=1/\delta$.
This statement of complex pole orders does not extend the
original real arithmetic parameter domain.

\section{Residue identities without replacing the original matrix}
Direct expansion of SF9 gives
\begin{equation}
 \det(\lambda I-R_\gamma)
   =(\lambda+\tfrac12)(\lambda-\tfrac12)^2(\lambda-\tfrac32),
 \qquad
 (2R_\gamma+I)(2R_\gamma-I)(2R_\gamma-3I)=0.
 \tag{SF12}
\end{equation}
The characteristic determinant and each entry of the latter
identity are verified as polynomial numerator identities in
$g$ by the exact certificate. The annihilating polynomial has
three distinct roots; its Lagrange interpolation polynomials
in $R_\gamma$ give complementary spectral idempotents.
This proves semisimplicity as a property of the displayed
matrix, rather than assuming a diagonal frame.

Two eigenvectors needed for the native metric statement are
particularly explicit:
\begin{equation}
 e_a=(1,0,0,0)^{\mathsf T},\qquad
 x_+=(0,1,i,35\gamma^2-7/4)^{\mathsf T},\qquad
 R_\gamma e_a=-\tfrac12e_a,\quad
 R_\gamma x_+=\tfrac32x_+.
 \tag{SF13}
\end{equation}
The first follows from the first column of SF9; substitution
in its other three rows proves the second. No coordinates of
the operator have been changed to use these identities.

\section{Exact fixed-conductor transport and Hermitian defect}
Fix one admitted reference conductor and its exact receiving
isomorphism $\Psi_*:\C^4\to W=\cP_3$, as constructed in
FC26--35 and GF19--28 above. Hold it fixed as $\delta$ varies here.
In the original target monomial basis let $H_{\Gamma,*}$ be
the full original Gram matrix of the norm
\[
 \|p\|_{\Gamma,*}^2=\int_{\mathbb R}|p(c_*+iy)|^2
 \frac{(2\pi)^{s/2}2^{s/2}}{4\pi\Gamma(s/2)}
 |\Gamma(s/4+iy/2)|^2\,dy,
 \qquad c_*=a_{\rm amp,*}/2-4.
\]
The source order $s\in\{1,a_{\rm amp,*}\}$ and every mass
in this expression remain fixed. Put
$G=\Psi_*^*H_{\Gamma,*}\Psi_*>0$. This is an exact pullback
of the original norm, not a replacement by a coefficient norm.

The transported frame and connection are
\begin{equation}
 O_W=\Psi_*O,\qquad
 \Omega_W=O_W'O_W^{-1}=\Psi_*\Omega_\delta\Psi_*^{-1},
 \qquad R_W=\Psi_*R_\gamma\Psi_*^{-1}.
 \tag{SF14}
\end{equation}
The identities follow by differentiation because the reference
map is fixed. In particular every coefficient of the original
receiving map remains in these formulas; no derivative of a
moving reference period has been silently omitted.

The entire original-coordinate Hermitian defect is
\begin{equation}
 \mathscr H_\delta=G\Omega_\delta+\Omega_\delta^*G
    =\frac{G B(\delta^2,g)+B(\delta^2,g)^*G}
                 {448\delta(\delta^2+g)(g+(2g-1)\delta^2)}
 \quad(\delta>0).
 \tag{SF15}
\end{equation}
The denominator is real and positive on the original interval,
so taking the conjugate transpose gives exactly this expression.
For the actual moving-frame Gram matrix $K_\delta=O^*GO$,
ordinary differentiation yields
\begin{equation}
 K_\delta'=O^*\mathscr H_\delta O.
 \tag{SF16}
\end{equation}
Its native determinant is also exact:
\[
 \det K_\delta=1792^2\delta^4\gamma^4
       (\gamma^2+(2\gamma^2-1)\delta^2)^2\det G,
 \qquad
 \partial_\delta\log\det K_\delta
  =\frac4\delta+
    \frac{4\delta(2\gamma^2-1)}{\gamma^2+(2\gamma^2-1)\delta^2}.
\]
The first equality is $\det K=|\det O|^2\det G$ with SF8;
the second is its derivative. Thus the native metric and its
complete mass are retained even where a scalar trace is used.

SF10 proves the operator-norm limits
\begin{equation}
 \delta\Omega_\delta\longrightarrow R_\gamma,
 \qquad
 \delta\mathscr H_\delta\longrightarrow
       G R_\gamma+R_\gamma^*G,
 \qquad
 \delta\|\Omega_W\|_{\Gamma,*}
       \longrightarrow\|R_\gamma\|_G\ge\tfrac32.
 \tag{SF17}
\end{equation}
Continuity of matrix norms in these fixed finite-dimensional
positive inner products gives the limits; the lower bound
follows by applying SF13 to $x_+$. Both signs of the leading
Hermitian defect are present for \emph{every} such fixed
original metric, as the exact identities
\begin{equation}
 e_a^*(G R_\gamma+R_\gamma^*G)e_a=-\|e_a\|_G^2<0,
 \qquad
 x_+^*(G R_\gamma+R_\gamma^*G)x_+=3\|x_+\|_G^2>0
 \tag{SF18}
\end{equation}
show. They follow from the two eigenvector equalities in SF13
and conjugate symmetry; they do not assert that $R_\gamma$
is self-adjoint or normal in this original metric. Equivalently
the physical test vectors are $\Psi_*e_a$ and $\Psi_*x_+$,
and the physical Hermitian defect pulls back exactly to SF15.
The full remainder in all these limits remains the explicit
rational remainder SF10, not an unspecified discarded term.

\section{Exact verification and source use}
The accompanying script
\texttt{derive\_signed\_frame\_connection.py} differentiates
the four labelled columns SF2, forms SF4, and saves their full
rational expressions. The independent display certificate
\texttt{verify\_signed\_frame\_connection.py} reconstructs
the sixteen entries in SF5, checks all sixteen entries of
SF6 by their polynomial numerators over $\mathbb Q(i)$,
checks the determinant SF3, both identities SF12, both vectors
SF13, the trace SF7, and the exact divisibility used in SF10.
No decimal approximation or sample parameter is used.
The captured output is
\texttt{verify\_signed\_frame\_connection\_output.txt}.
