\section{The signed-frame connection in all four target coordinates}
The full inverse GF7, proved from the original ES factor construction
\cite{ES488,ES}, determines a differential equation without changing its
coordinates. Let $u=(A,B,C,D)$ and retain
$h(x)=Ax^4+x^3+Bx^2+Cx+D$. Work on the explicitly defined open set
\[
 \mathcal U=\{u:A\,\operatorname{Disc}(h)\det V(u)\ne0\},
 \qquad V\text{ is the full matrix in SE4}.
 \tag{SC1}
\]
Every admitted literal quartet belongs to this set by GF21 and SE5.
For a local ordering of its four roots, put $W=(r_j^n)_{n=0}^3$.
With all choices $a_j^2h'(r_j)=1$ retained, its odd frame is
$O=VW\operatorname{diag}(a_j)$ as already proved in SE7.

In the original ordered polynomial coefficient basis $(1,x,x^2,x^3)$,
define the following four-by-four matrices:
\[
 \mathsf C=\begin{pmatrix}
 0&0&0&-D/A\\1&0&0&-C/A\\0&1&0&-B/A\\0&0&1&-1/A
 \end{pmatrix},\quad
 \mathsf F=4A\mathsf C^3+3\mathsf C^2+2B\mathsf C+CI_4,
 \quad
 \mathsf J=\begin{pmatrix}0&0&0&0\\1&0&0&0\\0&2&0&0\\0&0&3&0\end{pmatrix}.
 \tag{SC2}
\]
Here the scalar $C$ and the multiplication matrix $\mathsf C$ are
different objects. Multiplication by $x$ modulo the original polynomial
$h$ has precisely the displayed matrix: its last column follows from
$Ax^4=-x^3-Bx^2-Cx-D$. Evaluation proves
$W^T\mathsf C=\operatorname{diag}(r_j)W^T$. Consequently
\[
 \det\mathsf F=\prod_jh'(r_j)
   =A^4\prod_{j<k}(r_k-r_j)^2
   =\operatorname{Disc}(h)/A^2\ne0.
 \tag{SC3}
\]
The middle equality multiplies the twelve ordered differences in
$h'(r_j)=A\prod_{k\ne j}(r_j-r_k)$; its six sign reversals have
positive product. The discriminant here is the retained degree-four
discriminant $A^6\prod_{j<k}(r_k-r_j)^2$.

For $\nu=A,B,C,D$ respectively let
\[
 f_\nu(x)=(x^4,x^2,x,1)_\nu,\qquad
 \mathsf R_\nu=-f_\nu(\mathsf C)\mathsf F^{-1},\qquad
 \mathsf T_\nu=-\tfrac12\mathsf F^{-1}
 \bigl[f'_\nu(\mathsf C)
 +(12A\mathsf C^2+6\mathsf C+2BI_4)\mathsf R_\nu\bigr].
 \tag{SC4}
\]
All entries are specified rational functions of the original four
coordinates. No roots or square-root branches need be selected to
evaluate these matrices. The exact connection matrix is
\[
 \boxed{\quad
 \Omega_\nu=(\partial_\nu V)V^{-1}
       +V\bigl(\mathsf J\mathsf R_\nu^T+
                           \mathsf T_\nu^T\bigr)V^{-1}.
 \quad} \tag{SC5}
\]
This formula retains the complete SE4 matrix, its derivatives, the
original leading coefficient $A$, and every indicated inverse and
transpose. It is a finite rational matrix formula, rather than an
unspecified existence assertion.

Here is a direct proof of SC5. Differentiating $h(r_j)=0$ and
$a_j^2h'(r_j)=1$ gives, respectively,
\[
 \partial_\nu r_j=-\frac{f_\nu(r_j)}{h'(r_j)},\qquad
 \frac{\partial_\nu a_j}{a_j}
 =-\frac{f'_\nu(r_j)+h''(r_j)\partial_\nu r_j}{2h'(r_j)}.
 \tag{SC6}
\]
Evaluation of any rational function of $\mathsf C$ is legitimate here
when its denominator is invertible. SC3 verifies this for SC4.
The evaluation identity in SC2 implies that the two quantities in SC6
are the evaluations of $\mathsf R_\nu$ and $\mathsf T_\nu$.
More precisely,
\[
 W\operatorname{diag}(\partial_\nu r_j)W^{-1}
       =\mathsf R_\nu^T,\quad
 W\operatorname{diag}(\partial_\nu a_j/a_j)W^{-1}
       =\mathsf T_\nu^T,\quad
 (\partial_\nu W)W^{-1}=\mathsf J\mathsf R_\nu^T.
 \tag{SC7}
\]
The last identity follows by differentiating each column
$(1,r_j,r_j^2,r_j^3)^T$; its derivative with respect to $r_j$ is
$\mathsf J$ times that column. Applying the product rule to
$O=VW\operatorname{diag}(a_j)$ proves
\[
 \partial_\nu O=\Omega_\nu O. \tag{SC8}
\]
Changing the root marking or the four square-root choices multiplies
$O$ on the right by a constant signed permutation matrix on a connected
overlap. Hence SC8, and therefore SC5, is independent of all those
choices while retaining their action on the actual eight states.

Write $\Omega=\sum_\nu\Omega_\nu\,du_\nu$.
Since $\Omega=dO\,O^{-1}$ on every root chart, differentiation gives
\[
 d\Omega=\Omega\wedge\Omega,\qquad
 \partial_\mu\Omega_\nu-\partial_\nu\Omega_\mu
          =[\Omega_\mu,\Omega_\nu]. \tag{SC9}
\]
Indeed $d(O^{-1})=-O^{-1}(dO)O^{-1}$, so the product rule for the
one-form $dO$ gives $d(dO\,O^{-1})=dO\wedge O^{-1}dO\,O^{-1}$.
Thus $d-\Omega$ is flat, with the actual $O$ as a local fundamental
matrix. For a loop in $\mathcal U$ whose signed continuation is $R$,
its based transport is exactly $O(u_b)R O(u_b)^{-1}$: each continued
column is the specified sign of the specified endpoint column. This
statement identifies the transport of every such loop; it does not
replace the separately proved SM path calculations.

On the literal quartet with fixed $\gamma$, substitution of GF20 gives
\[
 \Omega_\delta=2\delta\Omega_B-2\delta\Omega_C+
  \delta(\tfrac12-2\delta^2-2\gamma^2)\Omega_D,
 \qquad O'=\Omega_\delta O. \tag{SC10}
\]
The coefficient of $\Omega_A$ is zero because $A=-1/2$ is retained.
SC10 is also independently checked against all sixteen entries of the
explicit quartet matrix SF5 by
\texttt{verify\_coefficient\_connection.py}. The certificate multiplies
out exact numerator identities; it uses no numeric root samples.

Fix a receiving original period and its maps $\Phi_*,\Psi_*$ and
$L\Phi_*=\Psi_*$ from FC31--35. The star denotes this fixed reference,
not a moving family of arithmetic periods. Define
\[
 \Omega^U_\nu=\Phi_*\Omega_\nu\Phi_*^{-1},\qquad
 \Omega^W_\nu=\Psi_*\Omega_\nu\Psi_*^{-1}.
 \quad L\Omega^U_\nu=\Omega^W_\nu L. \tag{SC11}
\]
The displayed intertwining follows by substituting $L\Phi_*=\Psi_*$
on each side. Hence both differential equations and their full path
transports pass through the actual fixed conductor. Let
$G=\Psi_*^*\langle\ ,\ \rangle_{\Gamma,W}\Psi_*$ and $G_U$ be its
source counterpart, retaining FC56a and all original Gamma masses
\cite{WCF}. For a real path and $z'=\Omega_tz$, differentiation of
the exact original norm gives
\[
 \frac{d}{dt}\|\Psi_*z\|_{\Gamma,W}^2
       =z^*(\Omega_t^*G+G\Omega_t)z. \tag{SC12}
\]
This is the full metric defect. Flatness SC9 does not remove it.
% END COEFFICIENT_CONNECTION

% BEGIN TOTAL_CONNECTION
\section{The connection of the complete eight-state object}
Fix the original receiving $X_*,Y_*,\Phi_*,\Psi_*$ of FC31--35 at
one admitted reference period. The auxiliary quartic is now varied
through GF20, while this original conductor and its norms stay fixed.
Keep the ordered roots
$r_j=\tfrac12+\epsilon_j\delta+i\eta_j\gamma$ in the original
$(\mathrm M,\mathrm N,\mathrm T,\mathrm E)$ marking, with
$(\epsilon_j,\eta_j)=(1,1),(1,-1),(-1,1),(-1,-1)$.
All eight states are still $q_{j,\pm}=e_j\pm o_j$ of SE1.
Define the complete coordinate map and its inverse by
\[
 \mathsf F_\delta=
 \begin{pmatrix}I_4&0\\ E_\delta&O_\delta\end{pmatrix},\qquad
 \mathsf F_\delta^{-1}=
 \begin{pmatrix}I_4&0\\-O_\delta^{-1}E_\delta&O_\delta^{-1}\end{pmatrix}.
 \tag{TC1}
\]
Here $\mathsf F_\delta(\alpha,\beta)=(\alpha,z)$ with
$z=E_\delta\alpha+O_\delta\beta$. SE7 proves invertibility on the
whole admitted interval. The physical maps are
$\mathsf J_U=\operatorname{diag}(X_*,\Phi_*)\mathsf F_\delta$ and
$\mathsf J_W=\operatorname{diag}(Y_*,\Psi_*)\mathsf F_\delta$.
They satisfy $(L\oplus L)\mathsf J_U=\mathsf J_W$ by FC32.

Differentiate TC1, retaining both its blocks. The exact differential
equation for a constant marked state $(\alpha,\beta)$ is
\[
 \frac{d}{d\delta}\begin{pmatrix}\alpha\\z\end{pmatrix}
 =\mathcal B_\delta\begin{pmatrix}\alpha\\z\end{pmatrix},\qquad
 \mathcal B_\delta=\mathsf F_\delta'\mathsf F_\delta^{-1}
 =\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
 E_\delta'-\Omega_\delta E_\delta&\Omega_\delta\end{pmatrix}.
 \tag{TC2}
\]
For clarity every entry of the lower-left block is specified by the
full SF matrix and the following original columns and derivatives:
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 d_j&=4\delta\gamma(\epsilon_j\gamma-i\eta_j\delta),&
 d_j'&=4\gamma(\epsilon_j\gamma-2i\eta_j\delta),\\
 e_j&=(0,-r_j,3id_j,
       (B-17r_j+Ar_j^2)d_j-2Ad_j^2)^T,& A&=-\tfrac12,\\
 e_j'&=(0,-\epsilon_j,3id_j',
 [2\delta-17\epsilon_j+2Ar_j\epsilon_j]d_j
 +(B-17r_j+Ar_j^2)d_j'-4Ad_jd_j')^T.
 \end{aligned} \tag{TC3}
\]
The $j$th lower-left column is exactly $e_j'-\Omega_\delta e_j$.
No term from the moving means has been omitted.

For any two points joined by a continued branch in the admitted
domain, the full transport, with its inverse obtained by swapping
the endpoints, is
\[
 \mathcal T_{2,1}=\mathsf F_{\delta_2}\mathsf F_{\delta_1}^{-1}
 =\begin{pmatrix}I_4&0\\
 E_{\delta_2}-O_{\delta_2}O_{\delta_1}^{-1}E_{\delta_1}&
 O_{\delta_2}O_{\delta_1}^{-1}\end{pmatrix}.
 \tag{TC4}
\]
Multiplication proves $\mathcal T_{3,2}\mathcal T_{2,1}
=\mathcal T_{3,1}$; differentiation at the first endpoint gives TC2.
Thus the lower-left term is part of the proved transport, including
when the initial evaluation $z$ is zero.

Put $r_+=\tfrac12+i\gamma$, $r_-=\tfrac12-i\gamma$, and retain
the exact residue $R_\gamma=\lim_{\delta\to0}\delta\Omega_\delta$
calculated in SF. Substitution in TC3 gives
\[
 E_0=e_y\ell_\gamma,\quad e_y=(0,1,0,0)^T,\quad
 \ell_\gamma=(-r_+,-r_-,-r_+,-r_-).
 \tag{TC5}
\]
Indeed every $d_j$ vanishes at $\delta=0$, while the two repeated
root values stay as displayed. Since $E_\delta$ is polynomial in
$\delta$, TC2 and the explicit simple pole of SF give
\[
 \mathcal B_\delta=\frac{\mathcal R_\gamma}{\delta}+O(1),\qquad
 \mathcal R_\gamma=
 \begin{pmatrix}0&0\\-R_\gamma E_0&R_\gamma\end{pmatrix}.
 \tag{TC6}
\]
The $O(1)$ refers to every entry in these unchanged coordinates.
The lower-left residue has rank exactly one. In fact SF proves
$\det R_\gamma=-3/16\ne0$, and $E_0$ has rank one because neither
$e_y$ nor $\ell_\gamma$ is zero. This rank statement therefore
retains the actual means and all four labels.

Direct block determinants and multiplication give
\[
 \det(\lambda I_8-\mathcal R_\gamma)
 =\lambda^4(\lambda+\tfrac12)
                  (\lambda-\tfrac12)^2(\lambda-\tfrac32),
 \qquad
 \mathcal R_\gamma(2\mathcal R_\gamma+I_8)
 (2\mathcal R_\gamma-I_8)(2\mathcal R_\gamma-3I_8)=0.
 \tag{TC7}
\]
For the product identity, powers of the lower block have the form
$\mathcal R_\gamma^n=[\,0\ 0;\,-R_\gamma^nE_0\ R_\gamma^n\,]$
for $n\ge1$. Substitution of the SF cubic annihilator proves TC7.
Its four roots are distinct, so the residue is semisimple: for
completeness the interpolation polynomials
$p_\lambda(t)=\prod_{\mu\ne\lambda}(t-\mu)/(\lambda-\mu)$,
with $\lambda,\mu\in\{0,-1/2,1/2,3/2\}$, give mutually
annihilating idempotents $p_\lambda(\mathcal R_\gamma)$ summing
to $I_8$, with image in the indicated eigenspace. This is a proof
about the displayed original matrix, without replacing its coordinates.

\section{A complete boundary loop and its exact original metric defect}
Choose any admitted real $\delta_b\in(0,1/2)$, and continue
$\delta(t)=\delta_b e^{2\pi it}$ for $0\le t\le1$, with the same
real $\gamma>2$. All quartic roots remain distinct: their possible
collisions occur at $\delta=0$ or $\delta=\pm i\gamma$. The other
zeros of SE5 lie at
$\delta=\pm i\gamma/\sqrt{2\gamma^2-1}$, whose moduli exceed
$1/\sqrt2$. Thus this entire loop belongs to SC1.
The factors
$\epsilon_j\gamma-i\eta_j\delta(t)$ trace circles of radius
$\delta_b$ about $\epsilon_j\gamma$; they have winding zero around
zero. Therefore each
$d_j=4\gamma\delta(\epsilon_j\gamma-i\eta_j\delta)$ has winding
one. Its inverse square root $a_j$ changes sign. The root marking
and every mean $e_j$ return unchanged. It follows, without a
resonance assumption, that the full based transport is
\[
 \mathcal T_{\rm loop}=
 \begin{pmatrix}I_4&0\\2E_{\delta_b}&-I_4\end{pmatrix},\qquad
 (\mathcal T_{\rm loop})^2=I_8.
 \tag{TC8}
\]
On the eight marked inverse states this is precisely the simultaneous
swap of all four signs, the central deck element already identified
in SM19. Its lower-left block has rank three by SE8. The rank-one
boundary residue in TC6 and the rank-three finite based holonomy in
TC8 are both calculated from the same original means, at their stated
different parameter values.

The fixed original target metric on the two retained coordinates is
$\mathbb G=\operatorname{diag}(G_Y,G)$, with
$G_Y=Y_*^*\langle\ ,\ \rangle_{\Gamma,W}Y_*$ and
$G=\Psi_*^*\langle\ ,\ \rangle_{\Gamma,W}\Psi_*$ from FC56a.
For $\zeta'=\mathcal B_\delta\zeta$ its complete defect is
\[
 \frac{d}{d\delta}(\zeta^*\mathbb G\zeta)
 =\zeta^*(\mathcal B_\delta^*\mathbb G+
                              \mathbb G\mathcal B_\delta)\zeta,
 \quad
 \delta(\mathcal B_\delta^*\mathbb G+
                 \mathbb G\mathcal B_\delta)
 \longrightarrow
 \begin{pmatrix}
 0&-E_0^*R_\gamma^*G\\-GR_\gamma E_0&R_\gamma^*G+GR_\gamma
 \end{pmatrix}. \tag{TC9}
\]
For the original vectors $e_a=(1,0,0,0)^T$ and
$x_+=(0,1,i,35\gamma^2-7/4)^T$, SF proves
$R_\gamma e_a=-e_a/2$ and $R_\gamma x_+=3x_+/2$.
Consequently TC9 on $(0,e_a)$ equals $-\|e_a\|_G^2<0$, and
on $(0,x_+)$ equals $3\|x_+\|_G^2>0$. In particular the original
metric defect has both signs for all sufficiently small positive
$\delta$; no positive fixed original metric makes it vanish there.
Continuity of finite matrix norms also gives the exact limits
\[
 \delta\|\mathcal B_\delta\|_{\mathbb G}
       \longrightarrow\|\mathcal R_\gamma\|_{\mathbb G}\ge\tfrac32,
 \qquad
 \delta\|\Omega_\delta\|_G\longrightarrow\|R_\gamma\|_G
       \ge\tfrac32. \tag{TC10}
\]
The lower bounds follow by evaluating the operators on the displayed
$3/2$ eigenvector, with its unchanged positive norm.

Finally conjugate TC2--10 by
$\operatorname{diag}(Y_*,\Psi_*)$ or
$\operatorname{diag}(X_*,\Phi_*)$ to obtain their exact physical
target and source maps. Since the original conductor is fixed,
\[
 (L\oplus L)\mathcal B^U_\delta
      =\mathcal B^W_\delta(L\oplus L),\qquad
 (L\oplus L)\mathcal T^U_{2,1}
      =\mathcal T^W_{2,1}(L\oplus L). \tag{TC11}
\]
These equalities follow by cancelling the stated fixed isomorphisms
on the two sides. They preserve the original inverse-exterior
constants of SE17 and audit (11). The new singularity is the proved
boundary connection of the nonlinear inverse states carried through
that conductor; the fixed original linear map retains its original
nonzero determinant.
% END TOTAL_CONNECTION

% BEGIN FACTOR_COMPLETION
\section{Completing the missing factor sign with exact chart morphisms}
The web continuation \cite{WebFable}, Sections 1--2, proposes the
two-chart factor completion. Here its chart inverses are made explicit
at the boundary, so the completed object and the original polynomial
are related by exact morphisms on their stated domains.
Keep the original factor coordinates, with
$L=aU+bV$ and $Q=cU^3+dU^2V+eUV^2+fV^3$, and define
\[
 \mathfrak X=\{(a,b,c,d,e,f)\in\C^6:
 ad+bc=1,\ -cb^3+dab^2-ea^2b+fa^3=1\},
 \quad\pi=(ac,ae+bd,af+be,bf). \tag{FX1}
\]
For $\sigma\in\{i,-i\}$ define $\varphi_\sigma:\C^4\to\mathfrak X$
by the complete formulas
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 b&=\sigma+ay,&c&=-\sigma+2ay+a^2z,\\
 d&=-\sigma y-2ay^2-\sigma az-a^2yz,&
 e&=-7\sigma y^2+2z+aw,\\
 f&=2a^2y^3z+awy+4ay^4+6\sigma ay^2z+\sigma w
                                      +3\sigma y^3-4yz.
 \end{aligned} \tag{FX2}
\]
The first coordinate is the unchanged $a$.
Substitution using $\sigma^2=-1$ gives both constraints in FX1.
The original map is exactly $P=\pi\varphi_i$; the other chart
map is $P_{-i}=\pi\varphi_{-i}$.

The variety $\mathfrak X$ is smooth of complex dimension four.
Indeed scaling $L$ alone and $Q$ alone gives two ambient tangent
vectors on which the derivatives of the two constraint functions
are the columns of
$\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&1\\3&1\end{smallmatrix}\right)$,
whose determinant is $-2$. Thus the two constraint gradients are
independent at every point. If a tangent vector is killed by
$d\pi$, then $\dot LQ+L\dot Q=0$, since the omitted cubic
coefficient has derivative zero by the first constraint. The
resultant-one constraint makes $L$ and $Q$ coprime. Hence $L$
divides $\dot L$, and homogeneity forces $\dot L=tL$;
then $\dot Q=-tQ$. The derivative of the resultant in this
direction is $2t$, which must vanish. Therefore $t=0$, and
$d\pi$ is an isomorphism between its four-dimensional tangent
spaces at every point.

At $a=0$, the constraints imply $bc=1$ and $-cb^3=1$,
so $b=\pm i$ and $c=-b$, with $d,e,f$ arbitrary. For a fixed
$\sigma$ set
\[
 U_\sigma=\mathfrak X\setminus\{a=0,b=-\sigma\}.
 \tag{FX3}
\]
On its part $a\ne0$ the inverse to FX2 is
\[
 y=(b-\sigma)/a,\quad
 z=(c+\sigma-2ay)/a^2,\quad
 w=(e+7\sigma y^2-2z)/a. \tag{FX4}
\]
Eliminating $d$ with the first constraint and $f$ with the second
shows these recover all six factor coefficients.
To prove regularity at $a=0,b=\sigma$, work in the explicit open
set $b(b+\sigma)\ne0$. The following formulas have only units
in their denominators and coincide with FX4:
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 y&=\frac{2db^2-aeb+a^2f}{b+\sigma},\\
 z&=\frac{7y^2-10\sigma ay^3-4a^2y^4-eb+af}{2b^3},\\
 w&=\frac{eb(2\sigma y+ay^2)-11\sigma y^3-17ay^4
                                      +7\sigma a^2y^5-f}{b^3}.
 \end{aligned} \tag{FX5}
\]
For a direct verification, eliminate $bc$ from the resultant to
get $b^2+1=a(2db^2-aeb+a^2f)$. Factoring its left side as
$(b-\sigma)(b+\sigma)$ proves the first formula and
$b=\sigma+ay$. Next $bc=1-ad$ gives
$c+\sigma-2ay=-a(d+\sigma y+2ay^2)/b$.
Substitution of $b=\sigma+ay$ into the preceding resultant
identity gives
\[
 2b^2(d+\sigma y+2ay^2)
 =a[-7y^2+10\sigma ay^3+4a^2y^4+eb-af].
 \tag{FX6}
\]
This proves the second formula, including its extra factor of $a$.
Finally expand $b^3=-\sigma-3ay+3\sigma a^2y^2+a^3y^3$;
substituting the second formula in $e+7\sigma y^2-2z$ proves
the third formula, with its factor of $a$. Thus no division by
zero or unproved divisibility is needed at the boundary.
At $a=0$ the formulas give
$y=\sigma d$, $z=(e+7\sigma y^2)/2$, and
$w=(f-3\sigma y^3+4yz)/\sigma$; substitution in FX2 recovers
$d,e,f$ there as well. FX4--5 cover $U_\sigma$ and agree on
their overlap. They therefore construct the full regular inverse.
In particular both $U_i,U_{-i}$ are actual affine four-space
charts, they cover $\mathfrak X$, and their overlap is exactly
$a\ne0$, isomorphic to $\C^*\times\C^3$.

The transition from the negative to the positive chart is
\[
 \varphi_i^{-1}\varphi_{-i}(a,Y,Z,W)
 =\left(a,Y-\frac{2i}{a},Z+\frac{6i}{a^2},
 W+\frac{14iY^2}{a}+\frac{28Y}{a^2}-\frac{40i}{a^3}\right).
 \tag{FX7}
\]
It follows by applying FX4 to FX2; its differential is triangular
with all diagonal entries one, so its determinant is exactly one.
The inverse transition is obtained by replacing $i$ with $-i$
in FX7. Their composition is the identity, as the same inverse
chart construction also proves. Multiplication gives
$P_i\varphi_i^{-1}\varphi_{-i}=P_{-i}$ on this overlap.

Every simple projective root of the original homogeneous quartic
now contributes both resultant-one factor signs. A multiple root
contributes none. At the finite roots this is GF7. At infinity,
$A=0$ and the fixed cubic coefficient is one, so infinity is
simple and its two signs are precisely $b=\pm i$ just constructed.
Consequently
\[
 \#\pi^{-1}(u)=2\#\{\text{simple projective roots of }H_u\},
 \qquad S_\pi=\{u:\operatorname{Disc}(H_u)=0\}.
 \tag{FX8}
\]
Here $S_\pi$ denotes finite target limits of factors escaping every
compact subset of the affine six-coordinate variety FX1.
For the nonproper inclusion, a multiple projective root must be
finite: infinity is simple whenever it is a root. Keep that root
$r$ and deform $h$ to $h+\varepsilon^2(x-r)$. Its selected
derivative is $\varepsilon^2$, so its factor coefficient
$a=\varepsilon^{-1}$ diverges, while all target coefficients
converge. This proves every discriminant point is in $S_\pi$.

Conversely fix a squarefree target. On a sufficiently small target
neighborhood its four projective roots lie in four disjoint coordinate
disks and are simple. Each root determines a factor line, locally
represented by a nonzero linear polynomial $\ell$. Polynomial
division gives $H=\ell q$ with bounded continuous coefficients,
and $R=\operatorname{Res}(\ell,q)\ne0$ throughout a smaller
neighborhood. Every factor pair over this selected root has the
unique form $(s\ell,s^{-1}q)$ with $s^2R=1$, since the
resultant of degrees one and three scales by $s^{3-1}$.
Thus both $s$ and $s^{-1}$ are bounded there, and every coefficient
of every lift is bounded. Four such disks cover all lifts. A
convergent sequence of targets in this neighborhood therefore
cannot have an escaping factor lift; this proves the reverse
inclusion and all of FX8. The fixed cubic coefficient ensures
the first constraint for every such factorization.

The original map has the additional nonproper hyperplane $A=0$
because it sees only $U_i$; FX7 exhibits its missing finite
representatives in $U_{-i}$. The selected-root escape RC1 persists
in both charts as a divergent factor coefficient. These are
explicitly different degenerations of the same completed object.
No identification of $\mathfrak X$ with the original four-space
or with the arithmetic source quotient is used.
% END FACTOR_COMPLETION

% BEGIN SELECTED_ROOT_COLLISION
\section{The second boundary: a selected root collides}
The incoming web continuation \cite{WebFable}, Section 4, provides
the following branch. Its polynomial $\widehat P_i$ is exactly the
original $P$ of FC31, under the sole coordinate dictionary $t=w$:
both use the identical five factor expressions in GF2, and multiplying
$(ac,ae+bd,af+be,bf)$ gives each of the four original components.
Thus no new polynomial is introduced. For $0<\varepsilon<1/2$ put
\[
 q_\varepsilon=(\varepsilon^{-1},-i\varepsilon,
          3i\varepsilon^2,\varepsilon^2-13i\varepsilon^3)^T.
 \quad P(q_\varepsilon)=(0,1,\varepsilon^2,0)^T.
 \tag{RC1}
\]
The displayed image follows by substitution in those four components.
Its selected root is $r=0$ of $h_\varepsilon(x)=x^3+x^2+
\varepsilon^2x$, so $h_\varepsilon'(0)=\varepsilon^2$ and
$a^2h_\varepsilon'(0)=1$ with the original $a=\varepsilon^{-1}$.
The discriminant is $\varepsilon^4(1-4\varepsilon^2)$, nonzero
on this interval. At its endpoint the selected zero root becomes
double. For fixed $\Psi_*$ and its original positive Gram $G$,
\[
 \varepsilon\|\Psi_*q_\varepsilon\|_{\Gamma,W}
       \longrightarrow\sqrt{G_{11}}>0,\qquad
 \frac{\|\Psi_*(P(q_\varepsilon)-(0,1,0,0)^T)\|_{\Gamma,W}}
      {\varepsilon^2}=\sqrt{G_{33}}.
 \tag{RC2}
\]
The first follows from $\varepsilon q_\varepsilon\to e_a$ and
continuity of this fixed norm; the second is exact for every
$\varepsilon$. In particular the square root of the displayed
output distance times the source norm tends to
$G_{33}^{1/4}G_{11}^{1/2}$. The factor coefficient $a$ itself
diverges. This escape therefore persists in the full factor variety,
not just its $b=i+ay$ chart.

The complete derivative and inverse in the original coordinate order
are
\[
 J_\varepsilon=DP(q_\varepsilon)=
 \begin{pmatrix}
 4i&2\varepsilon^{-2}&\varepsilon^{-3}&0\\
 2\varepsilon-14i\varepsilon^2&-13&2\varepsilon^{-1}&\varepsilon^{-2}\\
 -2\varepsilon^3-2i\varepsilon^2&2&0&0\\
 -i\varepsilon^4&\varepsilon^2&0&0
 \end{pmatrix},\quad\det J_\varepsilon=-2, \tag{RC3}
\]
\[
 J_\varepsilon^{-1}=
 \begin{pmatrix}
 0&0&-1/(2\varepsilon^3)&\varepsilon^{-5}\\
 0&0&-i/(2\varepsilon)&\varepsilon^{-2}+i\varepsilon^{-3}\\
 \varepsilon^3&0&3i&-2/\varepsilon-6i/\varepsilon^2\\
 -2\varepsilon^4&\varepsilon^2&1-39i\varepsilon/2&
                  17+39i/\varepsilon-2/\varepsilon^2
 \end{pmatrix}. \tag{RC4}
\]
Differentiation of the original components gives RC3; multiplying
RC3 and RC4 in either order gives $I_4$. The velocity identity is
$J_\varepsilon q_\varepsilon'=2\varepsilon e_z$, where
$e_z=(0,0,1,0)^T$. The complete exact checks are reproduced by
\texttt{derive\_selected\_root\_collision.py}, including every
compound matrix, rather than only the scalar determinant.

Use increasing multi-indices for each exterior coordinate and let
$E_{I,J}$ send the coefficient of $e_J$ to $e_I$. Direct expansion
of all minors in RC3 and RC4 gives the full leading matrices:
\[
 \begin{array}{c|c|c|c|c}
 r& m_r&\lim\varepsilon^{m_r}\Lambda^rJ_\varepsilon&
        n_r&\lim\varepsilon^{n_r}\Lambda^rJ_\varepsilon^{-1}\\\hline
 1&3&E_{1,3}&5&E_{1,4}\\
 2&5&E_{12,34}&5&-E_{12,34}/2\\
 3&5&2E_{123,234}&3&-E_{124,234}/2\\
 4&0&-2&0&-1/2
 \end{array} \tag{RC5}
\]
These are entrywise limits, with all entries outside the displayed
positions zero. For example the $(12,34)$ minor in RC3 is exactly
$\varepsilon^{-5}$, and the $(123,234)$ minor is exactly
$2\varepsilon^{-5}$. The finite Laurent entries in RC3--4 determine
every other minor by the determinant formula, and the certificate
lists all leading positions. Thus RC5 is an identity calculation,
not an inference from a sampled singular-value plot.

Let $G$ remain the complete fixed original target Gram from FC56a,
and let $G^{-1}$ denote its actual inverse. Define positive constants
\[
 \alpha=\sqrt{G_{11}(G^{-1})_{33}},\qquad
 \beta=\sqrt{\det G_{12,12}\det(G^{-1})_{34,34}},\qquad
 \xi=\sqrt{G_{11}(G^{-1})_{44}}. \tag{RC6}
\]
Every principal minor here is positive. In the induced exterior
norm the coordinate operator $cE_{I,J}$ has exact norm
$|c|\sqrt{\det G_{I,I}\det(G^{-1})_{J,J}}$: it is the vector
$ce_I$ times the coordinate functional dual to $e_J$, whose
dual squared norm is the indicated inverse Gram minor.
Continuity of that finite-dimensional operator norm in RC5, and
the complementary-minor identities
\[
 \det G_{123,123}\det(G^{-1})_{234,234}
       =G_{11}(G^{-1})_{44},\quad
 \det G_{124,124}\det(G^{-1})_{234,234}
       =G_{11}(G^{-1})_{33}, \tag{RC7}
\]
give the forward exterior constants $(\alpha,\beta,2\xi,2)$
with powers $(3,5,5,0)$, and inverse constants
$(\xi,\beta/2,\alpha/2,1/2)$ with powers $(5,5,3,0)$.
To verify RC7, use
$\det G_{I,I}=\det G\det(G^{-1})_{I^c,I^c}$ and its inverse
version, obtained by the corresponding cofactor identities in
$G^{-1}=\operatorname{adj}(G)/\det G$.

Let $\sigma_1\ge\cdots\ge\sigma_4>0$ be the singular values of
the literal operator $\Psi_*J_\varepsilon\Psi_*^{-1}$ in the
original Gamma norm. The norm of its $r$th exterior power is
$\sigma_1\cdots\sigma_r$: in orthonormal singular bases the
exterior eigenvalues are all $r$-fold products, whose greatest
one uses the $r$ greatest factors. Taking successive quotients
of the four proved exterior asymptotes yields
\[
 \boxed{
 \sigma_1\sim\alpha\varepsilon^{-3},\quad
 \sigma_2\sim(\beta/\alpha)\varepsilon^{-2},\quad
 \sigma_3\sim2\xi/\beta,\quad
 \sigma_4\sim\varepsilon^5/\xi.}
 \qquad
 \frac{\sigma_1}{\sigma_4}\sim\alpha\xi\varepsilon^{-8}.
 \tag{RC8}
\]
This extends the scales stated in the incoming web calculation to
all exact constants in the unchanged original metric. The product
is exactly $2$, and the squared volume Jacobian is exactly $4$
for every $\varepsilon$, because both sides use the same $G$.
Each squared singular value is the corresponding eigenvalue of
the relative pullback metric $G^{-1}J_\varepsilon^*GJ_\varepsilon$,
so RC8 also evaluates all four metric-degeneration constants.

The leading right and left exterior directions in ranks one,
two and three are determined without discarding the original
Gram. For RC5 position $(I,J)$ they are respectively
$G^{-1}\operatorname{span}\{e_j:j\in J\}$ and
$\operatorname{span}\{e_i:i\in I\}$, carried physically by
$\Psi_*$. Indeed the Riesz vector of the coefficient functional
$e_J^*$ is $(\Lambda^rG)^{-1}e_J$, and its decomposable factors
are the indicated $G^{-1}e_j$; the range is the stated $e_I$.
The limiting rank-one map is nonzero, so its largest singular
line is simple, and convergence of the finite Hermitian Gram
matrices forces the maximizing lines to converge to these
directions. This supplies the native singular flags for RC8.

Finally set $J_U=\Phi_*J_\varepsilon\Phi_*^{-1}$ and
$J_W=\Psi_*J_\varepsilon\Psi_*^{-1}$. Differentiating the already
proved nonlinear conductor intertwining gives
\[
 LJ_U=J_WL,\qquad
 J_W^{-1}=LJ_U^{-1}L^{-1},\quad
 \frac{\|\Lambda^rJ_U^{-1}\|}
      {\|\Lambda^rL\|\|\Lambda^rL^{-1}\|}
 \le\|\Lambda^rJ_W^{-1}\|
 \le\|\Lambda^rL\|\|\Lambda^rL^{-1}\|
                       \|\Lambda^rJ_U^{-1}\|. \tag{RC9}
\]
The lower bound follows by interchanging source and target in the
upper bound. Every norm is its actual original source or target
norm, and the same calculation RC6--8 with $G_U$ gives the exact
source constants. The original inverse-exterior factor in audit
(11) is thus retained explicitly in this second degeneration.
% END SELECTED_ROOT_COLLISION

% BEGIN CORNER_ACTION
\section{The original corner receiver and its exact multiplication residual}
The diagonal flow in the web continuation \cite{WebFable}, Section 6,
uses labelled corner coefficients. Its exact arithmetic receiver is
constructed here; the earlier cubic receiver $\Psi$ is retained through
an explicit comparison map. Fix the original admitted period with
$0<\delta_*<1/2$, $\gamma_*>2$, and let $k\equiv1\pmod4$,
$k\ge9$, $j=k-8$, $q_-=(j+1)^2$, $D=q_--1$. The original lower
grid and polynomial are
\[
 \lambda_{ab}=j/2+(2a-j)\delta_*+i(2b-j)\gamma_*,\quad
 \chi_j(S')=\prod_{a,b=0}^j(S'-\lambda_{ab}),\quad
 E_j=\C[S']/\bigl(\chi_j\bigr). \tag{CA1}
\]
Distinct pairs give distinct roots: their real and imaginary
differences force $a$ and $b$ separately equal. Order the four corners
as $(j,j),(j,0),(0,j),(0,0)$ with retained labels
$(\mathrm M,\mathrm N,\mathrm T,\mathrm E)$, and denote them by
$\lambda_l=j\rho_l$. Define the actual polynomials
\[
 p_l(S')=\frac{\chi_j(S')}
 {(S'-\lambda_l)\chi_j'(\lambda_l)},\qquad
 J_c:\C^4\longrightarrow\mathcal P_D,\quad
 J_c z=\sum_l z_l p_l(S'). \tag{CA2}
\]
Evaluation at the four marked corners is a left inverse, since
$p_l(\lambda_m)=\delta_{lm}$; evaluation at every other grid
point is zero. Thus $J_c$ is injective both in the polynomial
target and after reduction $I_D:\mathcal P_D\to E_j$.
The latter reduction is itself an isomorphism, because the
degree of $\chi_j$ is $D+1$. All its coefficients and all
cardinal denominators remain in CA2.

Retain the original finite-shift conductor, its actual vanishing
order $v$, and every moment $\mu_n$. In the source quotient
$U_D=\mathcal P_{v+D}/\mathcal P_{v-1}$ and target
$W_D=\mathcal P_D$, its monomial coefficient matrix is
\[
 (B_{D,v})_{rt}=\begin{cases}
 \binom{v+t}{r}\mu_{v+t-r},&0\le r\le t\le D,\\
 0,&r>t.
 \end{cases}\qquad
 b_r=(B_{D,v})_{rr}=\binom{v+r}{v}\mu_v\ne0. \tag{CA3}
\]
Indeed applying the finite-shift formula to $S^{v+t}$ and
using $\mu_0=\cdots=\mu_{v-1}=0$ gives exactly CA3.
For a complete finite expression for its inverse put
$D_b=\operatorname{diag}(b_0,\ldots,b_D)$ and
$N=D_b^{-1}B_{D,v}-I$; then $N^{D+1}=0$ and
\[
 B_{D,v}^{-1}=\left(\sum_{m=0}^D(-N)^m\right)D_b^{-1}.
 \tag{CA4}
\]
Multiplication of the finite geometric sum proves both inverse
identities. This formula is in the original monomial coordinates;
it is not a replacement of the physical norms. Let $T_D:U_D\to W_D$
be the original map, and define the source injection
$K_c=T_D^{-1}J_c$, using CA4 for every coefficient. Then
$T_DK_c=J_c$, and $K_c$ is injective.

On the indicated four-dimensional images the full nonlinear maps
and their exact comparison with the preceding cubic receiver are
\[
 P_c^W=J_cPJ_c^{-1},\quad P_c^U=K_cPK_c^{-1},\quad
 T_DP_c^U=P_c^WT_D,\qquad
 F_W=J_c\Psi_*^{-1}:W\to\operatorname{im}J_c,\quad
 F_U=K_c\Phi_*^{-1}:U\to\operatorname{im}K_c,
 \quad T_DF_U=F_WL. \tag{CA5}
\]
Every inverse here is on its stated image. The last equality is
obtained from $L\Phi_*=\Psi_*$; in particular this is a proved
relation between the two receivers, with every original moment
in the source comparison.
For the actual original target Gamma Gram $H_{D,s}$, the corner
metric is the complete positive matrix
$H_c=J_c^*H_{D,s}J_c$, $s\in\{1,a_{\rm amp,*}\}$.
Its positivity follows from injectivity and the original positive
norm \cite{WCF}; no column is declared orthonormal.
The source uses $K_c^*H_{U_D,s}K_c$ with its original quotient
metric. All previously proved fixed-metric singular formulas apply
with these explicit matrices, and CA5 preserves their differentiated
conductor relation.

Let $M_j$ be multiplication by $S'$ on the actual quotient $E_j$,
and put $B_c=\operatorname{diag}(\lambda_\mathrm M,
\lambda_\mathrm N,\lambda_\mathrm T,\lambda_\mathrm E)$.
The entire polynomial residual is
\[
 S'J_cz-J_cB_cz
 =\chi_j(S')\sum_l\frac{z_l}{\chi_j'(\lambda_l)},\qquad
 M_j I_DJ_c=I_DJ_c B_c. \tag{CA6}
\]
The first identity follows separately from each CA2 column and
has degree $D+1$, outside $W_D$ in general. Reduction by the
actual $\chi_j$ gives the second identity. Thus this is exactly
the arithmetic quotient action represented by $B_c$, with its
full polynomial residual displayed. It is not asserted to be the
unreduced multiplication operator on the earlier cubic space.
Exponentiating the finite-dimensional equality gives
$e^{tM_j}I_DJ_c=I_DJ_c e^{tB_c}$ for every complex $t$, term
by term in the convergent matrix series.

\section{Polynomial lift of this original action and finite complex escape}
For any constant $4\times4$ matrix $B$, the original constant
Jacobian $\det DP=-2$ gives the complete polynomial vector field
\[
 X_B(x)=-\tfrac12\operatorname{adj}(DP(x))\,BP(x),\qquad
 DP(x)X_B(x)=BP(x). \tag{CA7}
\]
The adjugate entry $(r,t)$ is explicitly
$(-1)^{r+t}\det DP(x)_{\widehat t,\widehat r}$, so CA7 is a
finite polynomial formula in the fully displayed original $P$.
The adjugate identity proves CA7, and invertibility of $DP$
proves uniqueness. Its exact correction to the constant linear
field is
\[
 X_B(x)-Bx=-\tfrac12\operatorname{adj}(DP(x))
                         [BP(x)-DP(x)Bx]. \tag{CA8}
\]
The possible zero corrections can also be classified completely.
If $DP(x)Bx=BP(x)$, comparison of linear terms forces $B$ to
commute with $DP(0)=\operatorname{diag}(-i,1,2i,-1)$.
These four eigenvalues are distinct, so $B$ is diagonal.
Writing its diagonal $(b_a,b_y,b_z,b_w)$, the monomials
$a^2y,a^3z$ in $P_0$ force $b_y=-b_a$, $b_z=-2b_a$;
the monomial $a^2w$ in $P_2$ forces $b_w=-3b_a$.
Conversely every monomial of each displayed component has
that component's weight for $(1,-1,-2,-3)$. Thus
\[
 \{B:DP(x)Bx=BP(x)\text{ for all }x\}
       =\C\operatorname{diag}(1,-1,-2,-3). \tag{CA9}
\]
For the corner action $B_c$, its trace is $2j$. A matrix
similar to a multiple of that displayed weight matrix would
have multiplier $-2j/5$, a real number, and therefore real
eigenvalues. The four eigenvalues $j\rho_l$ are nonreal.
Hence $B_c$ is not in that similarity class; CA7 supplies its
actual nonlinear lift, rather than an assumed linear symmetry.

Retain the CA1--2 ordering and put $\lambda_3=\lambda_\mathrm T$,
$\lambda_4=\lambda_\mathrm E$,
$\omega=3\lambda_3-2\lambda_4
=j(1/2-\delta_*+5i\gamma_*)\ne0$.
The following is an actual target orbit in the corner receiver:
\[
 u(t)=(0,0,-3e^{\lambda_3t},e^{\lambda_4t})^T,\qquad
 u'=B_cu,\qquad
 t_*=-\log(4)/\omega. \tag{CA10}
\]
Its original quotient image is $I_DJ_cu(t)=
e^{tM_j}I_DJ_cu(0)$ by CA6. The auxiliary factor cubic is
$h_t(x)=x^3-3p(t)x+q(t)$, with
$p(t)=e^{\lambda_3t}$ and $q(t)=e^{\lambda_4t}$.
Its discriminant is exactly
\[
 27e^{2\lambda_4t}(4e^{\omega t}-1), \tag{CA11}
\]
by the determinant definition or direct expansion of the cubic
resultant. This has a simple zero at $t_*$, because its
derivative there is $27e^{2\lambda_4t_*}\omega\ne0$.
Along $t=st_*$, $0\le s<1$, it is nonzero, since
$4e^{\omega st_*}=4^{1-s}>1$.
At $t_*$ set $r_*=q(t_*)/(2p(t_*))$. The equality
$q(t_*)^2=4p(t_*)^3$ gives $r_*^2=p(t_*)\ne0$,
and $h_{t_*}(x)=(x-r_*)^2(x+2r_*)$.

The exact leading local root behavior and its constants follow
without a root-identification guess. Put $t=t_*+s^2$ and
$x=r_*+s v$. Dividing $h_{t_*+s^2}(r_*+sv)$ by $s^2$
extends analytically to $s=0$, with value
$3r_*v^2-\omega r_*^3$ there. Its two roots
$v_\pm(0)=\pm r_*\sqrt{\omega/3}$ are nonzero and simple.
The analytic implicit-function theorem therefore gives two
actual root branches with
\[
 r_\pm(t)=r_*\pm r_*\sqrt{\omega/3}(t-t_*)^{1/2}
                   +O(t-t_*),\qquad
 h_t'(r_\pm(t))=
   c_\pm(t-t_*)^{1/2}+O(t-t_*),\quad
 c_\pm=\pm6r_*^2\sqrt{\omega/3}\ne0.
 \tag{CA12}
\]
The chosen square root fixes the branch and its sign; both are
retained. The four inverse factor states on these two roots
have
\[
 a(t)=\kappa(t-t_*)^{-1/4}(1+O((t-t_*)^{1/2})),
 \quad\kappa^2=c_\pm^{-1},\qquad
 y(t)\to-r_*,\ z(t)\to0,\ w(t)\to0. \tag{CA13}
\]
Indeed GF7 gives $a^2h_t'(r)=1$ and all three remaining
coordinates explicitly; here its coefficient $A=0$ and
$B=0$ throughout the orbit. Each colliding branch extends
backwards to $t=0$ along the stated segment, because the
cubic roots remain simple and finite there and their
nonzero derivatives provide continuous square-root lifts.
On every compact subsegment the resulting original source
coordinates are bounded. Differentiating $P(x(t))=u(t)$
then gives $x'=X_{B_c}(x)$ by CA7 and the nonzero Jacobian.
Thus these are actual trajectories of the polynomial field.

In the original corner metric their exact escape constant is
\[
 |t-t_*|^{1/4}\|J_c x(t)\|_{\Gamma,W_D}
           \longrightarrow |\kappa|\sqrt{(H_c)_{11}}>0.
 \tag{CA14}
\]
The same statement with $K_c$ uses its full original source
Gram. It follows by multiplying CA13 by $(t-t_*)^{1/4}$
on a chosen branch and using continuity of the fixed norms.
The target $J_cu(t)$ and its arithmetic quotient remain
finite at this complex time. A trajectory with unbounded
source norm has no holomorphic extension across that time,
so the polynomial lift is not complete in complex time.
No assertion of real-time escape follows from this calculation.
Through CA5 the two nonlinear vector fields, their trajectories,
and this singularity pass between the original high-degree
source and target by $T_D$ exactly.

Finally for any retained positive metric $H_c$ put
$G_P(x)=DP(x)^*H_cDP(x)$. The underlying real flow $\phi_t$
of $X_{B_c}$ satisfies $P\phi_t=e^{tB_c}P$ wherever defined.
Differentiating in the initial condition gives
$DP(\phi_t(x))D\phi_t(x)=e^{tB_c}DP(x)$.
Pulling back the metric and differentiating at real $t=0$
therefore proves the full Lie-derivative identity
\[
 \mathcal L_{X_{B_c}}G_P
     =DP^*(B_c^*H_c+H_cB_c)DP. \tag{CA15}
\]
It includes the derivative of the moving metric and both
vector-field derivatives. For every real center $c_0$, the
generalized characteristic polynomial of
$(\mathcal L_{X_{B_c}}G_P-2c_0G_P,G_P)$ equals that of
$(B_c^*H_c+H_cB_c-2c_0H_c,H_c)$: take the determinant of
the congruence and divide by $\det G_P=|\det DP|^2\det H_c$.
Thus the original metric defect is transported with its full
spectrum; the nonlinear escape does not set it to zero.
% END CORNER_ACTION

% BEGIN ACTUAL_MARKED_OBSERVATION
\section{The four original upper corners in the actual observation}
This continuation uses the actual invariant observation
$\Lambda_k:E_k\to B_k$, rather than selecting a lift into $B_k$.
Its original period and all its unit factors are those in FC13.
Retain $j=k-8$, $k\equiv1\pmod4$, $k\ge9$ and the two complete
grid polynomials in CA1, with $k$ and $j$ respectively. Define
$J_+:\C^4\to E_k$ and $J_-:\C^4\to E_j$ by the original
four corner cardinal classes in the order
$(\mathrm M,\mathrm N,\mathrm T,\mathrm E)$.
Their roots are $k\rho_l$ and $j\rho_l$. Write $p_l^+,p_l^-$
for their unique representatives of degrees less than $(k+1)^2$
and $(j+1)^2$. Every denominator is its original
$\chi_k'(k\rho_l)$ or $\chi_j'(j\rho_l)$.

Let $C:E_k\to E_j$ be the original conductor quotient map and
$\bar C:B_k\to E_j$ the map with $\bar C\Lambda_k=C$.
This is an actual factorization: the conductor-multiple invariant
polynomials give $\ker\Lambda_k\subset\ker C$, as proved in
\cite[OB3]{Observed}. The formula
$\bar C(\Lambda_kx)=Cx$ is independent of the choice of lift
by that kernel inclusion. Put
\[
 D_\partial=\diag(a_{88},a_{80},a_{08},a_{00}),\qquad
 O_+=\Lambda_kJ_+,\qquad
 CJ_+=J_-D_\partial,\quad
 \bar C O_+=J_-D_\partial. \tag{MO1}
\]
To prove the third identity, evaluate the finite-shift conductor
at every lower grid root. A shifted lower root reaches an upper
corner only when the lower root is that same corner and the
shift is the matching one among $(8,8),(8,0),(0,8),(0,0)$.
The values are exactly the corresponding original coefficients,
so equality of classes follows by interpolation. This proves
all four columns, including their actual phases.

Before reduction, the entire residual is retained:
\[
 T_Ap_l^+(S')=d_l p_l^-(S')+\chi_j(S')Z_l(S'),\qquad
 Z_l(S')=\frac{\sum_{u,w=0}^8a_{uw}
 p_l^+(S'+\beta_{8,u,w})-d_l p_l^-(S')}{\chi_j(S')},
 \tag{MO2}
\]
where $d_l=(D_\partial)_{ll}$.
The numerator vanishes at every distinct lower grid root by the
preceding evaluation proof, so $Z_l$ is a polynomial, not a
meromorphic substitution. Its degree is at most
$(k+1)^2-(j+1)^2-v-1=16k-49-v$; a negative bound means the
zero polynomial. MO2 gives every coefficient by exact finite
division of its displayed numerator. Thus MO1 has not silently
deleted a polynomial relation from the original WCF norm.

On the explicit original large-period domain of
\cite[OC50--74]{CornerNonzero}, every $d_l$ is nonzero. In the
original notation that domain is $\gamma\ge1000\delta$ and
$|\varpi|\ge R_{\rm corner}^{\rm explicit}$, where all constants
are given in OC53, OC64 and OC67--71; the exact lower bound is
$|d_l|\ge(m_{\rm corner}/2)^4$. This is an already evaluated
period-domain result, with the unit phase retained, rather than
an assumed coefficient sign. MO1 then proves that $O_+$ is
injective: if $O_+c=0$, injectivity of $J_-$ and invertibility
of $D_\partial$ give $c=0$. At every other original period,
the exact partial-support treatment AO1--23 below applies and
does not identify $d_l=0$ with $\Lambda_ke_l=0$.

Let $A_k$ and $A_j$ be the original multiplication operators
on $E_k,E_j$. They act on these corners by
\[
 A_kJ_+=J_+ \mathsf B_k,\quad A_jJ_-=J_-\mathsf B_j,\qquad
 \mathsf B_k=\diag(k\rho_l),\quad \mathsf B_j=\diag(j\rho_l).
 \tag{MO3}
\]
These are identities of the actual quotient operators by the
cardinal formula CA6. On the four-dimensional image of $O_+$,
the actual observed action is
$\Theta_+=O_+\mathsf B_kO_+^{-1}$: directly
$\Lambda_kA_kJ_+=\Theta_+\Lambda_kJ_+$.
No action on the full one-step quotient $B_k$ is inferred.
Its conductor defect is completely explicit:
\[
 (\bar C\Theta_+-A_j\bar C)O_+
       =8J_-D_\partial\diag(\rho_l),\qquad
 \bar C\Theta_+=\frac{k}{j}A_j\bar C
                 \quad\text{on }\operatorname{im}O_+.
 \tag{MO4}
\]
This follows from MO1--3 and the fact that the two displayed
diagonal matrices commute. The factor $k/j$ is retained;
neither original arithmetic action has been replaced by the other.

\section{The exact original minimum correction on these four corners}
Fix $N\ge(k+1)^2-1$ and the original polynomial Gram $H_N$
of $m_k(y)dy=w_h^{*k}(y)dy$ at $S=k/2+iy$, with
$w_h(y)=|(2\xi/h)(1/2+iy)|^2/(2\pi)$ and the original
quartet factor $h(S)=\prod_l(S-\rho_l)$.
Let $J_N:\mathcal P_N\to E_k$ be reduction and retain
\[
 G_N=(J_NH_N^{-1}J_N^*)^{-1},\quad
 Q_N=(\Lambda_kG_N^{-1}\Lambda_k^*)^{-1},\quad
 T_N=(\bar C Q_N^{-1}\bar C^*)^{-1},\quad
 R_N=Q_N^{-1}\bar C^*T_N. \tag{MO5}
\]
These are the original attained metrics of \cite[EQ1--5]{EightMinimum}.
Their inverses exist: the reductions and observation are onto their
defined images, and $C$ is onto because its triangular polynomial
map drops degree by $v\le80$ while the grid-degree difference is
$16k-48\ge96$. The actual density is positive almost everywhere;
its isolated zero set cannot annihilate a nonzero polynomial, so
the finite Grams are positive. The moments and original total mass
in $H_N$ are not replaced by a Gamma surrogate.

The difference between the original observation and its minimum
lift through the conductor is the exact matrix
\[
 \Delta_N=O_+-R_NJ_-D_\partial,\qquad
 \bar C\Delta_N=0. \tag{MO6}
\]
Since $R_N^*Q_N=T_N\bar C$, the two summands are orthogonal
in the original $Q_N$ metric. Thus the complete four-corner
observed Gram is
\[
 H_+=O_+^*Q_NO_+
 =D_\partial^*J_-^*T_NJ_-D_\partial+
                   \Delta_N^*Q_N\Delta_N. \tag{MO7}
\]
No assertion is made that the second term vanishes.
There is a separate exact correction from the original source
quotient: put $M_N=G_N^{-1}\Lambda_k^*Q_N$ and
$Z_N=(I-M_N\Lambda_k)J_+$. Then
$\Lambda_kM_N=I$, $M_N^*G_NM_N=Q_N$,
$\Lambda_kZ_N=0$, and direct orthogonal decomposition gives
\[
 J_+^*G_NJ_+=H_++Z_N^*G_NZ_N. \tag{MO8}
\]
Equations MO7 and MO8 retain the two different kernels and both
original positive residual energies. They also show precisely
which norms accompany an original observed trajectory.

\section{The literal Fable marking and its arithmetic generator}
The original four collision points are $q_l=Ke_l$, with the
complete invertible matrix $K$ in FC15a. Therefore the receiver
which sends each named collision point to its corresponding
original observed corner is
\[
 F_+=J_+K^{-1},\quad F_B=O_+K^{-1},\quad
 F_-=J_-D_\partial K^{-1},\qquad
 \Lambda_kF_+=F_B,\quad\bar CF_B=F_-.
 \tag{MO9}
\]
In particular $F_Bq_l=\Lambda_k[p_l^+]$, with all four labels
retained individually. On the nonzero-corner domain all three
maps are injections onto their indicated four-dimensional images.
Their nonlinear conjugates satisfy, exactly on these images,
$\Lambda_k(F_+PF_+^{-1})=(F_BPF_B^{-1})\Lambda_k$ and
$\bar C(F_BPF_B^{-1})=(F_-PF_-^{-1})\bar C$.
The polynomial relation before this second equality is precisely
MO2; it is not asserted to have zero original polynomial norm.

The arithmetic matrix in the unchanged Fable coordinates is
\[
 \widehat B_k=K\diag(k\rho_l)K^{-1},\qquad
 \Theta_+F_B=F_B\widehat B_k,
 \qquad \widehat G_N=K^{-*}H_+K^{-1}>0.
 \tag{MO10}
\]
This formula gives every coefficient through the displayed FC15a
$K$ and its exact adjugate inverse; all original signs and phases
remain. Replacing $\widehat B_k$ by $\diag(k\rho_l)$ in the
polynomial coordinates would change this marking. CA's different
corner-coefficient receiver remains related by the explicit $K$.
The unique polynomial lift of MO10 is
$X_{\widehat B_k}=-\operatorname{adj}(DP)\widehat B_kP/2$,
by the original determinant $-2$. Its image through $F_B$ is
therefore the exact polynomial lift of the actual observed
arithmetic action restricted to the four original corners.

\section{Every point of the original common fibre along its actual orbit}
Let $h_0=(0,0,-1,0)^T=2iKe_{\mathrm M}$ and
$\lambda=k\rho_\mathrm M$.
MO10 proves $\widehat B_k h_0=\lambda h_0$.
Set $b(t)=e^{\lambda t/2}$; this entire function never vanishes.
The complete original fibre over $e^{\lambda t}h_0$ consists of
the following seven trajectories:
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 q_\infty(t)&=(0,0,ib(t)^2/2,0)^T,\\
 q_{0,\epsilon}(t)&=
 (\epsilon i/b(t),-\epsilon b(t),-3ib(t)^2,
                       13\epsilon b(t)^3)^T,
                         &&\epsilon\in\{1,-1\},\\
 q_{\eta,\epsilon}(t)&=
 \bigl(\epsilon/(\sqrt2 b(t)),
  -(\eta+i\epsilon\sqrt2)b(t),
  (2\eta\epsilon\sqrt2+6i)b(t)^2,
  (-34\eta-19i\epsilon\sqrt2)b(t)^3\bigr)^T,
                         &&\eta,\epsilon\in\{1,-1\}.
 \end{aligned} \tag{MO11}
\]
GF7 gives them from the roots $0,\pm b(t)$ with derivatives
$-b(t)^2,2b(t)^2,2b(t)^2$, and GF8 gives the infinity point.
Substituting in the full original $P$ proves
$P(q(t))=e^{\lambda t}h_0$. Conversely that cubic has precisely
these three simple finite roots and the one chart infinity root,
so GF's full fibre classification proves completeness of the list.
The original four named points occur at $t=0$ as
$q_\infty,q_{0,+},q_{+,+},q_{-,+}$ in order M,N,T,E.

Differentiating their exact image equality and using $DP^{-1}$
shows that every trajectory solves $q'=X_{\widehat B_k}(q)$.
All seven are entire functions of complex time, so none escapes
at a finite complex time on this particular original orbit.
After the target period $2\pi i/\lambda$, $b$ changes sign:
$q_\infty$ returns, $q_{0,\epsilon}$ is exchanged with
$q_{0,-\epsilon}$, and $q_{\eta,\epsilon}$ with
$q_{-\eta,-\epsilon}$. This computes the original fibre's
continuation without merging any two of its marked states.

\section{An exact marked arithmetic orbit with five escaping branches}
The same original generator nevertheless has an explicit finite
boundary crossing. Put
\[
 u_*=(0,1,0,0)^T,\qquad
 u(t)=e^{t\widehat B_k}u_*
     =K\diag(e^{k\rho_l t})K^{-1}u_*.
 \tag{MO12}
\]
This is a complete formula in the fixed original period. Direct
multiplication of FC15a and its inverse gives
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \nu_A:=A'(0)&=\frac{k}{154}
       [-68\gamma+i(25\sqrt2\gamma-136\delta)]\ne0,\\
 \nu_D:=D'(0)&=\frac{k}{77}
       [-884\delta+(1631\sqrt2+442i)\gamma]\ne0.
 \end{aligned} \tag{MO13}
\]
Nonvanishing follows from the nonzero real part of the first
and the positive imaginary part of the second. These identities
are checked exactly over $\mathbb Q(i,\sqrt2)$ by
\texttt{derive\_marked\_arithmetic\_generator.py}.
At $u_*$ the quartic is $h_*(x)=x^2(x+1)$.
The full degree-four discriminant polynomial, retaining its
fixed cubic coefficient, has gradient $(0,0,0,-4)$ there.
This follows by differentiation of its resultant determinant
(and is reproduced by \texttt{verify\_marked\_fibre\_flow.py}).
Thus the discriminant along MO12 has derivative $-4\nu_D\ne0$.

There is a completely specified punctured time disk containing
only eight-state fibres. For an explicit radius form the finite
polynomial
\[
 \mathcal D(z_1,z_2,z_3,z_4)=
 \operatorname{Disc}_x\bigl(A(z)x^4+x^3+B(z)x^2+C(z)x+D(z)\bigr)
 =\sum_\mu c_\mu z^\mu,
 \quad (A(z),B(z),C(z),D(z))^T
       =K\diag(z_l)K^{-1}u_*.
 \tag{MO14}
\]
This specifies every coefficient by the finite resultant and the
literal original matrix. Write $A(z)=\sum_l\alpha_lz_l$ and
$\theta_\mu=k\sum_l\mu_l\rho_l$, and retain the finite sums
\[
 M_D=\sum_\mu|c_\mu|\,|\theta_\mu|^2e^{|\theta_\mu|},\quad
 M_A=\sum_l|\alpha_l|\,|k\rho_l|^2e^{|k\rho_l|},\quad
 r_0=\min\{1,4|\nu_D|/M_D,|\nu_A|/M_A\}>0.
 \tag{MO15}
\]
Both sums are nonzero since the respective first derivatives
are nonzero. Taylor's integral remainder for each exponential
gives errors at most $M_D|t|^2/2$ and $M_A|t|^2/2$ for
$|t|\le1$. Their constant terms vanish. It follows that,
for $0<|t|\le r_0$, the discriminant and $A(t)$ have moduli
at least $2|\nu_D||t|$ and $|\nu_A||t|/2$ respectively.
GF16 therefore gives exactly eight inverse states throughout
this punctured disk. The radius does not require a numerical
search for zeros or a discarded higher Taylor term.

Four states select the two roots colliding at zero. Substitute
$t=s^2$ and $x=sv$ in the full $h_{u(t)}(x)$ and divide by
$s^2$. The analytic extension at zero is $v^2+\nu_D$,
whose two roots $v_\pm=\pm\sqrt{-\nu_D}$ are simple.
Analytic implicit inversion gives
\[
 r_\pm(t)=v_\pm t^{1/2}+O(t),\quad
 h_{u(t)}'(r_\pm(t))=2v_\pm t^{1/2}+O(t),\quad
 t^{1/4}q_{\pm,\epsilon}(t)\longrightarrow\kappa_{\pm,\epsilon}e_a,
 \quad\kappa_{\pm,\epsilon}^2=(2v_\pm)^{-1}.
 \tag{MO16}
\]
Both values of each square root are retained on a chosen time
sector. The final limit follows from all four coordinates of
GF7: $a\sim\kappa t^{-1/4}$, while $y,z,w\to0$.

The projective root at infinity is simple at $t=0$ and supplies
two more factor states. In the negative chart its finite limiting
coordinates are $(a,Y,Z,W)=(0,1,-7i/2,11)$.
The smooth local inverse of $\pi$ from FX1--8 gives its analytic
lift, and $A=ac$ with $c(0)=i$ proves
$a'(0)=-i\nu_A$. Applying the complete transition FX7 gives
\[
 t^3q_{\rm lost}(t)\longrightarrow
                     -40\nu_A^{-3}e_w. \tag{MO17}
\]
Indeed the third-order term is $-40i/a^3$, with
$a=-i\nu_A t+O(t^2)$; its sign and full coefficient are as
displayed. The other terms of FX7 have orders at most two.
The positive infinity state is finite. The remaining root
at $-1$ is simple and supplies two finite states. Their three
limits in the original positive chart are exactly
\[
 (0,1,7i/2,11)^T,\qquad
 (\epsilon,1-i\epsilon,-2\epsilon+3i,18-6i\epsilon)^T,
                       \quad\epsilon\in\{1,-1\}. \tag{MO18}
\]
Substitution in $P$ verifies their common image $u_*$, and
the nonzero constant Jacobian gives their local analytic lifts.
The fibre count eight on the punctured disk and three at its
center proves that MO16--18 exhaust every branch: precisely
four intrinsic root-collision escapes, one missing-chart escape,
and three finite states.

Every inverse branch satisfies the actual polynomial lifted
equation $q'=X_{\widehat B_k}(q)$ by differentiating MO12.
For example start at $t=-r_0/2$ and continue in a sector toward
zero; the elapsed complex time is finite, with no intermediate
discriminant or $A$ zero by MO15. The original observed target
$F_Bu(t)$ follows $\Theta_+$ and stays finite. In the complete
original $Q_N$ norm the five source escape constants are
\[
 |t|^{1/4}\|F_Bq_{\pm,\epsilon}(t)\|_{Q_N}
       \longrightarrow|\kappa_{\pm,\epsilon}|
                             \sqrt{(\widehat G_N)_{11}},\qquad
 |t|^3\|F_Bq_{\rm lost}(t)\|_{Q_N}
       \longrightarrow\frac{40}{|\nu_A|^3}
                             \sqrt{(\widehat G_N)_{44}}.
 \tag{MO19}
\]
Positivity follows from MO7--10. These constants keep both
the original observation and its full minimum residual.
At other periods the actual stack of AO gives the corresponding
nonzero constants whenever any original corner is observable;
it preserves both $K^{-1}e_a$ and $K^{-1}e_w$ and every invisible
kernel, rather than assigning positivity to a vanished column.

At a partially invisible period, the visible stack by itself is
not declared to carry an induced nonlinear $P$. The full relation
has an exact receiver with the invisible labels retained. Let
$I$ and $Z=I^c$ be AO's actual active and invisible sets and let
$\mathcal R_I$ denote the image of its $m$-stack. Define
\[
 \mathcal F_{\rm full}:\C^4\longrightarrow
              \mathcal R_I\oplus\C^Z,\qquad
 q\longmapsto\bigl(Z_mK^{-1}q,(K^{-1}q)_Z\bigr),\qquad
 \mathcal F_{\rm full}^{-1}(y,c_Z)
       =K\bigl(\mathcal R_{\rm AO}y,c_Z\bigr).
 \tag{MO20}
\]
Here $\mathcal R_{\rm AO}$ is the complete active-coordinate
inverse AO7, and the parentheses on the right place the two
vectors in their original label positions, without reordering
them. For $I$ empty the visible component is zero and this is
just $q\mapsto K^{-1}q$ on the four retained invisible labels.
AO7 verifies both inverse identities in every case. Consequently
$\mathcal F_{\rm full}P\mathcal F_{\rm full}^{-1}$ and the
transport of each inverse branch are exact maps on this complete
receiver, and AO11 proves its arithmetic action is
$\mathcal S|_{\mathcal R_I}\oplus\diag(k\rho_l)_{l\in Z}$.
Equip it with the original full corner norm by assigning
$\|(y,c_Z)\|^2=(\mathcal R_{\rm AO}y,c_Z)^*
(J_+^*G_NJ_+)(\mathcal R_{\rm AO}y,c_Z)$.
This retains all cross terms; minimizing over $c_Z$ is exactly
the Schur formula AO13. Thus no invisible direction is deleted
in the completed nonlinear or arithmetic correspondence.

Finally the exact metric action identity CA15 applies with
$B=\widehat B_k$ and $H=\widehat G_N$; its proof used only the
complete polynomial inverse Jacobian. Together with MO2, MO4,
MO7 and MO8 this records all original polynomial, action and
metric residuals in the actual observed realization. The fixed
original linear conductor still has the inverse in FC24.
% END ACTUAL_MARKED_OBSERVATION

% BEGIN ACTUAL_CORNER_OBSERVATION


This calculation uses the original observation $\Lambda_k$, the
original upper corner idempotents, the full original output Gram,
and the actual four conductor corner coefficients. The coefficient
test and conductor lift are the explicit maps in
\cite[O1--3 and O10]{Observe}. The four labelled Fable source
columns are those of \cite{ES488}, and their original-conductor
receivers are retained from the FC/CA sections above. This note does not
replace the upper corner classes by their minimum-norm lifts.
It proves the restriction and its iterated recovery even when
some actual observation columns vanish.

\section{The actual invariant corner subspace}
Fix an original admitted simple quartet
\[
 (\rho_{\mathrm M},\rho_{\mathrm N},\rho_{\mathrm T},\rho_{\mathrm E})
 =\left(\tfrac12+\delta+i\gamma,\tfrac12+\delta-i\gamma,
        \tfrac12-\delta+i\gamma,\tfrac12-\delta-i\gamma\right),
 \quad 0<\delta<\tfrac12,\quad\gamma>2.
\]
At the original degree $k\ge9$ let $e_l\in E_k$ be the four
upper-grid root idempotents with indices
$(k,k),(k,0),(0,k),(0,0)$ in this order. Put
\begin{equation}
 J_+:\C^4\longrightarrow E_k,\quad J_+c=\sum_lc_le_l,
 \qquad D_k=\diag(\lambda_l),\quad\lambda_l=k\rho_l.
 \tag{AO1}
\end{equation}
The original arithmetic action satisfies exactly
$A_kJ_+=J_+D_k$, since each $e_l$ is its actual root idempotent.
All four $\lambda_l$ are distinct. Hence the four-dimensional
image of $J_+$ is invariant under that action.

Let $\mathcal B_k$ be the original output space, with its
complete positive Hermitian Gram matrix $Q$. Set
\begin{equation}
 b_l=\Lambda_k e_l,\qquad
 B=(b_{\mathrm M}\ b_{\mathrm N}\ b_{\mathrm T}\ b_{\mathrm E})
       =\Lambda_kJ_+,
 \qquad I=\{l:b_l\ne0\},\quad m=|I|.
 \tag{AO2}
\end{equation}
The letters $B$ and $Q$ in this note always denote these
actual observation data. No column is normalized. Inner
products are conjugate linear in the first argument.
On the corner subspace, for every integer $n\ge0$,
\begin{equation}
 \Lambda_k A_k^nJ_+c=B D_k^n c.\tag{AO3}
\end{equation}
This is obtained by iterating AO1. It does not require the
one-step kernel of $\Lambda_k$ in the entire $E_k$ to be invariant,
and makes no assertion that the whole observation quotient
inherits $A_k$.

\section{Full stacked Gram and exact recovery with at most four observations}
For $p\ge1$ retain the unscaled stack
\begin{equation}
 Z_p c=(BD_k^t c)_{t=0}^{p-1}\in\mathcal B_k^{\oplus p},
 \qquad Q_p=I_p\otimes Q.
 \tag{AO4}
\end{equation}
Its full original Gram has entries
\begin{equation}
 (K_p)_{lr}=(Z_p^*Q_pZ_p)_{lr}
   =b_l^*Qb_r\sum_{t=0}^{p-1}\overline{\lambda_l}^{\,t}\lambda_r^t.
 \tag{AO5}
\end{equation}
These are exact finite sums with all original root scales and
off-diagonal output correlations retained. If $l\notin I$,
the corresponding row and column are zero.

Suppose first $m>0$. For each $l\in I$ define the actual spectral
separating polynomial and actual output functional
\begin{equation}
 p_l(S)=\prod_{r\in I\setminus\{l\}}
              \frac{S-\lambda_r}{\lambda_l-\lambda_r}
        =\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}p_{l,t}S^t,
 \qquad
 \vartheta_l(y)=\frac{b_l^*Qy}{b_l^*Qb_l}.
 \tag{AO6}
\end{equation}
All denominators are nonzero because the original roots are
distinct and $Q>0$. The exact inverse on the active coordinates is
\begin{equation}
 c_l=\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}p_{l,t}\vartheta_l(y_t),
 \qquad (y_t)_{t=0}^{m-1}=Z_m c,\quad l\in I.
 \tag{AO7}
\end{equation}
To prove it, substitute $y_t=\sum_rb_r\lambda_r^tc_r$.
The sum over $t$ becomes
$\sum_{r\in I}p_l(\lambda_r)\vartheta_l(b_r)c_r=c_l$,
since $p_l(\lambda_r)=\delta_{lr}$ and
$\vartheta_l(b_l)=1$. Thus
\begin{equation}
 \ker Z_m=\operatorname{span}\{e_l^{\rm label}:l\notin I\},
 \qquad K_{m,I}>0.
 \tag{AO8}
\end{equation}
Here $e_l^{\rm label}$ is a coordinate vector of $\C^4$, and
$K_{m,I}$ is the active principal submatrix of AO5.
In particular even linearly dependent or collinear nonzero
$b_l$ are recovered by their original, distinct arithmetic
eigenvalues. At most $m\le4$ observations are needed.
If $m=0$, every stack is identically zero, and AO8 is to be
read as the full four-dimensional kernel; no inverse is assigned.

The polynomial
\begin{equation}
 \chi_I(S)=\prod_{l\in I}(S-\lambda_l)
       =S^m+\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}c_tS^t\tag{AO9}
\end{equation}
is the minimal polynomial of the actual active corner action.
Indeed an annihilating polynomial must vanish at each of its
$m$ distinct eigenvalues; divisibility by their product proves
minimality. The degree-$m-1$ separation in AO6 uses precisely
these roots, without the other $(k+1)^2-4$ grid roots.
For the particular columns the least number of observations is
exactly
\[
 p_{\min}=\min\{1\le p\le m:\rank K_{p,I}=m\}.
\]
This is a finite criterion using the full original matrices.
The upper bound $m$ is sharp in general: when all active columns
are nonzero multiples of one fixed vector, a stack of $p<m$
observations has rank at most $p$, so cannot recover $m$
coordinates. This last example states sharpness of the proved
general bound, not a replacement of the programme's columns.

\section{Exact finite action on the recovered observation space}
Let $\mathcal R_I=\operatorname{im}Z_m$ for $m>0$. Define on the
ambient stack the literal block companion operator
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal S(y_0,\ldots,y_{m-1})
    =(y_1,\ldots,y_{m-1},-\sum_{t=0}^{m-1}c_ty_t),
 \tag{AO10}
\end{equation}
with the $m=1$ formula interpreted as $y_0\mapsto-c_0y_0$.
For actual corner data, AO9 gives
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal S Z_m=Z_mD_k.\tag{AO11}
\end{equation}
The first $m-1$ components are shifts of the original observed
iterates; the last follows from
$\lambda_l^m+\sum_tc_t\lambda_l^t=0$ for every active label.
Consequently $\mathcal R_I$ is invariant, and the restriction
of $\mathcal S$ to it is exactly conjugate to the active
diagonal action $D_{k,I}$ by AO7. Its exponential satisfies
$e^{z\mathcal S}Z_m=Z_m e^{zD_k}$ for every complex $z$ by
the convergent finite-dimensional matrix series. This is a
constructed action on the actual stacked data, not an action
postulated on the whole one-step quotient.

The original inherited stack metric on active coordinates is
$K_{m,I}$. Its complete infinitesimal Hermitian defect is
\begin{equation}
 D_{k,I}^*K_{m,I}+K_{m,I}D_{k,I},\qquad
 [D_{k,I}^*K_{m,I}+K_{m,I}D_{k,I}]_{lr}
       =(\overline{\lambda_l}+\lambda_r)(K_m)_{lr}.
 \tag{AO12}
\end{equation}
Subtracting the original central term $kK_{m,I}$ leaves
$(\overline{\lambda_l}+\lambda_r-k)(K_m)_{lr}$.
On the single active eigenvector of label $l$ its value is
$2k\varepsilon_l\delta\,(K_m)_{ll}$, where
$\varepsilon_l$ is the original real-part sign. This proves
both signs whenever the active set contains both real-part
signs, with the entire original Gram still present.

For completeness, if $H=J_+^*G_{E_k}J_+>0$ is the original
corner Gram of a specified physical source metric, its quotient
by the invisible coordinate subspace has the exact Schur Gram
\begin{equation}
 H_{\rm quot}=H_{II}-H_{IZ}H_{ZZ}^{-1}H_{ZI},
 \qquad Z=I^c.
 \tag{AO13}
\end{equation}
With $Z$ empty this means $H_{II}$; with $I$ empty no inverse
is asserted. Complete the square in
$(c_I,c_Z)^*H(c_I,c_Z)$: its minimum occurs at
$c_Z=-H_{ZZ}^{-1}H_{ZI}c_I$ and gives AO13. This minimum
represents only the norm of the recovered corner quotient;
the receiver AO1 still consists of the original upper
idempotents. The exact squared inverse norm from the actual
stack to this quotient is
\begin{equation}
 \sup_{c_I\ne0}\frac{c_I^*H_{\rm quot}c_I}
                         {c_I^*K_{m,I}c_I}
 =\lambda_{\max}
     (K_{m,I}^{-1/2}H_{\rm quot}K_{m,I}^{-1/2}).\tag{AO14}
\end{equation}
This equality follows by substituting $K_{m,I}^{1/2}c_I$ in
the Rayleigh quotient. It retains both original metrics and
does not assign a small or degree-uniform value to this
finite expression.

\section{What the four actual conductor coefficients prove}
Set $j=k-8$. Let $J_-:\C^4\to E_j$ insert the four lower
corner idempotents in their same marked order. The original
conductor coefficient map has the exact corner restriction
\begin{equation}
 C J_+=J_-D_{\rm edge},\qquad
 D_{\rm edge}=\diag(d_{\mathrm M},d_{\mathrm N},d_{\mathrm T},d_{\mathrm E})
      =\diag(a_{88},a_{80},a_{08},a_{00}).\tag{AO15}
\end{equation}
This is the corner term of the original finite convolution,
with no other shift omitted: an extreme upper index can be
formed only from the corresponding extreme lower index
and the extreme degree-eight coefficient. Equivalently the
four entries equal the original point evaluations
$A_H(He_l)$, proved in FC16a of the FC/CA sections above.

Each nonzero $d_l$ certifies that $b_l\ne0$. To prove this
directly from the original observation, use the conductor
lift \cite[O3]{Observe}. Multiply the actual degree-eight
conductor biform by the lower corner monomial with index
$(j,j),(j,0),(0,j)$ or $(0,0)$. Its coefficient at the matching
upper corner is exactly $d_l$; there is no other contribution
to that extreme coefficient. The original five-term invariant
lift in O3 realizes this biform in $\Theta_k(S_k^G)$.
The original coefficient criterion O1 then gives
\begin{equation}
 I_{\rm edge}:=\{l:d_l\ne0\}\ \subseteq\ I.\tag{AO16}
\end{equation}
If $d_l=0$, this particular coefficient test is zero, but the
full column vanishes only if every original invariant
coefficient test in O1 is zero. Thus $I$ in AO2 is the exact
active set; $I_{\rm edge}$ is a proved subset, not an assumed
equality. On any locus where all four actual coefficients
are nonzero, $I$ consists of all four labels.

The arithmetic shift across AO15 is also exact:
\begin{equation}
 C A_kJ_+-A_j C J_+
       =J_-D_{\rm edge}\diag(8\rho_l).\tag{AO17}
\end{equation}
Indeed use AO1 and its lower counterpart
$A_jJ_-=J_-\diag(j\rho_l)$; the two diagonal matrices commute,
and $k-j=8$. This retains the original additive shift and
does not identify the upper and lower arithmetic actions.

\section{The labelled Fable receiver and all previously computed escapes}
Let the four original Fable collision columns be $q_l=Ke_l^{\rm label}$,
where
\[
 K=\begin{pmatrix}
 0&i&1/\sqrt2&1/\sqrt2\\
 0&-1&-1-\sqrt2i&1-\sqrt2i\\
 i/2&-3i&2\sqrt2+6i&-2\sqrt2+6i\\
 0&13&-34-19\sqrt2i&34-19\sqrt2i
 \end{pmatrix},\qquad \det K=77\sqrt2i/2.
\]
The label-preserving actual upper observation and its stack are
\begin{equation}
 q\longmapsto BK^{-1}q,\qquad
 \mathcal F_m(q)=Z_mK^{-1}q.\tag{AO18}
\end{equation}
At the four marked points these give respectively $b_l$ and
$(\lambda_l^t b_l)_{t=0}^{m-1}$. These formulas retain each
marked source identity; the different raw-coordinate insertion
$q\mapsto Bq$ does not have that property and is not substituted.
Their exact action satisfies
$\mathcal S\mathcal F_m=\mathcal F_m K D_kK^{-1}$ by AO11.

For an arbitrary original direction $v\in\C^4$, AO8 gives
the complete visibility test and its exact norm:
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal F_m(v)=0\iff (K^{-1}v)_l=0\text{ for every }l\in I,
 \qquad
 \|\mathcal F_m(v)\|_{Q_m}^2
    =(K^{-1}v)_I^*K_{m,I}(K^{-1}v)_I.
 \tag{AO19}
\end{equation}
In contrast a one-step observation can cancel nonzero columns;
AO19 is proved for the complete finite stack. The exact
one-step visibility test is simply $BK^{-1}v\ne0$, with
its full vector sum retained.

The two leading directions of the established Fable escapes
have particularly useful coefficients. With the original raw
coordinate vectors $e_a=(1,0,0,0)^{\mathsf T}$ and
$e_w=(0,0,0,1)^{\mathsf T}$, direct multiplication by $K$ gives
\begin{align}
 c_a:=K^{-1}e_a
 &=\left(-4\sqrt2-\frac{180i}{77},\ -\frac{30i}{77},\
          \frac{47\sqrt2-64i}{154},\
          \frac{47\sqrt2+64i}{154}\right)^{\mathsf T},\nonumber\\
 c_w:=K^{-1}e_w
 &=\left(\frac6{77},\frac1{77},\
          \frac{-3-\sqrt2i}{154},\
          \frac{3-\sqrt2i}{154}\right)^{\mathsf T}.
 \tag{AO20}
\end{align}
Every one of these eight entries is nonzero. Therefore either
direction is visible in the actual $m$-stack as soon as even
one actual corner column is nonzero. Its exact positive norm
is given by AO19. If $I$ is empty, both and every other
corner direction are invisible to all iterates.

In particular for the original sign-partner path
\[
 \widehat q_\epsilon=(-\epsilon,2i/\epsilon,
                     6i/\epsilon^2-i/2,40i/\epsilon^3),
 \qquad\epsilon>0,
\]
the literal vector limit $\epsilon^3\widehat q_\epsilon\to40ie_w$
and the fixed linearity of AO18 prove
\begin{equation}
 \epsilon^3\|\mathcal F_m(\widehat q_\epsilon)\|_{Q_m}
 \longrightarrow40\sqrt{(c_w)_I^*K_{m,I}(c_w)_I}>0
 \quad(I\ne\varnothing).
 \tag{AO21}
\end{equation}
For all eight auxiliary quartet-limit branches of GF25 above,
with the original receiver held fixed, the established coordinate
limit is $\sqrt\tau\,q_{\varepsilon,\eta,\pm}(\tau)
\to\kappa_{\varepsilon,\pm}e_a$, where
$|\kappa_{\varepsilon,\pm}|=1/(2\gamma_{\rm aux})$.
Here $\tau$ is the auxiliary quartet separation, not a change
in the fixed original arithmetic $\delta$ of AO1. Their exact
observed escape constants are
\begin{equation}
 \sqrt\tau\,\|\mathcal F_m(q_{\varepsilon,\eta,\pm}(\tau))\|_{Q_m}
 \longrightarrow\frac{\sqrt{(c_a)_I^*K_{m,I}(c_a)_I}}
                         {2\gamma_{\rm aux}}>0.
 \tag{AO22}
\end{equation}
The selected-root collision trajectories of CA13 above
have $(t-t_*)^{1/4}q(t)\to\kappa e_a$ on the retained branch;
the identical argument gives constant
$|\kappa|\sqrt{(c_a)_I^*K_{m,I}(c_a)_I}$ with the original
quarter-power rate. Every limit here follows from the actual
source coordinates and a fixed finite linear map, so no
estimate of an unproved one-step quotient action is involved.

These conclusions also have a direct conductor counterpart.
For any prescribed original positive lower-corner Gram
$H_->0$, the marked conductor image is
$J_-D_{\rm edge}K^{-1}q$. Its exact squared norm in a raw
direction $v$ is
\begin{equation}
 (K^{-1}v)^*D_{\rm edge}^*H_-D_{\rm edge}(K^{-1}v).
 \tag{AO23}
\end{equation}
For $v=e_a$ or $e_w$ this is strictly positive if and only
if at least one of the four explicit original $d_l$ is
nonzero, because AO20 has no zero entry and $H_->0$.
If all four $d_l$ vanish, the entire marked corner conductor
image is zero; the actual observation may still be nonzero,
and its definitive test remains AO2 and AO19. If even one
$d_l$ is nonzero, AO16 certifies that both Fable escape
directions survive the original finite observation stack.
On the explicit domain where all four $d_l$ are nonzero,
all four marked coordinates are recovered, with the exact
inverse AO7 and full original norm cost AO14.

\section{Exact coefficient certificate and provenance}
The accompanying certificate
\texttt{verify\_actual\_corner\_observation.py} verifies
the two complete columns AO20 over $\mathbb Q(i,\sqrt2)$,
their nonzero entries, and the actual quartet Vandermonde
determinant $-64k^6\delta^2\gamma^2(\delta^2+\gamma^2)$.
The Gram, recovery, and action assertions are proved above
for arbitrary actual columns and arbitrary positive original
$Q$; no sampled Gram substitutes for those proofs.
