% BEGIN EXACT_MARKED_SPECIALIZATION
\section{The exact two-leg specialization}
Original polynomial and marked points: The Clankers \cite{ES488}.


This note constructs two explicit target paths for the original
ES--Fable polynomial. Their concatenated specialization has
eight, then seven, then three finite points in the original
source chart. It is not the single arithmetic trajectory in
MO12 of the preceding sections: both paths and all their signs are
specified here. In particular its second-leg escape exponent
differs from that transverse arithmetic trajectory.

\section{The original map and its exact fibre coordinates}
Use the original source coordinates $(a,y,z,w)$. Its complete
factor-coordinate formulas and polynomial map are
\begin{align}
 b&=i+ay,&c&=-i+2ay+a^2z,\nonumber\\
 d&=-iy-a(iz+2y^2)-a^2yz,&e&=2z-7iy^2+aw,\nonumber\\
 f&=iw+3iy^3-4yz+a(6iy^2z+wy+4y^4)+2a^2y^3z,&&\nonumber\\
 P(a,y,z,w)&=(ac,ae+bd,af+be,bf).
 &&\tag{VA1}
\end{align}
Here $L=aU+bV$, $C=cU^3+dU^2V+eUV^2+fV^3$ satisfy
$ad+bc=1$ and $C(-b,a)=1$, directly by substitution. Thus
their product at target $u=(A,B,C_1,D)$ is
$H_u=AU^4+U^3V+BU^2V^2+C_1UV^3+DV^4$.
The symbol $C_1$ is the third target coefficient, distinct
from the cubic factor denoted $C$. No source coordinate or
polynomial coefficient has been changed in VA1.

For a simple finite root $r$ of
$h_u(x)=Ax^4+x^3+Bx^2+C_1x+D$, the two inverse states are
\begin{align}
 a^2&=1/h_u'(r),&b&=-ar,&c&=A/a,&d&=(1+Ar)/a,\nonumber\\
 e&=(B+r+Ar^2)/a,& f&=(C_1+Br+r^2+Ar^3)/a,&&\nonumber\\
 y&=-r-i/a,&z&=A/a^3+2r/a+3i/a^2,&&\nonumber\\
 w&=7ir^2/a+(B-17r+Ar^2)/a^2-13i/a^3-2A/a^4.
 &&\tag{VA2}
\end{align}
Both nonzero square roots for $a$ are retained. To prove
completeness, divide $H_u$ by $a(U-rV)$ and compare successive
coefficients to obtain the six factor entries in VA2.
The resultant is $C(-b,a)=a^2h_u'(r)$, which must be one;
thus a multiple root gives no state. The original chart inverse
$y=(b-i)/a$, $z=(c+i-2ay)/a^2$,
$w=(e-2z+7iy^2)/a$ gives the remaining entries of VA2.
All operations are reversible for $a\ne0$.
At $A=0$ the root at infinity is simple, because the original
cubic coefficient is one. Its positive-chart state is exactly
\[
 q_\infty(A=0,B,C_1,D)
 =\left(0,B,\frac{i(7B^2-C_1)}2,11B^3-2BC_1-D\right).
\]
Indeed VA1 at $a=0$ gives
$P=(0,y,2iz+7y^2,-4iyz-w-3y^3)$, which solves to this formula.
The opposite factor sign has $b=-i$ and is outside the original
chart, since $a=0$ forces $b=i$ in VA1. Therefore every
simple finite root contributes two source states and infinity
contributes one in this chart.

\section{First leg: eight states specialize to the original seven}
For $0<\epsilon<1/2$ put $m_\epsilon=1-\epsilon^2/2$ and
$d_\epsilon=\sqrt{4-9\epsilon^2/4}>0$. The first target path,
its factorization and its four distinct marked roots are
\begin{align}
 u^{(1)}_\epsilon
   &=(-i\epsilon+i\epsilon^3/2,3i\epsilon/2,-1,0)
                \longrightarrow h_0=(0,0,-1,0),\nonumber\\
 h^{(1)}_\epsilon(x)
   &=(\epsilon x+i)x
             \left(-im_\epsilon x^2+\frac\epsilon2 x+i\right),\nonumber\\
 r_{\mathrm M}&=-i/\epsilon,&r_{\mathrm N}&=0,&
 r_{\mathrm T}&=\frac{d_\epsilon-i\epsilon/2}{2m_\epsilon},&
 r_{\mathrm E}&=\frac{-d_\epsilon-i\epsilon/2}{2m_\epsilon},\nonumber\\
 \operatorname{Disc} H_{u^{(1)}_\epsilon}&=4-9\epsilon^2/4>0.
 &&\tag{VA3}
\end{align}
The factorization follows by multiplying its displayed factors.
The quadratic formula gives the last two roots. The full
quartic discriminant is obtained from the resultant of this
polynomial and its derivative; substitution and expansion
gives exactly the last line of VA3. Its leading coefficient
$-i\epsilon m_\epsilon$ is nonzero on the stated interval.
Consequently all four roots are finite and distinct and VA2
gives exactly eight source states, with no root collision
anywhere before the endpoint. Their full inverse coordinates
are VA2 evaluated at the four explicit roots VA3 and both
values of $a^2=1/(h^{(1)}_\epsilon)'(r)$.

For the M root these two original-coordinate states simplify
completely to
\[
 q_{\mathrm M,+}(\epsilon)=(\epsilon,0,i/2,0),\qquad
 q_{\mathrm M,-}(\epsilon)
 =(-\epsilon,2i/\epsilon,6i/\epsilon^2-i/2,40i/\epsilon^3).
\]
Substitution in VA1 proves both have image VA3. Equivalently
their selected derivatives are $\epsilon^{-2}$ and their
factor scalings are $a=\pm\epsilon$. Their factor states
are negatives of each other. The positive one tends to
$(0,i,-i,0,i,0)$; the negative one tends to
$(0,-i,i,0,-i,0)$, a finite point in the factor completion
excluded from the original chart. Only its chart coordinates
escape. For the three remaining roots, VA3 gives limits
$0,1,-1$ and nonzero derivative limits $-1,2,2$.
Every expression in VA2 then has a finite limit on each
retained square-root branch. Thus exactly one of the eight
source states escapes, and seven remain finite at $h_0$.

\section{The complete signed marking at the junction}
The four original displayed collision points are
\[
\begin{aligned}
 q_{\mathrm M}&=(0,0,i/2,0),&q_{\mathrm N}&=(i,-1,-3i,13),\\
 q_{\mathrm T}&=(1/\sqrt2,-1-\sqrt2i,2\sqrt2+6i,-34-19\sqrt2i),\\
 q_{\mathrm E}&=(1/\sqrt2,1-\sqrt2i,-2\sqrt2+6i,34-19\sqrt2i).
\end{aligned}
\]
Define the original linear involution
$J(a,y,z,w)=(-a,-y,z,-w)$. The exact marked factor-root
associations and factor states at $h_0$ are
\begin{equation}
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
\text{label}&\text{root}&\text{positive factor state }(a,b,c,d,e,f)
                         &\text{two source-chart states}\\\hline
\mathrm M&\infty&(0,i,-i,0,i,0)&q_{\mathrm M},\ \text{opposite sign omitted}\\
\mathrm N&0&(i,0,0,-i,0,i)&q_{\mathrm N},\ Jq_{\mathrm N}\\
\mathrm T&1&(1/\sqrt2,-1/\sqrt2,0,\sqrt2,\sqrt2,0)
                       &q_{\mathrm T},\ Jq_{\mathrm E}\\
\mathrm E&-1&(1/\sqrt2,1/\sqrt2,0,\sqrt2,-\sqrt2,0)
                       &q_{\mathrm E},\ Jq_{\mathrm T}
\end{array}\tag{VA4}
\end{equation}
The negative factor state in each row is the negative of
every one of its six displayed entries. Multiplying $L$ and
$C$ in every row gives $UV(U-V)(U+V)$, with resultant one.
Substituting the original source states in VA1 recovers these
rows and their negatives. This proves all associations in
VA4. In particular $J$ interchanges the T/E factor-root
marking; $Jq_{\mathrm T}$ is not the T negative-sign state.
The missing M negative factor state is the one displayed
at the end of the preceding section. Thus all eight signed
factor states are described, and exactly seven of them
belong to the original source chart.

\section{Second leg: seven states specialize to three finite states}
Take $0\le s<1$ and put $\tau=1-s\in(0,1]$. The second
target path is
$u^{(2)}(s)=(0,s,s-1,0)=(0,1-\tau,-\tau,0)$.
It starts at $h_0$ when $s=0$ and tends to
$u_*=(0,1,0,0)$ when $s\uparrow1$. Its complete polynomial
and all seven inverse states are
\begin{align}
 h^{(2)}_\tau(x)&=x(x-\tau)(x+1),&
 \operatorname{Disc} H_{u^{(2)}}&=\tau^2(1+\tau)^2>0,\nonumber\\
 q_{\mathrm M}(\tau)&=
 \left(0,1-\tau,\frac{i(7(1-\tau)^2+\tau)}2,
                      11(1-\tau)^3+2\tau(1-\tau)\right),\nonumber\\
 q_{\mathrm N,\sigma}(\tau)&=
 \left(\frac{\sigma i}{\sqrt\tau},-\sigma\sqrt\tau,-3i\tau,
                     -\tau+\tau^2+13\sigma\tau^{3/2}\right),\nonumber\\
 q_{\mathrm T,\sigma}(\tau)&=
 \left(\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{\tau(1+\tau)}},
       -\tau-i\sigma\sqrt{\tau(1+\tau)},\right.\nonumber\\
 &\hspace{12mm}2\sigma\tau^{3/2}\sqrt{1+\tau}+3i\tau(1+\tau),
 \left.\tau(1+\tau)(1-18\tau)
      -i\sigma\tau^{3/2}\sqrt{1+\tau}(13+6\tau)\right),\nonumber\\
 q_{\mathrm E,\sigma}(\tau)&=
 \left(\frac{\sigma}{\sqrt{1+\tau}},1-i\sigma\sqrt{1+\tau},
       -2\sigma\sqrt{1+\tau}+3i(1+\tau),\right.\nonumber\\
 &\hspace{12mm}\left.(18-\tau)(1+\tau)
               -i\sigma\sqrt{1+\tau}(6+13\tau)\right),
 \qquad\sigma\in\{1,-1\}.\tag{VA5}
\end{align}
All real square roots in VA5 are the positive ones; $\sigma$
retains both actual signs. The finite roots are precisely
$0,\tau,-1$ and their derivatives are $-\tau,\tau(1+\tau),1+\tau$.
They are pairwise distinct for every $0<\tau\le1$, and
their squared pair differences give the discriminant in
VA5. Substitution of these roots and both scalings into
VA2 gives every displayed finite-root formula. The infinity
formula after VA2 gives $q_{\mathrm M}(\tau)$.
Thus VA5 is the complete fibre and has no other root
coalescence before its endpoint.

At $\tau=1$ these formulas agree exactly with VA4:
the N positive and negative states are $q_{\mathrm N}$ and
$Jq_{\mathrm N}$; the T states are $q_{\mathrm T}$ and
$Jq_{\mathrm E}$; the E states are $q_{\mathrm E}$ and
$Jq_{\mathrm T}$. At $\tau=0$ the N and T roots collide.
Their four original source states escape, since their
$a$ coordinates diverge. The three remaining finite limits are
\[
 (0,1,7i/2,11),\qquad
 (\sigma,1-i\sigma,-2\sigma+3i,18-6i\sigma),
 \qquad\sigma\in\{1,-1\}.
\]
The complete fibre rule proved after VA2 gives exactly these
three states at the endpoint: the double root zero gives
none, the simple root $-1$ gives two, and infinity gives
the single retained chart state. The N and T factor
coefficients $a$ themselves diverge, so these four escapes
persist in the two-chart factor completion. The opposite
infinity sign was already outside the original chart all
along this second leg and is not a further escape on it.

\section{Exact fixed original-Gram transfer of the two legs}
Let $F:\C^4\to V$ be any one of the already constructed
fixed injective original receiving maps, with its unchanged
physical Hermitian inner product. Put $G=F^*H_VF>0$.
For example $F$ may be the fixed original conductor map
$\Psi_*$, or the actual injective marked observer $F_B$
on its proved nonzero-corner domain. Its full Gram, including
off-diagonal terms and original mass, is retained below.
Write $e_a=(1,0,0,0)^{\mathsf T}$,
$e_y=(0,1,0,0)^{\mathsf T}$,
$e_z=(0,0,1,0)^{\mathsf T}$ and
$e_w=(0,0,0,1)^{\mathsf T}$. The exact limiting vectors
and norm constants are
\begin{align}
 \epsilon^3q_{\mathrm M,-}(\epsilon)&\longrightarrow40ie_w,&
 \epsilon^3\|Fq_{\mathrm M,-}(\epsilon)\|
                       &\longrightarrow40\sqrt{G_{44}},\nonumber\\
 \sqrt\tau\,q_{\mathrm N,\sigma}(\tau)&\longrightarrow\sigma i e_a,&
 \sqrt\tau\,q_{\mathrm T,\sigma}(\tau)&\longrightarrow\sigma e_a,\nonumber\\
 \sqrt\tau\,\|Fq_{\mathrm N,\sigma}(\tau)\|
    &\longrightarrow\sqrt{G_{11}},&
 \sqrt\tau\,\|Fq_{\mathrm T,\sigma}(\tau)\|
    &\longrightarrow\sqrt{G_{11}},\nonumber\\
 \|F(u^{(2)}(s)-u_*)\|
   &=\tau\sqrt{(e_y+e_z)^*G(e_y+e_z)},&&\nonumber\\
 \|F(u^{(2)}(s)-u_*)\|^{1/2}\|Fq_{l,\sigma}(\tau)\|
   &\longrightarrow
     \bigl((e_y+e_z)^*G(e_y+e_z)\bigr)^{1/4}\sqrt{G_{11}},
 &&l\in\{\mathrm N,\mathrm T\}.
 \tag{VA6}
\end{align}
The vector limits are immediate from the complete coordinates
above. Applying the fixed linear $F$ and continuity of its
original norm proves each limit, with positive constants
because $F$ is injective. The target-distance equality is
exact at every $\tau$, since
$u^{(2)}(s)-u_*=-\tau(e_y+e_z)$.
In particular its coefficient is
$G_{22}+G_{33}+2\Re G_{23}$ under the square root;
the off-diagonal metric term has not been deleted.

These statements prove an exact two-leg specialization
$8\to7\to3$, including its missing factor sign and its
four original root-collision escapes. They do not claim a
single flow trajectory traverses those two paths. The
literal arithmetic orbit through $h_0$ has seven entire
finite trajectories, as proved in MO11 of the preceding sections.
The separate transverse arithmetic orbit MO12--19 has
four quarter-power escapes, one cubic chart escape and
three finite limits at $u_*$. The second path VA5 is
tangent to the discriminant: its discriminant vanishes
quadratically in $\tau$ and its four escape rates are
$\tau^{-1/2}$, exactly as VA6 proves. These are compatible
specializations of the same original polynomial, with
their different paths and rates retained explicitly.

The exact certificate
\texttt{verify\_exact\_marked\_specialization.py} verifies
the two first-leg M states, both discriminants, and all
seven original-coordinate second-leg formulas. For the
latter it uses $t=\sqrt\tau$ and $b=\sqrt{1+\tau}$ and
reduces every polynomial numerator modulo $b^2-t^2-1$
over $\mathbb Q(i)$; no numerical sample replaces an
identity. Its captured output is
\texttt{verify\_exact\_marked\_specialization\_output.txt}.
% END EXACT_MARKED_SPECIALIZATION

% BEGIN RATIONAL_NATIVE_RECEIVER
\section{The rational test receiver with the full original Gamma metric}
The received continuation \cite[Equations (33)--(36)]{ResidueIntake}
proposes a rational-function receiver of the signed object. Here its
entire coefficient matrix, inverse, original metric, graph kernel and
spectral comparison are calculated. No positivity claim from that
intake is used in this calculation. Its full-Weil form below is the
actual zero sum, including all multiplicities.

Retain the original $a_{\rm amp}$, $s\in\{1,a_{\rm amp}\}$,
$v$, $c_*=a_{\rm amp}/2-4$, $\kappa=c_*-1/2$,
the original measure $dm_s$ in FC28, and the exact maps
$\Phi,\Psi$ in FC32. For the test pole set
\[
 \omega=\sigma+i\tau,\quad\sigma>1,\quad
 h=2\sigma-1>0,\quad b_\omega=\bar\omega-1,\quad
 d=\sigma-\tfrac12=h/2.
 \tag{RW1}
\]
The positive square root $\sqrt h$ is retained. The symbol $s$ in
$dm_s$ is the original Gamma parameter; the complex variable of
the rational functions will be $z$ throughout this section.
Define, for $0\le j\le n$,
\[
 N_j^{(n)}(z)=\sqrt h\,(z+b_\omega)^j(\omega-z)^{n-j},\qquad
 \varphi_j^\omega(z)=\frac{N_j^{(n)}(z)}{(\omega-z)^{n+1}}
 =\frac{\sqrt h}{\omega-z}
           \left(\frac{z+b_\omega}{\omega-z}\right)^j.
 \tag{RW2}
\]
In particular this defines the same rational function for every
$n\ge j$; it does not change the denominator convention of a
fixed finite numerator space.

Let $R_\omega^{(n)}$ take a column $d_0,\ldots,d_n$ to the
coefficients of $\sum_jd_jN_j^{(n)}$ in $(1,z,\ldots,z^n)$.
Every entry and every inverse entry is the following finite sum:
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 (R_\omega^{(n)})_{\ell j}
  &=\sqrt h\sum_{r=0}^j
    \binom jr b_\omega^{j-r}
    \binom{n-j}{\ell-r}\omega^{n-j-\ell+r}(-1)^{\ell-r},\\
 ((R_\omega^{(n)})^{-1})_{j\ell}
  &=h^{-n-1/2}\sum_{r=0}^{\ell}
     \binom\ell r\omega^r(-b_\omega)^{\ell-r}
                         \binom{n-\ell}{j-r}.
 \end{aligned}\tag{RW3}
\]
In the first line only indices with $0\le\ell-r\le n-j$
are included, so there is no negative power hidden behind a
vanishing binomial coefficient. Binomial coefficients outside
their nonnegative range in the second line are zero.
Expansion of RW2 proves the first formula. For the inverse put
$u=(z+b_\omega)/(\omega-z)$, so
$z=(\omega u-b_\omega)/(1+u)$ and
$\omega-z=h/(1+u)$. For an arbitrary numerator $p$, the polynomial
\[
 h^{-n-1/2}(1+u)^n
                   p\left(\frac{\omega u-b_\omega}{1+u}\right)
\]
has coefficient column $d$. Expanding each monomial of $p$
gives exactly RW3. Substitution back proves both inverse identities.

The determinant, with the original coefficient order, is
\[
 \det R_\omega^{(n)}=h^{(n+1)^2/2},\qquad
 R_\omega^{(3)}=\sqrt h
 \begin{pmatrix}
 \omega^3&b_\omega\omega^2&b_\omega^2\omega&b_\omega^3\\
 -3\omega^2&\omega^2-2b_\omega\omega&2b_\omega\omega-b_\omega^2&3b_\omega^2\\
 3\omega&b_\omega-2\omega&\omega-2b_\omega&3b_\omega\\
 -1&1&-1&1
 \end{pmatrix}.
 \tag{RW4}
\]
For a direct determinant proof use $t=\omega-z$. The $j$th
polynomial is $\sqrt h(h-t)^jt^{n-j}$. Reversing the column
order makes its coefficient matrix triangular with diagonal
$\sqrt h h^j$. Reversal contributes
$(-1)^{n(n+1)/2}$, and changing $t$ back to $z$ contributes the
same sign, because the substitution matrix has diagonal $(-1)^\ell$.
Their product is positive and the remaining power is
$(n+1)/2+n(n+1)/2=(n+1)^2/2$. This proves RW4 without dropping
the square-root factor or changing a coefficient basis.

\section{Exact conductor return and the native Toeplitz matrix}
On $\mathcal P_n$ let
\[
 (C_\kappa)_{r\ell}=\binom\ell r\kappa^{\ell-r}\quad(r\le\ell),
 \qquad C_\kappa^{-1}=C_{-\kappa},\quad\det C_\kappa=1.
 \tag{RW5}
\]
This is precisely $p(S')\mapsto p(z+\kappa)$, by the binomial
formula; composition of translations proves the inverse.
For the four-dimensional receiver take $n=3$ and write $R=R_\omega^{(3)}$.
With the original monomial realization of $\Psi$, define
\[
 B_\omega=R^{-1}C_\kappa\Psi,\quad
 B_\omega^{-1}=\Psi^{-1}C_{-\kappa}R,\qquad
 B_{\omega,U}=R^{-1}C_\kappa T_A\Phi=B_\omega.
 \tag{RW6}
\]
Thus an original target state $p=\Psi q$ has the exact rational
image $p(z+\kappa)/(\omega-z)^4=\sum_j(B_\omega q)_j\varphi_j^\omega(z)$.
Equation RW6 is the original conductor commutation, including
all the moments in $\Psi$ and $T_A$; it is not an identification
of $P$ with $T_A$.

Let $\mathcal H$ be the original target monomial Gram in FC56a,
and $G=\Psi^*\mathcal H\Psi$. In rational coefficient coordinates
the original physical target norm has the exact positive Gram
\[
 H_\omega=B_\omega^{-*}GB_\omega^{-1}
       =R^*C_{-\kappa}^*\mathcal HC_{-\kappa}R.
 \tag{RW7}
\]
To evaluate it, put $S'=c_*+iy$ in the unchanged original
measure. Since $S'-\kappa=1/2+iy$, the actual numerator
polynomial is $N_j^{(3)}(1/2+iy)$. Its two factors are
$d+i(y-\tau)$ and $d-i(y-\tau)$, which are conjugate.
Consequently all sixteen entries are
\[
 (H_\omega)_{ij}=g_{j-i},\qquad
 g_r=h\int_{\R}(d+i(y-\tau))^{3+r}
                    (d-i(y-\tau))^{3-r}\,dm_s(y),
 \quad -3\le r\le3,\quad g_{-r}=\bar g_r.
 \tag{RW8}
\]
Thus the original metric itself is Toeplitz in this basis.
This is a proved identity, not a unitary-metric substitution.
The original center moved through the stated translation;
the measure, its total mass and every correlation remain.

Here is a complete finite evaluation of RW8. Write
$\mu_j(\tau)=\int(y-\tau)^jdm_s(y)$ and retain
\[
 \mu_j(\tau)=\sum_{\ell=0}^j\binom j\ell(-\tau)^{j-\ell}m_\ell,
 \quad m_0=M_s=(2\pi)^{s/2},\quad m_2=M_s\beta,
 \quad m_4=M_s(3\beta^2+2\beta),\quad
 m_6=M_s(15\beta^3+30\beta^2+16\beta),\quad\beta=s/2,
 \tag{RW9}
\]
where all odd $m_\ell$ vanish. These are exactly the proved
original moments of FC56a, with their cited DLMF provenance
\cite{WCF}. In terms of these full shifted moments,
\[
 g_r=h\sum_{p=0}^{3+r}\sum_{q=0}^{3-r}
 \binom{3+r}p\binom{3-r}q d^{6-p-q}i^p(-i)^q\mu_{p+q}(\tau).
 \tag{RW10}
\]
Every sum is finite and includes all odd shifted moments.
It is obtained by expanding the two factors in RW8, so it
specifies every entry without a quadrature or asymptotic error.
For example $g_0=h(d^6m_0+3d^4\mu_2+3d^2\mu_4+\mu_6)$.

The full determinant is also explicit:
\[
 \det H_\omega
 =h^{16}\prod_{j=0}^3\bigl[M_s j!(s/2)_j\bigr]
 =12h^{16}M_s^4\beta^3(\beta+1)^2(\beta+2)>0.
 \tag{RW11}
\]
Indeed RW7, RW4 and $\det C_{-\kappa}=1$ give
$\det H_\omega=h^{16}\det\mathcal H$; the exact monomial
Gram determinant is the product of the original monic
orthogonal squared norms in FC28--29. This proves independence
of $\tau$ for the determinant, not bounded conditioning as
$|\tau|$ grows. Each entry in RW8 still retains its full height
dependence.

\section{The same exact comparison for the full eight-state object}
Define the actual full-Weil form of the rational functions by
\[
 (T_3(\omega))_{ij}
 =\sum_{\rho}m_\rho\overline{\varphi_i^\omega(1-\bar\rho)}
                                     \varphi_j^\omega(\rho).
 \tag{RW12}
\]
Every sum includes the complete nontrivial zero multiset.
For fixed $\omega,i,j$ each summand is $O(|\rho|^{-2})$:
both pole factors have size comparable to $|\Im\rho|$, and
the finite powers of their ratio are bounded for large height.
For completeness the convergence needed here also follows
directly from the classical theta integral used in the residue
calculation below. Its Gaussian bound gives, for $|z|\le R$,
$|\xi(z)|\le\exp(CR\log(R+2))$ with a fixed constant $C$:
bound the power of $x\ge1$ by $x^{\lceil(R+1)/2\rceil}$,
integrate against $e^{-\pi x}$ and use $k!\le k^k$.
Jensen's formula centered at $\xi(0)=1/2$, on a circle between
radii $2R$ and $3R$ avoiding zeros, then gives
$N(R)\log2\le C'(R+1)\log(R+2)$ for the zero count with
multiplicity in $|z|\le R$. Splitting into annuli
$2^j\le|\rho|<2^{j+1}$ proves
$\sum_\rho m_\rho|\rho|^{-2}<\infty$, because
$\sum_j(j+1)2^{-j}<\infty$. This is the same absolute
convergence underlying the classical canonical product
\cite[25.2.12]{DLMFXi}. Reflection of the zero multiset
proves it Hermitian by changing $\rho$ to $1-\bar\rho$.
The exact identity
$\overline{U_\omega(1-\bar\rho)}=U_\omega(\rho)^{-1}$,
where $U_\omega(z)=(z+b_\omega)/(\omega-z)$, also proves
\[
 (T_3(\omega))_{ij}=c_{j-i},\qquad
 c_r=h\sum_\rho m_\rho
       \frac{(\rho+b_\omega)^{r-1}}{(\omega-\rho)^{r+1}},
 \quad c_{-r}=\bar c_r.
 \tag{RW13}
\]
Neither denominator vanishes in the open critical strip, since
$\sigma>1$. This verifies the exact form needed for the
receiving calculation; finite arithmetic evaluation and tail
bounds of $c_r$ belong to the separately retained full-Weil
calculation, not to a claim that these sums have been numerically
evaluated here.

Retain both original label and evaluation coordinates of SE/TC.
Let $E,O$ be their full mean and difference matrices, and let
$G_Y=Y^*\langle\ ,\ \rangle_{\Gamma,W}Y>0$.
The complete original target direct-sum Gram and Weil form are
\[
 \mathbb H=
 \begin{pmatrix}I&0\\E&O\end{pmatrix}^{\!*}
 \begin{pmatrix}G_Y&0\\0&G\end{pmatrix}
 \begin{pmatrix}I&0\\E&O\end{pmatrix},\qquad
 \mathbb W=[E\ O]^*B_\omega^*T_3(\omega)B_\omega[E\ O].
 \tag{RW14}
\]
The original $O$ is invertible by SE5 on its stated quartet
domain. The exact simultaneous change and inverse are
\[
 S_\omega=\begin{pmatrix}I&0\\-O^{-1}E&O^{-1}B_\omega^{-1}\end{pmatrix},
 \qquad
 S_\omega^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}I&0\\B_\omega E&B_\omega O\end{pmatrix}.
 \tag{RW15}
\]
Multiplying either way proves the inverse; multiplying by the
two actual receiving matrices proves simultaneously
\[
 S_\omega^*\mathbb W S_\omega=\diag(0_4,T_3(\omega)),\qquad
 S_\omega^*\mathbb H S_\omega=\diag(G_Y,H_\omega).
 \tag{RW16}
\]
Thus no positive physical norm has been discarded to obtain
the Weil congruence. In the full original native metric the
generalized eigenvalues are four zeros together with those
of $H_\omega^{-1/2}T_3(\omega)H_\omega^{-1/2}$.
To prove this, apply the positive square root of each diagonal
block in RW16 to the equation $\mathbb Wv=\lambda\mathbb Hv$.
Both transformations are invertible; the resulting equation is
the direct sum of a zero Hermitian matrix and the displayed
Hermitian matrix. If $T_3$ itself has a kernel, the total
nullity is $4+\dim\ker T_3$, and the complete original kernel is
\[
 \left\{\left(\alpha,-O^{-1}E\alpha+
          O^{-1}B_\omega^{-1}d\right):\ d\in\ker T_3\right\}.
 \tag{RW17}
\]
In particular the four-dimensional evaluation graph is retained
exactly, without claiming that it exhausts every possible Weil
kernel. Positive and negative inertia are those of the actual
four-dimensional $T_3$. This statement is an exact consequence
of two constructed forms, not a supposition about their signs.

For every $d$ the canonical lift with zero retained-label
coordinate is the explicit original state
$(0,O^{-1}B_\omega^{-1}d)$. Its physical target polynomial is
$C_{-\kappa}Rd$, and its original conductor source is
\[
 T_A^{-1}C_{-\kappa}Rd,
 \tag{RW18}
\]
where the full original inverse FC24 supplies every coefficient
and its source norm. Hence any evaluated test vector is carried
back through the actual conductor, rather than only through a
formal Hermitian congruence. For degrees $n>3$, RW2--5 use the
actual $\mathcal P_n$ conductor restriction in FC21 and FC24;
the original eight-state space itself stays eight-dimensional.

The actual original source direct-sum norm is retained as well.
Let $G_X=X^*\langle\ ,\ \rangle_{\Gamma,U}X$ and
$G_U=\Phi^*\langle\ ,\ \rangle_{\Gamma,U}\Phi$, where the
bracket is the original quotient norm. Its full Gram and
transported rational coefficient Gram are
\[
 \mathbb H_U=
 \begin{pmatrix}I&0\\E&O\end{pmatrix}^{\!*}
 \begin{pmatrix}G_X&0\\0&G_U\end{pmatrix}
 \begin{pmatrix}I&0\\E&O\end{pmatrix},\qquad
 H_{U,\omega}=B_\omega^{-*}G_UB_\omega^{-1}.
 \tag{RW19}
\]
The source state represented by $(\alpha,\beta)$ is
$(X\alpha,\Phi(E\alpha+O\beta))$. Applying $T_A$ on
each component gives exactly the target state
$(Y\alpha,\Psi(E\alpha+O\beta))$, because
$T_AX=Y$ and $T_A\Phi=\Psi$.
Consequently the same explicit matrix RW15 proves
\[
 S_\omega^*\mathbb H_U S_\omega=\diag(G_X,H_{U,\omega}),
 \qquad
 S_\omega^*\mathbb W S_\omega=\diag(0_4,T_3).
 \tag{RW20}
\]
No mixed source--target direct-sum metric is being substituted.
The actual source generalized eigenvalues are four zeros and
those of $H_{U,\omega}^{-1/2}T_3H_{U,\omega}^{-1/2}$, by
the same invertible square-root argument used for RW16.
Its kernel is still exactly RW17. The complete entries and
all source height constants of $H_{U,\omega}$ are evaluated
in NH26 onward using the original FC24 inverse and quotient
Gram. Thus the two original sides of the conductor have
separate fully specified metrics and the same exact Weil receiver.
% END RATIONAL_NATIVE_RECEIVER

% BEGIN RATIONAL_NATIVE_HEIGHT
\section{Exact target and source height spectra}

This calculation evaluates the entire high-height spectrum of the
original native Gram in RW7--11 of
\texttt{RATIONAL\_NATIVE\_RECEIVER\_BODY.tex}. Its polynomial
basis, original Gamma measure, center translation, phases, and total
mass remain exactly those of that receiver. The original Gamma
moments are those proved in FC56a and WCF \cite{WCF}, with the
human source of the orthogonal-polynomial dictionary recorded there.
No assertion about the sign of the separate full-Weil form is used.

\section{The exact original coefficient map and Gram}
Fix $\sigma>1$, let $\omega=\sigma+i\tau$ with $\tau\in\R$,
and retain
\[
 h=2\sigma-1,\qquad d=\sigma-\tfrac12=h/2,\qquad
 b=s/2>0,\qquad M=(2\pi)^{s/2}.
 \tag{NH1}
\]
Here $s$ is the fixed original Gamma parameter. The original measure is
\[
 dm_s(y)=\frac{M2^{s/2}}{4\pi\Gamma(s/2)}
               |\Gamma(s/4+iy/2)|^2dy.
 \tag{NH2}
\]
It has the original moments
\[
 m_0=M,\quad m_2=Mb,\quad m_4=M(3b^2+2b),\quad
 m_6=M(15b^3+30b^2+16b),\qquad m_1=m_3=m_5=0.
 \tag{NH3}
\]
The exact polynomials in the rational receiver are
\[
 N_j(z)=\sqrt h\,(z+\bar\omega-1)^j(\omega-z)^{3-j},
 \qquad 0\le j\le3.
 \tag{NH4}
\]
For the original physical target center $c_*$ put
$\kappa=c_*-1/2$. Define the actual numerator map
\[
 \mathcal M_\omega:\C^4\longrightarrow\mathcal P_3,
 \qquad \mathcal M_\omega c(S')=\sum_{j=0}^3c_jN_j(S'-\kappa).
 \tag{NH5}
\]
The target has its unchanged norm
$\|p\|_\Gamma^2=\int|p(c_*+iy)|^2dm_s(y)$.
In NH5, the domain is the displayed coefficient space with its
ordinary Euclidean norm when singular values are computed below.
This declaration identifies the operator whose singular values are
being evaluated; it changes no original target norm.

At $S'=c_*+iy$, the polynomial in NH4 is
$\sqrt h(d+i(y-\tau))^j(d-i(y-\tau))^{3-j}$.
Let $Q(\tau)$ be its coefficient matrix in $(1,y,y^2,y^3)$ after
factoring out the displayed constant $\sqrt h$ from the formula.
Its complete entries, still retaining all phases, are
\[
\begin{pmatrix}
d^3+3id^2\tau-3d\tau^2-i\tau^3&d^3+id^2\tau+d\tau^2+i\tau^3&
d^3-id^2\tau+d\tau^2-i\tau^3&d^3-3id^2\tau-3d\tau^2+i\tau^3\\
-3id^2+6d\tau+3i\tau^2&-id^2-2d\tau-3i\tau^2&
id^2-2d\tau+3i\tau^2&3id^2+6d\tau-3i\tau^2\\
-3d-3i\tau&d+3i\tau&d-3i\tau&-3d+3i\tau\\
i&-i&i&-i
\end{pmatrix}.
\tag{NH6}
\]
The Gram of the displayed $y$-monomials is exactly
\[
 K=M\begin{pmatrix}
 1&0&b&0\\0&b&0&3b^2+2b\\
 b&0&3b^2+2b&0\\0&3b^2+2b&0&15b^3+30b^2+16b
 \end{pmatrix}.
 \tag{NH7}
\]
It is positive definite: every nonzero polynomial has positive
integral of its squared modulus against the strictly positive
density NH2. Direct computation also gives the positive leading
principal determinants
\[
 M,\quad M^2b,\quad2M^3b^2(b+1),\quad
 12M^4b^3(b+1)^2(b+2).
 \tag{NH8}
\]
Consequently the original coefficient Gram is the exact matrix
\[
 H_\omega=hQ(\tau)^*KQ(\tau),\qquad
 (H_\omega)_{ij}=h\int(d+i(y-\tau))^{3+j-i}
                      (d-i(y-\tau))^{3-j+i}dm_s(y).
 \tag{NH9}
\]
The second identity follows by conjugating the $i$th polynomial
before multiplying by the $j$th. It proves the native Toeplitz
formula and keeps every shifted odd moment as well as the even ones.

The determinant of NH6 is $-64d^6$, for every $\tau$.
One proof is the translation factorization in NH11 below and
direct expansion at $\tau=0$. Hence
\[
 \det H_\omega
 =h^4(64d^6)^2\det K
 =12h^{16}M^4b^3(b+1)^2(b+2)>0.
 \tag{NH10}
\]
The coefficient map $\sqrt h Q$ in the $y$-basis has determinant
$-h^8$. The receiver matrix $R_\omega^{(3)}$ in the original
$z$-monomial basis has determinant $+h^8$: substitution
$z=1/2+iy$ has determinant $i^{0+1+2+3}=-1$. Thus NH10
agrees with RW11 with its original sign conventions, not only
with its determinant modulus.

\section{All compound leading matrices}
Use row and column indices $0,1,2,3$, preserving the ordered
coefficient basis. Define the full translation matrix
\[
 T(\tau)_{k\ell}=\begin{cases}
 \binom\ell k(-\tau)^{\ell-k},&k\le\ell,\\0,&k>\ell.
 \end{cases}
 \qquad Q(\tau)=T(\tau)Q(0).
 \tag{NH11}
\]
Indeed substituting $y-\tau$ into each degree-$\ell$ monomial
gives exactly the indicated coefficient column. This identity
proves every entry and keeps the actual shift rather than discarding
it in the measure.

For increasing $r$-subsets $I,J\subset\{0,1,2,3\}$, the
$(I,J)$ entry of $\wedge^rT$ is
\[
 (-\tau)^{\sum J-\sum I}
       \det\bigl(\tbinom j i\bigr)_{i\in I,j\in J},
 \tag{NH12}
\]
whenever the coefficient determinant is nonzero. This follows
by factoring the powers out of each row and column of the
minor. Its maximal possible degree is
$m_r=r(4-r)$, attained only at
$I_r=\{0,\ldots,r-1\}$ and $J_r=\{4-r,\ldots,3\}$.
The binomial minors at those positions are one: in degrees
one and four this is immediate, in degree two the matrix is
$\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&1\\2&3\end{smallmatrix}\right)$,
and in degree three it is
$\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&1&1\\1&2&3\\0&1&3\end{smallmatrix}\right)$.
Each determinant is one. All other nonzero minors have strictly
smaller degree, so there is no omitted term of a higher order.

Here are convenient unchanged input vectors:
\[
 v_1=(1,-1,1,-1)^T,\quad v_2=(3,-1,-1,3)^T,\quad
 v_3=(1,1,-1,-1)^T,\quad v_4=(1,3,3,1)^T.
 \tag{NH13}
\]
They have Gram $\diag(4,20,4,20)$ in coefficient Euclidean
coordinates. Multiplying the unique leading row of each compound
in NH12 by $\wedge^rQ(0)$ gives the following full limits:
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c}
 r&m_r&\lim_{\tau\to\pm\infty}\tau^{-m_r}\wedge^rQ(\tau)\\\hline
 1&3&-i\,e_0v_1^{\mathsf T}\\
 2&4&id\,e_{01}(v_1\wedge v_2)^{\mathsf T}\\
 3&3&-2d^3\,e_{012}(v_1\wedge v_2\wedge v_3)^{\mathsf T}\\
 4&0&-64d^6
\end{array}.
\tag{NH14}
\]
For direct coefficient verification, the three nonzero row
vectors are respectively
\[
 (-i,i,-i,i),\qquad
 (2id,-4id,6id,2id,-4id,2id),\qquad
 (-8d^3,24d^3,-24d^3,8d^3).
\]
The compound subsets use increasing lexicographic order. These
rows follow by minors of $Q(0)$; all other rows vanish in the
limit by NH12. The limit with $\tau^{-m_r}$ retains the sign
for negative $\tau$; its squared Gram limits use $|\tau|^{2m_r}$
and therefore coincide on both ends.

\section{Full eigenvalue and native singular-value asymptotics}
Taking exterior powers of the exact Gram NH9 gives
\[
 \wedge^rH_\omega
 =h^r(\wedge^rQ)^*(\wedge^rK)(\wedge^rQ).
 \tag{NH15}
\]
The squared $K$-lengths of $e_0,e_{01},e_{012}$ are the first
three determinants NH8. Thus the full leading matrices in
NH15 are rank one for $r=1,2,3$. If $w_2=v_1\wedge v_2$
and $w_3=v_1\wedge v_2\wedge v_3$, they are
\[
\begin{aligned}
 |\tau|^{-6}H_\omega&\longrightarrow hM v_1v_1^{\mathsf T},\\
 |\tau|^{-8}\wedge^2H_\omega&\longrightarrow
              h^2d^2M^2b\,w_2w_2^{\mathsf T},\\
 |\tau|^{-6}\wedge^3H_\omega&\longrightarrow
              8h^3d^6M^3b^2(b+1)\,w_3w_3^{\mathsf T}.
\end{aligned}\tag{NH16}
\]
Their nonzero eigenvalues are their traces. Orthogonality in
NH13 gives $\|v_1\|^2=4$, $\|w_2\|^2=80$ and
$\|w_3\|^2=320$. Substituting the retained identity $d=h/2$
therefore yields all four largest compound-eigenvalue constants
\[
 C_1=4hM,\qquad C_2=20h^4M^2b,\qquad
 C_3=40h^9M^3b^2(b+1),\qquad
 C_4=12h^{16}M^4b^3(b+1)^2(b+2).
 \tag{NH17}
\]
Here $C_4$ is the exact determinant NH10. Every constant is
strictly positive, with the entire original mass $M$ retained.

Let $\lambda_1\ge\lambda_2\ge\lambda_3\ge\lambda_4>0$
be the eigenvalues of the displayed coefficient matrix $H_\omega$
with respect to coefficient Euclidean coordinates. A positive
matrix has an orthonormal eigenbasis, whose coordinate wedges
diagonalize its compounds with all products of eigenvalues.
Consequently $\|\wedge^rH_\omega\|=\lambda_1\cdots\lambda_r$.
Continuity of the norm in NH16 and successive ratios of the
constants NH17 prove the complete spectrum
\[
\boxed{\begin{aligned}
 \lambda_1&\sim4hM|\tau|^6,\\
 \lambda_2&\sim5h^3Mb|\tau|^2,\\
 \lambda_3&\sim2h^5Mb(b+1)|\tau|^{-2},\\
 \lambda_4&\sim\tfrac3{10}h^7Mb(b+1)(b+2)|\tau|^{-6}.
\end{aligned}}\tag{NH18}
\]
The product of the four constants is precisely NH10. This
also gives the exact coefficient condition-number asymptote
\[
 \frac{\lambda_1}{\lambda_4}
 \sim\frac{40}{3h^6b(b+1)(b+2)}|\tau|^{12}.
 \tag{NH19}
\]

Because the Gram of the typed numerator operator NH5 is
$H_\omega$, its singular values are $\sqrt{\lambda_j}$.
Thus they are, in decreasing order,
\[
\boxed{\begin{aligned}
 s_1(\mathcal M_\omega)&\sim2\sqrt{hM}|\tau|^3,\\
 s_2(\mathcal M_\omega)&\sim\sqrt{5h^3Mb}|\tau|,\\
 s_3(\mathcal M_\omega)&\sim\sqrt{2h^5Mb(b+1)}|\tau|^{-1},\\
 s_4(\mathcal M_\omega)&\sim\sqrt{\tfrac3{10}h^7Mb(b+1)(b+2)}|\tau|^{-3}.
\end{aligned}}\tag{NH20}
\]
Its inverse uses the original native space as domain and the
coefficient Euclidean space as codomain, and has the reciprocal
singular values in reverse order. All inverse exterior norms
are determined with constants by
\[
 \|\wedge^r\mathcal M_\omega^{-1}\|
 \sim\sqrt{\frac{C_{4-r}}{C_4}}\,
            |\tau|^{m_{4-r}},\qquad 0\le r\le4,
 \quad C_0=1, m_0=m_4=0.
 \tag{NH21}
\]
This follows either by complementary products of the four
singular values or by the exact identity
$\|\wedge^r\mathcal M_\omega^{-1}\|
=\|\wedge^{4-r}\mathcal M_\omega\|/\sqrt{\det H_\omega}$.

\section{Original input and output singular flags}
The limiting coefficient eigenlines of $H_\omega$ in descending
eigenvalue order are exactly
\[
 \C(v_1/2),\qquad\C(v_2/\sqrt{20}),\qquad
 \C(v_3/2),\qquad\C(v_4/\sqrt{20}).
 \tag{NH22}
\]
There is no assertion about individual phases of eigenvectors.
To prove the statement, the rank-one limits of NH16 show that
the leading one-, two-, and three-dimensional coefficient
eigenflags converge to
$\operatorname{span}(v_1)$, $\operatorname{span}(v_1,v_2)$ and
$\operatorname{span}(v_1,v_2,v_3)$. More explicitly the normalized compound
matrices converge to a rank-one orthogonal projection onto
$v_1$, $w_2$ or $w_3$. Their top eigenspace projections converge:
any unit eigenvector subsequential limit solves the limiting
eigenvector equation and lies in its unique one-dimensional
range. A nonzero decomposable wedge determines its plane.
The eigenvalue ratios in NH18 tend to infinity, so these are
the unique leading flags for sufficiently large $|\tau|$.
Taking orthogonal successive differences yields NH22, using
the exact orthogonality in NH13.

The native output singular flags of NH5 converge to the full
polynomial degree filtration $\mathcal P_0\subset\mathcal P_1
\subset\mathcal P_2\subset\mathcal P_3$ in its original
Gamma metric. Indeed each rank-one forward compound limit
NH14 has output range $e_0$, $e_{01}$ or $e_{012}$; the
same positive-matrix argument applied using the original
$K$ metric proves these output flags. The limiting individual
native output lines have the following original unit
representatives on $S'=c_*+iy$:
\[
 \frac1{\sqrt M},\qquad
 \frac{y}{\sqrt{Mb}},\qquad
 \frac{y^2-b}{\sqrt{2Mb(b+1)}},\qquad
 \frac{y^3-(3b+2)y}{\sqrt{6Mb(b+1)(b+2)}}.
 \tag{NH23}
\]
As polynomials in the original physical coordinate, each $y$
in NH23 means $(S'-c_*)/i$; the phases and center are thereby
specified. Orthogonality and the displayed squared norms
follow by substituting the exact moments NH3, so these
expressions are the original metric's unit representatives,
not a replacement metric. They retain its total mass $M$.

\section{Exact meaning for the original conductor receiver}
Retain the original map $\Psi:\C^4\to\mathcal P_3$,
its Gram $G=\Psi^*\langle\ ,\ \rangle_\Gamma\Psi$, and
the original receiver coefficient map
$B_\omega=R_\omega^{-1}C_\kappa\Psi$ from RW6. The exact
identity NH5 gives
\[
 \mathcal M_\omega B_\omega=\Psi,\qquad
 B_\omega^*H_\omega B_\omega=G.
 \tag{NH24}
\]
Thus $B_\omega$ from the original metric $G$ to the original
coefficient metric $H_\omega$ is an exact isometry. Its
singular values with those two specified native norms are
all one. The height-dependent eigenvalues NH18 compare the
actual coefficient Gram to the Euclidean coordinate metric;
the numerator singular values NH20 refer to the explicitly
typed map NH5. These identities specify their mathematical
meaning without dropping the original norm or inferring a
degeneration of the fixed linear conductor.

Finally let $L=T_A|_U$ be the original invertible four-plane
conductor from its original quotient space to the target.
The complete original source numerator receiver is
$\mathcal M_{U,\omega}=L^{-1}\mathcal M_\omega$. For every
$0\le r\le4$, composition gives
\[
 \frac{\|\wedge^r\mathcal M_\omega\|}{\|\wedge^rL\|}
 \le\|\wedge^r\mathcal M_{U,\omega}\|
 \le\|\wedge^rL^{-1}\|\|\wedge^r\mathcal M_\omega\|.
 \tag{NH25}
\]
The left inequality is the right one applied to
$\mathcal M_\omega=L\mathcal M_{U,\omega}$.
The original WCF inverse-exterior bound applies to the fixed
factor $\|\wedge^rL^{-1}\|$ here; all target numerator
constants have already been evaluated in NH17--21. No
substitution of the separate full-Weil form for this original
positive Gram is made.

The independent exact certificate
\texttt{derive\_rational\_native\_height.py} constructs NH6
directly from NH4, verifies the full factorization NH11,
computes every compound and its leading coefficient,
checks rank one and every squared native compound constant,
and verifies the Gram of the four polynomials NH23 using
NH3. Its complete machine-readable output is
\texttt{rational\_native\_height\_exact.json}.

\section{Every original source Gram entry and its evaluated spectrum}
The source side of NH25 can be evaluated completely. Retain
the actual conductor order $v$, its original moments $\mu_n$,
$\mu_v\ne0$, its centers $c_0=c_*+4$, $c_1=c_*$, and the
exact function $g$ of WCF5. No conductor coefficient varies
with $\tau$. To give the inverse coefficients explicitly, put
\[
 \nu_{v+j}=\sum_{r=0}^j\binom{v+j}{v+r}(-4)^{j-r}\mu_{v+r},
 \qquad 0\le j\le3.
 \tag{NH26}
\]
These are precisely the original centered moments
$\sum_{u,w}a_{uw}(\beta_{8,u,w}-4)^{v+j}$; the omitted
lower terms vanish by the actual order $v$. Expanding the
unchanged $w(t)=-i\arctan t$ gives every needed coefficient:
\[
\begin{aligned}
 g_0&=\frac{(-i)^v\mu_v}{v!},&
 g_1&=\frac{(-i)^{v+1}\nu_{v+1}}{(v+1)!},\\
 g_2&=\frac{(-i)^{v+2}\nu_{v+2}}{(v+2)!}
                           -\frac v3g_0,&
 g_3&=\frac{(-i)^{v+3}\nu_{v+3}}{(v+3)!}
                           -\frac{v+1}{3}g_1.
\end{aligned}\tag{NH27}
\]
Indeed $g(t)=t^{-v}\sum_{n\ge v}\nu_nw(t)^n/n!$,
and $w(t)^n=(-i)^nt^n(1-nt^2/3+O(t^4))$; only the
four displayed moments contribute through degree three.
Thus the four exact FC24 inverse coefficients are
\[
 \xi_0=g_0^{-1},\quad \xi_1=-g_1g_0^{-2},\quad
 \xi_2=g_1^2g_0^{-3}-g_2g_0^{-2},\quad
 \xi_3=-g_1^3g_0^{-4}+2g_1g_2g_0^{-3}-g_3g_0^{-2}.
 \tag{NH28}
\]
Multiplication by $g_0+g_1t+g_2t^2+g_3t^3$ gives one
through degree three, proving these are the original inverse
coefficients. In particular
$\xi_0=i^vv!/\mu_v$ and
$|\xi_0|^2=(v!)^2/|\mu_v|^2$; its original phase is retained.

Let $\phi_n(S)=p_{n,s}((S-c_0)/i)/\sqrt{Mn!(b)_n}$ be
the original orthonormal polynomials at the original source
center, where
\[
 p_{0,s}(y)=1,\quad p_{1,s}(y)=y,\qquad
 p_{n+1,s}(y)=yp_{n,s}(y)-n(n-1+b)p_{n-1,s}(y).
 \tag{NH29}
\]
Their classes $[\phi_v],\ldots,[\phi_{v+3}]$ form the
original quotient orthonormal frame. Each representative is
orthogonal to the full removed kernel $\mathcal P_{v-1}$;
hence every combination in this frame is its class's unique
minimum-norm representative. This follows directly from
Pythagoras upon adding any vector of $\mathcal P_{v-1}$.
No nonminimal monomial representative is used below.

Put $\eta_j(S')=((S'-c_*)/i)^j$ and let
$u_j=L^{-1}\eta_j$ in this original quotient, $0\le j\le3$.
Define the positive original weights
\[
 k_j=\rho_{v+j}^{-2}=\frac{(b)_{v+j}}{(v+j)!},
 \qquad 0\le j\le3.
 \tag{NH30}
\]
The complete coefficient matrix of the minimum representatives
$u_0,\ldots,u_3$ in the frame NH29 is
\[
 U=\sqrt M\diag(\sqrt{k_0},\sqrt{k_1},\sqrt{k_2},\sqrt{k_3})F,
 \qquad
 F=\begin{pmatrix}
 \xi_0&\xi_1&b\xi_0+2\xi_2&(3b+2)\xi_1+6\xi_3\\
 0&\xi_0&2\xi_1&(3b+2)\xi_0+6\xi_2\\
 0&0&2\xi_0&6\xi_1\\
 0&0&0&6\xi_0
 \end{pmatrix}.
 \tag{NH31}
\]
This is an evaluated matrix of actual inverse polynomials:
$u_j=\sum_{r=0}^jU_{rj}[\phi_{v+r}]$ with $\phi$ given
by NH29 and every $\xi$ given by NH26--28.

To prove NH31 directly from FC24, the original target
orthonormal frame expands the four monomials by
\[
 \begin{pmatrix}
 \sqrt M&0&b\sqrt M&0\\
 0&\sqrt{Mb}&0&(3b+2)\sqrt{Mb}\\
 0&0&\sqrt{2Mb(b+1)}&0\\
 0&0&0&\sqrt{6Mb(b+1)(b+2)}
 \end{pmatrix}.
 \tag{NH32}
\]
These are exactly $1$, $y$, $y^2=p_2+b$ and
$y^3=p_3+(3b+2)y$, with their original squared norms.
FC24 has entry $\xi_{j-r}\rho_j/\rho_{v+r}$ for $r\le j$.
The identity
$\rho_j\sqrt{Mj!(b)_j}=\sqrt M\,j!$
therefore proves NH31 by multiplication, retaining all phases
in the inverse coefficients and every factorial.

For fully explicit entries put
$A_2=b\xi_0+2\xi_2$, $A_3=(3b+2)\xi_1+6\xi_3$,
$B_3=(3b+2)\xi_0+6\xi_2$. The original source Gram
$K_U=(\langle u_j,u_\ell\rangle)$ has these ten entries;
the remaining six are their Hermitian conjugates:
\[
\begin{aligned}
 (K_U)_{00}/M&=k_0|\xi_0|^2,&
 (K_U)_{01}/M&=k_0\bar\xi_0\xi_1,\\
 (K_U)_{02}/M&=k_0\bar\xi_0A_2,&
 (K_U)_{03}/M&=k_0\bar\xi_0A_3,\\
 (K_U)_{11}/M&=k_0|\xi_1|^2+k_1|\xi_0|^2,\\
 (K_U)_{12}/M&=k_0\bar\xi_1A_2+2k_1\bar\xi_0\xi_1,\\
 (K_U)_{13}/M&=k_0\bar\xi_1A_3+k_1\bar\xi_0B_3,\\
 (K_U)_{22}/M&=k_0|A_2|^2+4k_1|\xi_1|^2+4k_2|\xi_0|^2,\\
 (K_U)_{23}/M&=k_0\bar A_2A_3+2k_1\bar\xi_1B_3
                                       +12k_2\bar\xi_0\xi_1,\\
 (K_U)_{33}/M&=k_0|A_3|^2+k_1|B_3|^2
                                +36k_2|\xi_1|^2+36k_3|\xi_0|^2.
\end{aligned}\tag{NH33}
\]
The proof is the exact product $K_U=U^*U$
$=MF^*\diag(k_j)F$ in the original quotient orthonormal
frame. It evaluates every source Gram entry in finite original
conductor moments and the original Gamma weights, rather
than leaving an unknown positive metric.

All four leading principal minors are explicitly
\[
\begin{aligned}
 D_{U,1}&=Mk_0|\xi_0|^2,\\
 D_{U,2}&=M^2k_0k_1|\xi_0|^4,\\
 D_{U,3}&=4M^3k_0k_1k_2|\xi_0|^6,\\
 D_{U,4}&=144M^4k_0k_1k_2k_3|\xi_0|^8.
\end{aligned}\tag{NH34}
\]
For the first $r$ columns of NH31, every row below the
$r$th is zero. Their Gram determinant is the squared
modulus of their upper triangular $r$-by-$r$ determinant.
The diagonal of $F$ is $(\xi_0,\xi_0,2\xi_0,6\xi_0)$,
which proves each factor in NH34. Positivity is strict
because $M,k_j>0$ and $\xi_0\ne0$. The coefficients
$\xi_1,\xi_2,\xi_3$ remain in NH33; their cancellation
from these particular determinants follows from the proved
triangular matrix, not from setting them to zero.

In the source orthonormal frame the exact numerator map is
\[
 \mathcal M_{U,\omega}=L^{-1}\mathcal M_\omega,
 \qquad [\mathcal M_{U,\omega}]=\sqrt h\,UQ(\tau),
 \quad H_{U,\omega}=hQ(\tau)^*K_UQ(\tau).
 \tag{NH35}
\]
To record all leading phases as well, let $f_0,\ldots,f_3$
be the fixed original source frame $[\phi_v],\ldots,
[\phi_{v+3}]$. Combining NH14 with NH31 proves
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c}
 r&m_r&\lim\tau^{-m_r}\wedge^r\mathcal M_{U,\omega}\\\hline
 1&3&-i\sqrt{hMk_0}\,\xi_0 f_0v_1^{\mathsf T}\\
 2&4&ihdM\sqrt{k_0k_1}\,\xi_0^2
                           f_{01}(v_1\wedge v_2)^{\mathsf T}\\
 3&3&-4h^{3/2}d^3M^{3/2}\sqrt{k_0k_1k_2}\,\xi_0^3
                           f_{012}(v_1\wedge v_2\wedge v_3)^{\mathsf T}\\
 4&0&-12h^8M^2\sqrt{k_0k_1k_2k_3}\,\xi_0^4.
\end{array}\tag{NH36}
\]
Each is a full matrix limit in the unchanged original
source metric, because the displayed $f$ frame is the
actual quotient orthonormal frame. The last value is the
exact determinant for every $\tau$.

Taking squared norms of NH36 gives the four exact leading
compound constants of the source Gram:
\[
\begin{aligned}
 C_{U,1}&=4hMk_0|\xi_0|^2,\\
 C_{U,2}&=20h^4M^2k_0k_1|\xi_0|^4,\\
 C_{U,3}&=80h^9M^3k_0k_1k_2|\xi_0|^6,\\
 C_{U,4}&=144h^{16}M^4k_0k_1k_2k_3|\xi_0|^8.
\end{aligned}\tag{NH37}
\]
Their exponents are $6,8,6,0$. Successive ratios, by the
same proved singular-product identity as NH18, now give
every source Gram eigenvalue in decreasing order:
\[
\boxed{\begin{aligned}
 \lambda_{U,1}&\sim4hMk_0\frac{(v!)^2}{|\mu_v|^2}|\tau|^6,\\
 \lambda_{U,2}&\sim5h^3Mk_1\frac{(v!)^2}{|\mu_v|^2}|\tau|^2,\\
 \lambda_{U,3}&\sim4h^5Mk_2\frac{(v!)^2}{|\mu_v|^2}|\tau|^{-2},\\
 \lambda_{U,4}&\sim\tfrac95h^7Mk_3\frac{(v!)^2}{|\mu_v|^2}|\tau|^{-6}.
\end{aligned}}\tag{NH38}
\]
The source receiver singular values are the positive square
roots of these expressions, because its domain is again
the displayed Euclidean coefficient space and its codomain
has the original quotient norm. Its inverse exterior norms
are fully evaluated by
\[
 \|\wedge^r\mathcal M_{U,\omega}^{-1}\|
 \sim\sqrt{C_{U,4-r}/C_{U,4}}\,
                         |\tau|^{m_{4-r}},
 \quad C_{U,0}=1,\quad 0\le r\le4.
 \tag{NH39}
\]
The source Gram condition number is consequently
\[
 \frac{\lambda_{U,1}}{\lambda_{U,4}}
 \sim\frac{20}{9h^6}
 \frac{(v+1)(v+2)(v+3)}{(b+v)(b+v+1)(b+v+2)}|\tau|^{12}.
 \tag{NH40}
\]
This follows from NH30, with the complete Gamma quotient
weight ratio retained.

The input eigenlines are still the four lines NH22: the
input covectors in NH36 are unchanged and the positive
source constants only multiply their rank-one Gram
limits. The native output singular flags are now exactly
the limiting quotient-degree flags
\[
 \operatorname{span}([\phi_v])\ \subset\
 \operatorname{span}([\phi_v],[\phi_{v+1}])\ \subset\
 \operatorname{span}([\phi_v],[\phi_{v+1}],[\phi_{v+2}])
 \ \subset\ U.
 \tag{NH41}
\]
The individual limiting native output lines are
$\C[\phi_{v+j}]$, $j=0,1,2,3$, in that order. The proof
is the same rank-one compound projection argument used
for NH22--23, now applied to the actual orthonormal
quotient frame in NH36. Formula NH29 specifies every
polynomial, its original center, phase and squared norm.

Finally the source determinant gives a direct exact
comparison with the original conductor:
\[
 \frac{\det H_{U,\omega}}{\det H_\omega}
 =\frac{144M^4\prod_{j=0}^3 k_j|\xi_0|^8}
        {12M^4b^3(b+1)^2(b+2)}
 =|\det A_{3,s}^{-1}|^2.
 \tag{NH42}
\]
To verify the last equality from FC24,
$\det A_{3,s}^{-1}=\xi_0^4\prod_{j=0}^3\rho_j/\rho_{v+j}$
and $\prod_{j=0}^3\rho_j^2=12/[b^3(b+1)^2(b+2)]$.
Both sides of NH42 are independent of the test-pole height,
while the full source and target spectra above retain
their explicitly calculated height dependence. This
completes the source calculation in NH25 rather than
leaving it as an enclosure with unspecified constants.
% END RATIONAL_NATIVE_HEIGHT

% BEGIN ARITHMETIC_RESIDUES
\section{Arithmetic residues and complete confluent jets}

The original marked polynomial is credited to The Clankers \cite{ES488}. This continuation independently checks equations (1)--(17) of the incoming
calculation \cite{ResidueIntake}. It retains the original quartic
coefficient $-1/2$, both factor signs, the projective root at
infinity and the original polynomial coordinates. It corrects
the incoming unnumbered zeta determinant following (16).
No numerical zero calculation is used.

\section{The complete original factors and arithmetic residue map}
In original coordinates $q=(a,y,z,w)$ the polynomial is
\begin{align}
b&=i+ay,&c&=-i+2ay+a^2z,\nonumber\\
d&=-iy-a(iz+2y^2)-a^2yz,&e&=2z-7iy^2+aw,\nonumber\\
f&=iw+3iy^3-4yz+a(6iy^2z+wy+4y^4)+2a^2y^3z,\nonumber\\
P(q)&=(ac,ae+bd,af+be,bf). \tag{AR1}
\end{align}
Substitution proves $ad+bc=1$ and $C(-b,a)=1$, where
$L=aU+bV$ and $C=cU^3+dU^2V+eUV^2+fV^3$.
The full factor space and its exact marked-root morphism are
\begin{align}
{\cal E}&=\{ad+bc=1,\ a^3f-a^2be+ab^2d-b^3c=1\},\nonumber\\
{\cal P}&=(ac,ae+bd,af+be,bf),\qquad
{\cal E}\longrightarrow\{(u,[U:V]):H_u(U,V)=0\},\nonumber\\
(a,b,c,d,e,f)&\longmapsto({\cal P},[-b:a]),\qquad
H_u=AU^4+U^3V+BU^2V^2+C_1UV^3+DV^4.
\tag{AR2}
\end{align}
The first equation ensures $(a,b)\ne(0,0)$. Simultaneously
negating all six factors leaves the target and marked root fixed.
For a finite simple root $r$ of
$h_u(s)=As^4+s^3+Bs^2+C_1s+D$, division by $a(U-rV)$ gives
\[
b=-ar,\quad c=A/a,\quad d=(1+Ar)/a,\quad
e=(B+r+Ar^2)/a,\quad f=(C_1+Br+r^2+Ar^3)/a.
\]
The resultant becomes $a^2h'_u(r)=1$. This proves that both
square roots give exactly the two full factor states over a
simple finite root, while a multiple root gives none.
For the original completed function \cite{DLMFXi},
\begin{equation}
r=-y-i/a,\quad
d(\xi\circ r)=\xi'(r)(ia^{-2}da-dy),\qquad
\operatorname{Res}_r\frac{\xi(s)\,ds}{h_u(s)}
=\frac{\xi(r)}{h'_u(r)}=a^2\xi(r).
\tag{AR3}
\end{equation}
Thus each pair of factor signs gives one arithmetic value
and one residue, with no sign-dependent zero test.

For the other projective chart, put $t=V/U$, $g_u(t)=H_u(1,t)$
and $t_0=-a/b$. Division by $b(V-t_0U)$ gives
\begin{equation}
-b^2g'_u(t_0)=1,\qquad
\frac{\xi(s)\,ds}{h_u(s)}
=-\frac{t^2\xi(1/t)}{g_u(t)}\,dt.
\tag{AR4}
\end{equation}
Indeed $C(-b,a)=(-b)^3C(1,t_0)=-b^2g'_u(t_0)$.
For $t_0\ne0$ the residue is
$b^2t_0^2\xi(1/t_0)=a^2\xi(r)$, proving exact equality
of the chart formulas. At $A=0$ the root $t_0=0$ is simple
because $g'_u(0)=1$. Its two full factor states are
\begin{equation}
(a,b,c,d,e,f)=
(0,\sigma i,-\sigma i,-\sigma iB,-\sigma iC_1,-\sigma iD),
\qquad \sigma=\pm1.
\tag{AR5}
\end{equation}
Only $\sigma=1$ occurs in AR1, since $a=0$ forces $b=i$.
Its original coordinates are
$(0,B,i(7B^2-C_1)/2,11B^3-2BC_1-D)$.

\section{The exact finite remainder and infinity residue}
Let $n=\deg h_u$, equal to four or three, and let $\alpha_n$
be its actual leading coefficient, respectively $A$ or $1$.
For each entire function $F$ there is exactly one polynomial
$R_F$ of degree less than $n$ such that
\begin{equation}
F=R_F+h_uG_F,\quad G_F\text{ entire},\qquad
R_F^{(j)}(r)=F^{(j)}(r)\quad(0\le j<m_r),\qquad
{\cal A}_u=\C[s]/(h_u).
\tag{AR6}
\end{equation}
Here $m_r$ is the root multiplicity. For a complete existence
proof, the ideals $(s-r)^{m_r}$ are pairwise coprime; the
Euclidean algorithm gives their Bezout identities and the
polynomial Chinese remainder construction. Prescribing the
displayed Taylor data and reducing modulo $h_u$ gives a
representative of degree less than $n$. Its quotient in AR6
has only removable singularities. Uniqueness follows because
a polynomial of degree less than $n$ divisible by $h_u$ is
zero. Hence $j_uF=[R_F]$ is an algebra morphism.
In each local Taylor basis its multiplication matrix is
triangular with $F(r)$ on the diagonal $m_r$ times, so
\begin{equation}
\det M_{j_uF}=\prod_rF(r)^{m_r},\qquad
j_uF\text{ is a unit exactly when every }F(r)\ne0.
\tag{AR7}
\end{equation}
This includes nonreduced root schemes, without asserting
existence of a finite factor state at their multiple roots.

Write $h_u=(s-r)^{m_r}k_r(s)$, $k_r(r)\ne0$. The full
principal part and the infinity residue are
\begin{equation}
\left[\frac{F}{h_u}\right]_{\mathrm{pp},r}
=\sum_{j=1}^{m_r}\frac{(F/k_r)^{(m_r-j)}(r)}{(m_r-j)!}
 (s-r)^{-j},\qquad
\operatorname{Res}_\infty\frac{F\,ds}{h_u}
=-\frac{[s^{n-1}]R_F}{\alpha_n}.
\tag{AR8}
\end{equation}
The first formula is Taylor expansion. For the second,
$F/h_u=R_F/h_u+G_F$; an entire $G_F$ contributes no
$s^{-1}$ coefficient at infinity. The rational first term
has coefficient $[s^{n-1}]R_F/\alpha_n$, and $t=1/s$
gives the minus sign. Equivalently this residue is minus
the sum of all finite residues. It has the same value at
both infinity signs AR5.

There is no holomorphic point value $\xi(\infty)$.
Indeed $\xi(2m)=m(2m-1)\pi^{-m}(m-1)!\zeta(2m)$ and
$\zeta(2m)>1$ by its positive Dirichlet series. The factorial
grows faster than any fixed power, so $\xi$ is not a polynomial.
An entire function meromorphic at infinity would have a
polynomial growth bound, and Cauchy's coefficient formula would
make it a polynomial. Thus $\xi(1/t)$ is essential at $0$.
AR8 supplies a differential residue, not an invented value
or local zero test at infinity.

At $h_0=s^3-s$, the completion and reflection give
$\xi(0)=\xi(1)=1/2$ and $\xi(-1)=\xi(2)=\pi/6$.
Evaluation at the three roots proves
\begin{align}
R_\xi&=\tfrac12+\frac{\pi-3}{12}(s^2-s),&
R_\xi^{-1}&=2+(3/\pi-1)(s^2-s)\pmod{s^3-s},\nonumber\\
\det M_{R_\xi}&=\pi/24,&
(\operatorname{Res}_0,\operatorname{Res}_1,\operatorname{Res}_{-1},\operatorname{Res}_\infty)\frac{\xi\,ds}{s^3-s}
&=(-\tfrac12,\tfrac14,\tfrac{\pi}{12},-\tfrac{\pi-3}{12}).
\tag{AR9}
\end{align}
Each value occurs at both full factor signs; only one of the
infinity signs is in the original chart. All are nonzero.

\section{The literal quartet and every completion factor}
Fix $0<\delta<1/2$, $\gamma>0$, and retain
$r_{\epsilon,\eta}=1/2+\epsilon\delta+i\eta\gamma$,
with original order $(+,+),(+,-),(-,+),(-,-)$.
Put $\rho=r_{+,+}$, $x=s-1/2$, $k=\delta^2-\gamma^2$,
$l=2\delta\gamma$, $H_\delta=(x^2-k)^2+l^2$ and
$h_\rho=-H_\delta/2$. The last factor is forced by the
original cubic coefficient one. Write
$Z=\xi(\rho)=U+iV$, $\alpha=V/l$, $\beta=U-k\alpha$.
Reflection and conjugation give the four values
$(Z,\overline Z,\overline Z,Z)$, so
\begin{equation}
R_\xi=\alpha x^2+\beta=U+\alpha(x^2-k),\qquad
\det M_{R_\xi}=|Z|^4,\qquad
R_\xi^{-1}=\frac{U-\alpha(x^2-k)}{|Z|^2}\quad(Z\ne0).
\tag{AR10}
\end{equation}
Evaluation proves the remainder by AR6. The identity
$(x^2-k)^2=-l^2$ in the quotient proves the inverse.
For both original sign states at every root,
\begin{equation}
h'_\rho(r_{\epsilon,\eta})
=4\delta\gamma(\epsilon\gamma-i\eta\delta),\quad
a_{\epsilon,\eta,\pm}^2
=\frac{1}{4\delta\gamma(\epsilon\gamma-i\eta\delta)},\quad
\operatorname{Res}_r\frac{\xi\,ds}{h_\rho}=a^2\xi(r),\quad
\operatorname{Res}_r\frac{2\xi\,ds}{H_\delta}=-a^2\xi(r).
\tag{AR11}
\end{equation}
Multiplication of the three root differences and $-1/2$
proves the derivative, retaining the original factor and sign.

On the open critical strip the exact completion is
$\xi(s)=\Phi(s)\zeta(s)$ with
$\Phi(s)=s(s-1)\pi^{-s/2}\Gamma(s/2)/2$.
It is holomorphic and nonzero there: the polynomial factors
do not vanish, and Gamma has no poles or zeros in the right
half-plane. Thus its jet is a unit, including at the doubled
quartet below, and the complete multiplication morphism is
\begin{equation}
M_{j_u\xi}=M_{j_u\Phi}M_{j_u\zeta},\qquad
M_{j_u\zeta}=M_{j_u(1/\Phi)}M_{j_u\xi}.
\tag{AR12}
\end{equation}
This is an invertible map in the same coefficient algebra,
not a replacement of the original coordinates or norm.
It is not a global unit assertion: $\Phi(0)=-1$,
$\Phi(1)=0$, $\Phi'(1)=1/2$, and $\Phi$ has poles at the
negative even integers. The zeta pole and trivial zeros
compensate these factors in the entire completion.
AR12 therefore is not applied to the root $1$ of AR9 or to
the projective root at infinity.

The exact zeta determinant is
\begin{align}
\det M_{j_{H_\delta}\zeta}
&=|\zeta(\rho)\zeta(1-\rho)|^2
=\frac{|Z|^4}{|\Phi(\rho)\Phi(1-\rho)|^2}\nonumber\\
&=\left|\frac{\Phi(\rho)}{\Phi(1-\rho)}\right|^2
                         |\zeta(\rho)|^4,\nonumber\\
\frac{\Phi(\rho)}{\Phi(1-\rho)}
&=\pi^{1/2-\rho}\frac{\Gamma(\rho/2)}{\Gamma((1-\rho)/2)},\nonumber\\
\left|\frac{\Phi(\rho)}{\Phi(1-\rho)}\right|^2
&=\pi^{-2\delta}
\frac{|\Gamma(1/4+\delta/2+i\gamma/2)|^2}
     {|\Gamma(1/4-\delta/2+i\gamma/2)|^2}.
\tag{AR13}
\end{align}
Indeed the four values are
$\zeta(\rho),\overline{\zeta(\rho)},
\overline{\zeta(1-\rho)},\zeta(1-\rho)$.
Apply AR7 and then $\xi(\rho)=\xi(1-\rho)$, dividing only
by the nonzero completion factors, never by a possible zero
of zeta. The polynomial factors cancel since
$(1-s)(-s)=s(s-1)$, leaving the displayed Gamma and $\pi$
factors. The incoming $|\zeta(\rho)|^4$ formula omits this
generally nonunit-modulus ratio. It is not an identity:
as $\rho\to1$ near the real axis inside the strip, the ratio
tends to zero while $\xi(\rho)\to1/2$. Sufficiently nearby
nonreal points have nonzero zeta values and ratio modulus
different from one.

\section{Confluence in the unchanged coefficient coordinates}
Write $H_\delta=s^4+c_3s^3+c_2s^2+c_1s+c_0$, where
\[
c_3=-2,\quad c_2=\tfrac32+2(\gamma^2-\delta^2),\quad
c_1=-\tfrac12-2(\gamma^2-\delta^2),\quad
c_0=\tfrac1{16}+\tfrac{\gamma^2-\delta^2}{2}
                              +(\delta^2+\gamma^2)^2.
\]
In the original basis $(1,s,s^2,s^3)$, multiplication by
$s$ and by the completed remainder are exactly
\begin{equation}
C_\delta=\begin{pmatrix}
0&0&0&-c_0\\1&0&0&-c_1\\0&1&0&-c_2\\0&0&1&2
\end{pmatrix},\qquad
[M_{j\xi}]=\alpha(C_\delta-\tfrac12I)^2+\beta I .
\tag{AR14}
\end{equation}
The last column is the defining polynomial relation; the
others multiply the first three original monomials.
Let $r_\eta=1/2+i\eta\gamma$. Retain the exact paired
evaluation morphism
\[
{\cal V}_\delta p=
\left(\frac{p(r_++\delta)+p(r_+-\delta)}2,\
\frac{p(r_++\delta)-p(r_+-\delta)}{2\delta},\
\frac{p(r_-+\delta)+p(r_--\delta)}2,\
\frac{p(r_-+\delta)-p(r_--\delta)}{2\delta}\right)^{\mathsf T}.
\]
Its original-coordinate rows and determinant are
\begin{equation}
(1,r_\eta,r_\eta^2+\delta^2,r_\eta^3+3r_\eta\delta^2),\quad
(0,1,2r_\eta,3r_\eta^2+\delta^2),\qquad
\det{\cal V}_\delta=16\gamma^2(\gamma^2+\delta^2).
\tag{AR15}
\end{equation}
Each pair recovers both original evaluations by $u\pm\delta v$.
The complete inverse in original coefficient coordinates, for
$\delta>0$, is the polynomial
\[
{\cal V}_\delta^{-1}(u_+,v_+,u_-,v_-)(s)
=\sum_{\eta=\pm1}\sum_{\epsilon=\pm1}
(u_\eta+\epsilon\delta v_\eta)
\frac{H_\delta(s)}
 {(s-r_{\epsilon,\eta})H'_\delta(r_{\epsilon,\eta})}.
\]
Every quotient in this sum is a polynomial of degree three;
evaluation at each marked root proves both inverse compositions.
Its product law is $(u,v)(u',v')=(uu'+\delta^2vv',uv'+vu')$,
the algebra $\C[t]/(t^2-\delta^2)$. The Vandermonde and the
two explicit pair changes give the determinant; direct
expansion gives the same expression. It remains nonzero
at $\delta=0$. No root value or factor sign has been discarded.
At zero the map is
$ {\cal V}_0p=(p(r_+),p'(r_+),p(r_-),p'(r_-))$.
Put $d_\eta=r_\eta-r_{-\eta}$ and
\[
L_\eta(s)=(s-r_{-\eta})/d_\eta,\quad
A_\eta(s)=(1-2(s-r_\eta)/d_\eta)L_\eta(s)^2,\quad
B_\eta(s)=(s-r_\eta)L_\eta(s)^2.
\]
Direct evaluation and differentiation prove its full inverse
\begin{equation}
{\cal V}_0^{-1}(u_+,v_+,u_-,v_-)
=u_+A_++v_+B_++u_-A_-+v_-B_-.
\tag{AR16}
\end{equation}
For any holomorphic $F$, its local multiplication block is
$\begin{psmallmatrix}F(r_\eta)&0\\F'(r_\eta)&F(r_\eta)\end{psmallmatrix}$.
Conjugation by the explicit $ {\cal V}_0$ gives the matrix
back in the original coefficient basis.
Put $X(\gamma)=\xi(1/2+i\gamma)\in\mathbb R$.
Differentiation gives $\xi'(r_\eta)=-i\eta X'$;
Taylor expansion in real $\delta$ gives $U\to X$,
$V/\delta\to-X'$. Hence the full limiting data are
\begin{align}
H_0&=((s-\tfrac12)^2+\gamma^2)^2,\quad
T=(s-\tfrac12)^2+\gamma^2,\quad T^2=0\text{ in }{\cal A}_0,\nonumber\\
R_{\xi,0}&=X-\frac{X'}{2\gamma}T,\qquad
{\cal V}_0M_{j\xi}{\cal V}_0^{-1}
=\diag\left(\begin{pmatrix}X&0\\-iX'&X\end{pmatrix},
            \begin{pmatrix}X&0\\iX'&X\end{pmatrix}\right),\nonumber\\
\det M_{j\xi}&=X^4,\qquad
R_{\xi,0}^{-1}=X^{-1}+\frac{X'}{2\gamma X^2}T\quad(X\ne0).
\tag{AR17}
\end{align}
The same identities follow from AR16 using
$T(r_\eta)=0$, $T'(r_\eta)=2i\eta\gamma$.
If $X'\ne0$, there are two Jordan blocks of size two with
eigenvalue $X$. If $X'=0$, the operator is exactly $XI$.
At $X=0$, $X'\ne0$, its rank is two and its square is zero;
at $X=X'=0$, it is zero. These exhaustive alternatives follow
from the matrices, without any assumed zero multiplicity.
Thus $H_0$ divides $\xi$ exactly when $X=X'=0$.

\section{Completion jets and the converging residues}
Let $\phi_\eta=\Phi(r_\eta)\ne0$, $z_\eta=\zeta(r_\eta)$.
The complete original completion data are
\begin{align}
L_\Phi(s)&=\frac{\Phi'(s)}{\Phi(s)}
=\frac1s+\frac1{s-1}-\frac{\log\pi}{2}
                  +\frac12\frac{\Gamma'(s/2)}{\Gamma(s/2)},\nonumber\\
{\cal V}_0M_\Phi{\cal V}_0^{-1}\big|_\eta
&=\begin{pmatrix}\phi_\eta&0\\\phi_\eta L_\Phi(r_\eta)&\phi_\eta\end{pmatrix},
\quad
({\cal V}_0M_\Phi{\cal V}_0^{-1})^{-1}\big|_\eta
=\begin{pmatrix}\phi_\eta^{-1}&0\\
-L_\Phi(r_\eta)\phi_\eta^{-1}&\phi_\eta^{-1}\end{pmatrix},\nonumber\\
z_\eta&=X/\phi_\eta,\quad
z_\eta'=\frac{-i\eta X'-XL_\Phi(r_\eta)}{\phi_\eta},\qquad
\det M_{j_{H_0}\zeta}=|z_+|^4=\frac{X^4}{|\phi_+|^4}.
\tag{AX1}
\end{align}
Differentiate the original product defining $\Phi$ to prove its
logarithmic derivative. Multiplication of the triangular matrices
proves their inverse. Conjugation gives
$\phi_-=\overline{\phi_+}$ and the determinant. Thus the bare
$|\zeta(\rho)|^4$ formula holds at the doubled critical-line
limit, but needs AR13 at the distinct off-line quartet.
The exact unit map preserves the vanishing order even at $X=0$.

At $r_\eta$, the original polynomial $h_{\rho,0}=-H_0/2$
has complete arithmetic principal part
\begin{equation}
\left[\frac{\xi(s)}{h_{\rho,0}(s)}\right]_{\mathrm{pp},r_\eta}
=\frac{X}{2\gamma^2}(s-r_\eta)^{-2}
+\frac{i\eta(X-\gamma X')}{2\gamma^3}(s-r_\eta)^{-1}.
\tag{AX2}
\end{equation}
Expand $-2\xi(s)/(s-r_{-\eta})^2$ at $r_\eta$.
Its constant and linear terms are
$-2X/d_\eta^2$ and
$-2\xi'(r_\eta)/d_\eta^2+4X/d_\eta^3$.
Substitution $d_\eta=2i\eta\gamma$ proves every sign in AX2.

For $\delta>0$ denote the two finite-root residues at fixed
$\eta$ by $ {\cal R}_{\epsilon,\eta}$; each still occurs twice
among the signed states. AR11 and Taylor expansion give
\begin{align}
{\cal R}_{\epsilon,\eta}(\delta)
&=\frac{\epsilon X}{4\delta\gamma^2}
  +\frac{i\eta(X-\gamma X')}{4\gamma^3}+O(\delta),\nonumber\\
\sum_{\epsilon=\pm1}{\cal R}_{\epsilon,\eta}(\delta)
&\longrightarrow\frac{i\eta(X-\gamma X')}{2\gamma^3},&
\sum_{\epsilon=\pm1}\epsilon\delta\,{\cal R}_{\epsilon,\eta}(\delta)
&\longrightarrow\frac{X}{2\gamma^2}.
\tag{AX3}
\end{align}
The remainder is a convergent local Taylor remainder for fixed
$\gamma>0$. The exact rational identity
\[
\sum_{\epsilon=\pm1}\frac{{\cal R}_{\epsilon,\eta}}
 {s-r_\eta-\epsilon\delta}
=\frac{(s-r_\eta)({\cal R}_{+,\eta}+{\cal R}_{-,\eta})
       +\delta({\cal R}_{+,\eta}-{\cal R}_{-,\eta})}
       {(s-r_\eta)^2-\delta^2}
\]
then gives precisely AX2, locally uniformly away from $r_\eta$.
At $X=0$, $X'\ne0$, the individual residues tend to
$-i\eta X'/(4\gamma^2)$ while their factors $a$ diverge.
No finite full factor state exists over the doubled roots:
$a^2h'(r)=1$ excludes every finite root and $A=-1/2$ excludes
an infinity root. AR6, AR16 and AX2 nevertheless give the exact
jet and residue limit of these escaping factors.

\section{The whole explicit escape family: an exact arithmetic exclusion}
For every real $\varepsilon>0$, the original source
$(\varepsilon,0,i/2,0)$ has the exact target polynomial
\begin{equation}
h_\varepsilon(s)=i\varepsilon s(s+i/\varepsilon)
 [(\varepsilon^2/2-1)s^2-i\varepsilon s/2+1],\qquad
r_\pm=\frac{i\varepsilon\pm\sqrt{16-9\varepsilon^2}}
                 {2(\varepsilon^2-2)}.
\tag{AX4}
\end{equation}
Multiplication gives target
$(-i\varepsilon+i\varepsilon^3/2,3i\varepsilon/2,-1,0)$.
The other two labels are $0,-i/\varepsilon$.
At $\varepsilon^2=2$ the last factor is linear, its root is
$-2i/\varepsilon$, and the fourth projective root is infinity.
At $\varepsilon^2=16/9$ the two quadratic roots coincide
and are purely imaginary; for larger squares they are also
purely imaginary. When $0<\varepsilon^2<16/9$, their real
parts are opposite. The positive part equals
$R=\sqrt{16-9\varepsilon^2}/[2(2-\varepsilon^2)]$.
Squaring positive denominators proves $0<R<1$ exactly when
$7/4<\varepsilon^2<16/9$. There
$|\Im r_\pm|=\varepsilon/[2(2-\varepsilon^2)]<3$.
This covers all parameters, including changes of degree
and multiplicity.

Here is the complete classical boundary argument used for
these labels. For $\sigma>1$, the absolutely convergent Euler
logarithm gives, when $t\ne0$,
\[
3\log|\zeta(\sigma)|+4\log|\zeta(\sigma+it)|
 +\log|\zeta(\sigma+2it)|
=\sum_{p,m\ge1}
\frac{3+4\cos(mt\log p)+\cos(2mt\log p)}{mp^{m\sigma}}\ge0,
\]
since the numerator is $2(1+\cos(mt\log p))^2$.
If $\zeta$ had a zero of order $m_0\ge1$ at $1+it$,
the product obtained by exponentiating would be
$O((\sigma-1)^{4m_0-3})\to0$, using the simple pole
at $1$ and boundedness at $1+2it$. This contradicts its
lower bound one. Thus zeta has no zeros on $\Re s=1$;
the Euler product gives nonvanishing for $\Re s>1$.
The original completion factors are nonzero there away
from $1$, where $\xi(1)=1/2$. Reflection gives the same
for $\xi$ on $\Re s\le0$. This is the classical
Hadamard--de la Vallée Poussin line-one argument, reproduced
here and not claimed as a new theorem.

The precise theta input is the original series and modular
transformation in \cite{DLMFTheta}. At $z=0,\tau=ix$ these give,
with $\psi(x)=\sum_{n\ge1}e^{-\pi n^2x}$,
$1+2\psi(x)=x^{-1/2}(1+2\psi(1/x))$.
For $\Re s>1$, termwise integration gives
$\int_0^\infty\psi(x)x^{s/2-1}dx
=\Gamma(s/2)\pi^{-s/2}\zeta(s)$.
Split the integral at one and substitute $x=1/y$ in its
lower part. The elementary contribution is
$1/(s-1)-1/s=1/[s(s-1)]$.
Multiplying by $s(s-1)/2$ and using the completion gives
\[
\xi(s)=\tfrac12+\frac{s(s-1)}2
\int_1^\infty(x^{s/2}+x^{(1-s)/2})\psi(x)\,\frac{dx}{x}.
\]
The integral and all its $s$ derivatives converge locally
uniformly on the entire plane by exponential decay, so
analytic continuation proves this displayed identity for all $s$.
On $0\le\Re s\le1$, $|\Im s|\le3$, the product
$|s(s-1)|\le10$, the powers have combined modulus at most
$2x^{1/2}$, and $n^2\ge1+3(n-1)$ for positive integers.
Using $x^{-1/2}\le1$ for $x\ge1$ gives the full integral bound
\begin{equation}
\left|\xi(s)-\tfrac12\right|
\le\frac{10e^{-\pi}}{\pi(1-e^{-3\pi})}
<\frac{8000}{47994}<\frac13,\qquad \Re\xi(s)>\frac16.
\tag{AX5}
\end{equation}
The strict rational bound uses only $\pi>3$, $e^3>20$ and
$e^9>8000$. No tail or Gamma factor was discarded.
The classification AX4, the boundary argument and AX5 prove
\begin{equation}
\xi(r)\ne0\text{ at every finite root of }h_\varepsilon
\quad(\varepsilon>0),\qquad
j_{h_\varepsilon}\xi\text{ is a unit in }\C[s]/(h_\varepsilon).
\tag{AX6}
\end{equation}
AR7 retains root multiplicity at the double root. At
$\varepsilon^2=2$ this algebra has degree three; the infinity
residue is AR8, not a fictitious value $\xi(\infty)$.

\section{The precise obstruction to extending the selected residue}
The global infinity residue AR8 is not the continuous extension
of the selected finite-root residue AR3. This failure already
occurs at the original finite source point $q_{\mathrm M}$,
not only at the missing sign chart. For complex $a\ne0$ take
$q_a=(a,0,i/2,0)$, so $q_a\to q_{\mathrm M}=(0,0,i/2,0)$
and the selected root is $-i/a$. For real $\varepsilon>0$,
the completion, reflection and the exact Gamma identity
$|\Gamma(1/2+it)|^2=\pi/\cosh(\pi t)$ give the equality
below for all $\varepsilon>0$, and its upper bound for
$0<\varepsilon\le1$:
\begin{equation}
\left|\varepsilon^2\xi(-i/\varepsilon)\right|
=\frac{\sqrt{1+\varepsilon^2}}
       {2\sqrt{\cosh(\pi/(2\varepsilon))}}
 |\zeta(1+i/\varepsilon)|
\le\sqrt{\frac{1+\varepsilon^2}{2}}\,
 [\log(1+\varepsilon^{-1})+4]e^{-\pi/(4\varepsilon)}
\longrightarrow0 .
\tag{AX7}
\end{equation}
For completeness, the elementary bound used here follows by
summation against the integer counting function: for $\Re s>0$,
$s\ne1$, and integer $N\ge1$,
\[
\zeta(s)=\sum_{n=1}^{N}n^{-s}
       +\frac{N^{1-s}}{s-1}
       -s\int_N^\infty\{x\}x^{-s-1}\,dx .
\]
It follows first for $\Re s>1$ by integrating the tail counting
function, and then throughout the stated half-plane by uniform
convergence of the integral on compacta. At $s=1+iT$, $T\ge1$,
$N=\lceil T\rceil$, its three absolute bounds are
$1+\log N$, $1/T$, and $|1+iT|/N$, whose sum is less than
$\log(T+1)+4$. Since $\cosh t\ge e^t/2$, AX7 follows.
The Gamma identity follows directly from its reflection
formula at $1/2+it$, retaining the conjugate factor.

In contrast, take $a_n=-i/(2n)$, with $n$ a positive integer.
The same original source path has selected root $2n$ and
exact selected residue
\begin{equation}
a_n^2\xi(2n)
=-\frac{2n-1}{4n}\,(n-1)!\,\pi^{-n}\zeta(2n),
\qquad |a_n^2\xi(2n)|\longrightarrow\infty.
\tag{AX8}
\end{equation}
This follows by inserting $\Gamma(n)=(n-1)!$ in the original
completion. The lower bound $\zeta(2n)>1$ and the ratio
$n/\pi$ of successive factorial-over-$\pi$ factors prove
divergence. Both $q_{a_n}$ and $q_{1/(2n)}$ tend to the
same original point $q_{\mathrm M}$, but their selected
residues have incompatible limits. Hence AR3 has no continuous
extension there. The same conclusion holds at the negative
factor state in the full completion: its residue is identical
for each $a$, and its six factors have the opposite finite limit.
At the limiting target $h_0=s^3-s$, AR9 instead gives the
global residue $\operatorname{Res}_\infty=(3-\pi)/12\ne0$.
It therefore cannot repair this noncontinuity by being assigned
as the missing selected-root value. The exact relation that
does hold is the global residue identity AR8 and the complete
finite remainder map AR6, with all marked signs retained.
Every fixed injective receiving map transports these two
sequences and their unequal arithmetic limits unchanged;
no change of the original Gamma norm removes the obstruction.

The exact certificate
\texttt{verify\_arithmetic\_residues\_and\_jets.py}
checks the original factors, both residue charts, the quartet
matrix and inverse, the confluent evaluation and Hermite inverse,
the principal-part constants and the original escape polynomial.
It treats transcendental values and derivatives as formal scalars.
