\section{Continuous positivity and the exact original native return}
The five isolated cubic pole certificates in WP26 of \cite{WeilCertificates}
are strengthened here to one continuous real interval. The
proof uses the actual finite-height theorem of Platt and Trudgian
and the unconditional zero-count bound of Hasanalizade, Shen
and Wong. Every unverified zero is retained in the tail bound;
no location of such a zero is assumed. The original factor
$h=3$, the cubic coefficient coordinates and both original
conductor norms are preserved by explicit receiving maps.

\section{The complete form and the two human theorems used}
For every real $\tau$ set
\begin{equation}
\omega_\tau=2+i\tau,\qquad h=3,\qquad
U_\tau(s)=\frac{s+1-i\tau}{2+i\tau-s},\qquad
\varphi_j^\tau(s)=\frac{\sqrt3}{2+i\tau-s}U_\tau(s)^j
\quad(0\le j\le3).
\tag{CP1}
\end{equation}
For $p=(p_0,p_1,p_2,p_3)^{\mathsf T}\in\C^4$ write
$F_p^\tau=\sum_{j=0}^3p_j\varphi_j^\tau$.
Use the full reflected Weil form
\begin{equation}
\W(F,G)=\sum_\rho m_\rho
       \overline{F(1-\bar\rho)}G(\rho),\qquad
T_3(\omega_\tau)=\bigl(\W(\varphi_i^\tau,\varphi_j^\tau)\bigr)_{i,j=0}^3 .
\tag{CP2}
\end{equation}
Here $\rho$ ranges over all distinct nontrivial zeta zeros,
and $m_\rho$ is its actual multiplicity. Absolute convergence
is proved below. Thus $p^*T_3p=\W(F_p^\tau,F_p^\tau)$.

The first human input is \cite[Theorem 1]{PlattTrudgian}, using only
the strictly smaller height
\begin{equation}
T_0=3\cdot10^{12}:
\qquad \Re\rho=\tfrac12
\quad\hbox{for every nontrivial zero with }|\Im\rho|\le T_0.
\tag{CP3}
\end{equation}
The published theorem reaches $3\,000\,175\,332\,800$.
The negative-height assertion in CP3 follows by conjugation.
The second input is \cite[Corollary 1.2]{HSW}, with its exact
original constants and counting convention:
\begin{equation}
N(T)=\sum_{0<\Im\rho\le T}m_\rho,\quad
|N(T)-f(T)|\le E(T)\quad(T\ge e),\qquad
f(T)=\frac{T}{2\pi}\log\frac{T}{2\pi e},\quad
E(T)=.1038\log T+.2573\log\log T+9.3675.
\tag{CP4}
\end{equation}
These are previously proved unconditional theorems, not
additional assumptions about the zeros being tested.

\section{Four separated actual zero intervals at every pole height}
For every $A\ge100$, CP4 implies
\begin{equation}
N(A+100)-N(A)>13\log A-52>0.
\tag{CP5}
\end{equation}
Here are all constants in the deduction. Since
$\log\log T\le\log T$ for $T\ge e$,
$E(T)\le\log T+10$. Also
$f'(T)=(2\pi)^{-1}\log(T/(2\pi))$ increases. Hence
\[
N(A+100)-N(A)
\ge\frac{100}{2\pi}\log\frac{A}{2\pi}
                 -\log A-\log(A+100)-20 .
\]
The elementary bounds $\pi<22/7$, $\log(2\pi)<2$ and
$\log2<1$ show that the right side is strictly larger than
$15(\log A-2)-2\log A-22=13\log A-52$.
Here $\log A-2>0$, so the inequality in its multiplier is
in the correct direction. Finally $\log100>4$:
$e<3$ gives $e^4<81<100$. For the other elementary bound,
$e^2>7>2\pi$ follows from the first five positive exponential
terms, whose sum is exactly seven. This proves CP5 including
its domain and endpoints.

Set
\begin{equation}
T_*=\frac{99}{100}T_0=2\,970\,000\,000\,000 .
\tag{CP6}
\end{equation}
For $|\tau|\le T_*$, let $a=|\tau|$ and choose the least
actual zero ordinate in each interval
\begin{equation}
I_j(a)=(a+100+200j,\ a+200+200j]\quad(j=0,1,2,3).
\tag{CP7}
\end{equation}
CP5 proves that each interval contains a zero. Such a least
ordinate exists because there are finitely many zeros in
each bounded interval. Every interval is strictly below
$T_0$, since $T_*+800<T_0$, so CP3 proves that these selected
zeros lie on the critical line. No simplicity is needed.
Write their positive ordinates as $\gamma_j$. For $\tau\ge0$
set $t_j=\gamma_j$; for $\tau<0$ set $t_j=-\gamma_j$.
These are four actual critical zeros in either case.
They are distinct, and their relative heights satisfy
\begin{equation}
100+200j<|t_j-\tau|\le200+200j\le800,\qquad
|t_j-t_i|\ge100(2|j-i|-1)\quad(i\ne j).
\tag{CP8}
\end{equation}
The intervals supply these inequalities directly; no continuity
of the selected zero labels as a function of $\tau$ is needed.
The resulting matrix bound will hold for every real $\tau$
in CP6, with the same numerical constant.

\section{An exact rational Vandermonde inverse bound}
For a critical zero of height $t$, put $z(t)=U_\tau(1/2+it)$.
Direct substitution into CP1 gives
\begin{equation}
z(t)=\frac{3/2+i(t-\tau)}{3/2-i(t-\tau)},\quad |z(t)|=1,\qquad
|z(t)-z(u)|
=\frac{3|t-u|}
 {\sqrt{9/4+(t-\tau)^2}\sqrt{9/4+(u-\tau)^2}}.
\tag{CP9}
\end{equation}
Retain the exact constants
\begin{equation}
L=800^2+\frac94=\frac{2560009}{4},\qquad
w_*=\frac3L,\qquad d_*=\frac{300}{L}.
\tag{CP10}
\end{equation}
For $z_j=z(t_j)$, each of the four row weights
$w_j=3/[9/4+(t_j-\tau)^2]$ is at least $w_*$, and
CP8--9 give
$|z_j-z_i|\ge d_*(2|j-i|-1)$.

Let $V=(z_j^\ell)_{0\le j,\ell\le3}$.
Its inverse has column $j$ equal to the coefficient vector of
the exact Lagrange polynomial
\begin{equation}
\ell_j(z)=\frac{\prod_{i\ne j}(z-z_i)}
                     {\prod_{i\ne j}(z_j-z_i)}.
\tag{CP11}
\end{equation}
Evaluation at the four distinct $z_i$ proves both inverse
compositions. Since $|z_i|=1$, the four numerator coefficient
moduli are at most $1,3,3,1$, so their squared Euclidean norm
is at most twenty. The three denominator separations are
at least $(d_*,3d_*,5d_*)$ for $j=0,3$ and
$(d_*,d_*,3d_*)$ for $j=1,2$. Therefore
\begin{equation}
\|V^{-1}\|_2^2\le\|V^{-1}\|_{\mathrm F}^2
\le20d_*^{-6}\left(\frac2{225}+\frac2{9}\right)
=\frac{208}{45}d_*^{-6}.
\tag{CP12}
\end{equation}
This bound retains all four separated intervals; it does not
replace their product by a determinant divided by a trace.

Taking one copy of each selected zero gives a positive Gram
$H_{\mathrm{sel},\tau}=V^*\diag(w_0,w_1,w_2,w_3)V$.
Because $p=V^{-1}Vp$, CP12 proves for every coefficient vector
\begin{equation}
p^*H_{\mathrm{sel},\tau}p\ge b_*\|p\|_2^2,\qquad
b_*=\frac{45}{208}w_*d_*^6
=\frac{135\cdot1200^6}{52\cdot2560009^7}>10^{-26}.
\tag{CP13}
\end{equation}
The last inequality is exact integer arithmetic: the difference
$135\cdot1200^6\cdot10^{26}-52\cdot2560009^7$ is the positive
integer
$2839912978528311935972297436813173952969845612$.
Multiplicities can only increase the contribution of these
verified zeros.

\section{Every unverified zero and the complete reflected tail}
Write a point in the closed strip as $s=\beta+it$,
$0\le\beta\le1$, and put $x=t-\tau$. For $|x|\ge10$,
the exact factors in CP1 imply
\[
|U_\tau(s)|^2
=\frac{(\beta+1)^2+x^2}{(2-\beta)^2+x^2}
\le1+\frac3{x^2}<\left(\frac{51}{50}\right)^2,\qquad
|U_\tau'(s)|=\frac3{|\omega_\tau-s|^2}\le\frac3{x^2}.
\]
Differentiate each of the four original functions without
changing its degree or the factor $\sqrt3$. For $0\le j\le3$,
\begin{equation}
|(\varphi_j^\tau)'(s)|
\le\frac{\sqrt3}{x^2}
\left[\left(\frac{51}{50}\right)^3+
 \frac9{|x|}\left(\frac{51}{50}\right)^2\right]
<\frac4{x^2},\qquad
|(F_p^\tau)'(s)|\le\frac8{x^2}\|p\|_2 .
\tag{CP14}
\end{equation}
For the strict numerical inequality it is enough to use
$\sqrt3<7/4$ and $|x|\ge10$, giving the rational upper
coefficient $3.495744<4$.
The last inequality is Cauchy--Schwarz on the four derivatives.

The reflected zeros $\rho=\beta+it$ and
$\rho^\vee=1-\bar\rho=1-\beta+it$ have equal multiplicity.
Integrating CP14 on their horizontal segment, whose length
is at most one and whose imaginary part stays $t$, gives
\[
|F_p^\tau(\rho)-F_p^\tau(\rho^\vee)|
\le\frac8{|t-\tau|^2}\|p\|_2.
\]
Every noncritical reflected pair of multiplicity $m$ consequently
has its exact contribution bounded below by
\begin{align}
2m\Re(\overline{F_p^\tau(\rho^\vee)}F_p^\tau(\rho))
&=m\bigl(|F_p^\tau(\rho)|^2+|F_p^\tau(\rho^\vee)|^2
 -|F_p^\tau(\rho)-F_p^\tau(\rho^\vee)|^2\bigr)\nonumber\\
&\ge-\frac{64m}{|t-\tau|^4}\|p\|_2^2.
\tag{CP15}
\end{align}
Critical zeros, whether verified or not, contribute nonnegatively.
For each positive-height noncritical pair, its two members
contribute $2m$ to $N$. The conjugate negative-height pair
has the same multiplicity and relative height $-t-\tau$.
Therefore the total possible negative contribution from all
unverified zeros is bounded by
\[
32\|p\|_2^2
\sum_{\Im\rho>T_0}m_\rho
\left[\frac1{|\Im\rho-\tau|^4}
      +\frac1{|\Im\rho+\tau|^4}\right].
\]
For $|\tau|\le T_*$ and $t>T_0$,
$|t\pm\tau|\ge t/100>T_0/100>10$. Thus CP14 applies,
and this last expression is at most
$64\cdot100^4\|p\|_2^2\sum_{\Im\rho>T_0}m_\rho/(\Im\rho)^4$.

The zero count CP4 also gives $N(T)\le T\log T$ for $T\ge100$.
In fact $f(T)<T\log T/6$, and
$E(T)\le\log T+10<T$, while $\log T>4$.
The last strict bound follows at $100$ and persists because
$T-\log T-10$ increases. Stieltjes integration by parts now gives
\begin{align}
\sum_{\Im\rho>T_0}\frac{m_\rho}{(\Im\rho)^4}
&=-\frac{N(T_0)}{T_0^4}
     +4\int_{T_0}^\infty\frac{N(t)}{t^5}\,dt\nonumber\\
&\le\frac4{T_0^3}
              \left(\frac{\log T_0}{3}+\frac19\right).
\tag{CP16}
\end{align}
The boundary term at infinity is zero by the same count bound.
Every term above the verified height has therefore been retained
in a convergent upper estimate.
For completeness CP4 also proves absolute convergence of CP2:
for fixed $\tau$, each $\varphi_j^\tau(\rho)=O(|\Im\rho|^{-1})$,
uniformly across the strip at large height, and
$\sum_{\Im\rho>R}m_\rho/(\Im\rho)^2<\infty$ follows by the
same Stieltjes calculation. Conjugate negative heights satisfy
the same bound. Hence the reflection grouping used above is valid.

Combining CP15--16, the whole unknown negative tail is at most
$e_*\|p\|_2^2$, where the following constants are exact:
\begin{equation}
e_*=\frac{256\cdot100^4}{T_0^3}\frac{91}{9}
=\frac{23296}{243\cdot10^{28}}
<\frac{24}{25}\,10^{-26},\qquad
\mu_*=b_*-e_*>4\cdot10^{-28}.
\tag{CP17}
\end{equation}
Here $\log T_0<30$. One elementary exact check is that the
finite sum $\sum_{k=0}^{30}30^k/k!$ exceeds $3\cdot10^{12}$,
so $e^{30}>T_0$. The rational comparison for $e_*$ is
$23296/243<96$, equivalent to $23296<23328$.
The last lower bound follows from CP13:
$10^{-26}-(24/25)10^{-26}=4\cdot10^{-28}$.

Every remaining zero below $T_0$ is critical by CP3 and
contributes nonnegatively. Adding CP13 and subtracting this
complete tail proves the continuous, unconditional result
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\quad
T_3(2+i\tau)\succeq\mu_* I_4
\succeq4\cdot10^{-28}I_4
\quad\hbox{for every real }|\tau|\le2\,970\,000\,000\,000.
\quad}
\tag{CP18}
\end{equation}
The same coefficient bound holds for all four coordinates
and all relative phases simultaneously. The selection of four
verified zeros proves one positive contribution; the rest
of the complete form is controlled, not omitted.

\section{Return to the unchanged original conductor and its Gamma norm}
Retain any fixed admitted reference conductor from FC21--32
of \cite{Fable029}, its actual symbol order $v$, its original shifts
and coefficients, and its exact source quotient and target:
\[
T_A:\mathcal P_{v+3}/\mathcal P_{v-1}\longrightarrow\mathcal P_3,
\qquad T_Ap(S')=\sum_{u,w=0}^8 a_{uw}p(S'+\beta_{8,u,w}).
\]
The reference roots are denoted $r_{\ell,*}$ and remain fixed
as the pole height $\tau$ varies. With the original marked
collision matrix $K$, let
\[
\ell_{\ell,*}(S)=
\prod_{b\ne\ell}\frac{S-r_{b,*}}{r_{\ell,*}-r_{b,*}},\qquad
y_\ell(S')=\sum_{u,w=0}^8a_{uw}
 (S'+\beta_{8,u,w})^v\ell_{\ell,*}(S'+\beta_{8,u,w}).
\]
Writing $Y$ for their columns in the original target monomials,
the retained map is $\Psi=YK^{-1}$. Its source partner is
$\Phi_{\rm FC}$ with $T_A\Phi_{\rm FC}=\Psi$.
All these original maps are the proved isomorphisms on the
actual quotient; the reference periods, moments and norms do
not depend on $\tau$.

Let $c_*=a_{\rm amp}/2-4$ and $\kappa=c_*-1/2$.
The translation matrix and rational numerator matrix are
\begin{align}
(C_\kappa)_{r\ell}&=\binom{\ell}{r}\kappa^{\ell-r}
 \quad(0\le r\le\ell\le3),\qquad
C_\kappa^{-1}=C_{-\kappa},\nonumber\\
R_\tau&=\sqrt3
\begin{pmatrix}
\omega^3&b\omega^2&b^2\omega&b^3\\
-3\omega^2&\omega^2-2b\omega&2b\omega-b^2&3b^2\\
3\omega&b-2\omega&\omega-2b&3b\\
-1&1&-1&1
\end{pmatrix},
\quad \omega=2+i\tau,\quad b=1-i\tau,\quad \det R_\tau=3^8.
\tag{CP19}
\end{align}
The $j$th column is exactly the coefficient vector of
$\sqrt3(z+b)^j(\omega-z)^{3-j}$.
For clarity its full inverse, also fixing every sign, is
\[
(R_\tau^{-1})_{j\ell}
=3^{-7/2}\sum_{r=0}^{\ell}
\binom{\ell}{r}\omega^r(-b)^{\ell-r}\binom{3-\ell}{j-r}.
\]
Indeed $u=(z+b)/(\omega-z)$ gives
$z=(\omega u-b)/(1+u)$ and $\omega-z=3/(1+u)$;
expanding $3^{-7/2}(1+u)^3p((\omega u-b)/(1+u))$
proves the inverse formula by substitution.
The determinant follows by changing to $t=\omega-z$:
the columns $\sqrt3(3-t)^jt^{3-j}$ become triangular
after reversal, and the two reversal signs cancel.

The exact original receiver and its inverse are consequently
\begin{equation}
B_\tau=R_\tau^{-1}C_\kappa\Psi,\qquad
B_\tau^{-1}=\Psi^{-1}C_{-\kappa}R_\tau,\qquad
F_{B_\tau q}^\tau(z)=\frac{(\Psi q)(z+\kappa)}{(\omega-z)^4}.
\tag{CP20}
\end{equation}
Multiplying the numerator coefficients proves the last identity.
It also proves the original conductor return
$B_\tau=R_\tau^{-1}C_\kappa T_A\Phi_{\rm FC}$.
Thus CP18 gives the actual, continuously parameterized form
on the original Fable coordinates:
\begin{equation}
\W_{\tau,\rm original}
=B_\tau^*T_3(2+i\tau)B_\tau
\succeq\mu_* B_\tau^*B_\tau
\quad(|\tau|\le T_*).
\tag{CP21}
\end{equation}
This is a strict positive form because $B_\tau$ is invertible.
The coefficient norm on the right is not substituted for
the original physical norm; its exact comparison is next.

For the original positive parameter $s_0\in\{1,a_{\rm amp}\}$,
retain the full target measure
\[
dm_{s_0}(y)=
\frac{(2\pi)^{s_0/2}2^{s_0/2}}{4\pi\Gamma(s_0/2)}
 \left|\Gamma(s_0/4+iy/2)\right|^2dy,
\quad
\|p\|_{\Gamma,W}^2=\int_{\R}|p(c_*+iy)|^2\,dm_{s_0}(y).
\]
Let $\mathcal H$ be this Gram in $(1,S',S'^2,S'^3)$ and
$G=\Psi^*\mathcal H\Psi$. In rational coefficients the same
norm has the exact Gram
\begin{equation}
H_\tau=B_\tau^{-*}GB_\tau^{-1}
=R_\tau^*C_{-\kappa}^*\mathcal HC_{-\kappa}R_\tau,\qquad
(H_\tau)_{ij}
=3\int_{\R}
 (3/2+i(y-\tau))^{3+j-i}
 (3/2-i(y-\tau))^{3+i-j}\,dm_{s_0}(y).
\tag{CP22}
\end{equation}
Here $H_\tau$ denotes the full native Gram; the selected-zero
Gram $H_{\mathrm{sel},\tau}$ was used only in CP13.
To prove CP22, evaluate its numerator at
$S'=c_*+iy$: the translated argument is exactly $1/2+iy$.
The two factors are conjugate, with exponents displayed
after multiplying conjugate column $i$ and column $j$.
This retains the original center, total mass and every cross term.

Put $M=(2\pi)^{s_0/2}$ and $\beta_0=s_0/2$. The original
Gamma moments are
$m_0=M$, $m_2=M\beta_0$,
$m_4=M(3\beta_0^2+2\beta_0)$,
$m_6=M(15\beta_0^3+30\beta_0^2+16\beta_0)$, with odd
moments zero, as proved for this unchanged measure in
FC56a/RW9 \cite{Fable029}. Hence every diagonal entry of CP22 is
the following explicit positive polynomial:
\begin{align}
g_0(\tau)=3\bigg[&
\frac{729}{64}m_0+\frac{243}{16}(m_2+\tau^2m_0)
+\frac{27}{4}(m_4+6\tau^2m_2+\tau^4m_0)\nonumber\\
&+m_6+15\tau^2m_4+15\tau^4m_2+\tau^6m_0\bigg],
\qquad \operatorname{tr}H_\tau=4g_0(\tau).
\tag{CP23}
\end{align}
Expansion of $3(9/4+(y-\tau)^2)^3$ proves this formula.
Since a positive Hermitian matrix has largest eigenvalue
at most its trace, CP18 yields the exact native lower bound
\begin{equation}
q^*\W_{\tau,\rm original}q
\ge\frac{\mu_*}{4g_0(\tau)}\,q^*Gq
\quad(|\tau|\le T_*).
\tag{CP24}
\end{equation}
Every coefficient of $g_0$ as a polynomial in $\tau^2$ is
positive. Thus $\mu_*/[4g_0(T_*)]$ is also a single explicit
positive lower constant for the full continuous interval
in the unchanged original target norm.

\section{All eight signed states and the exact remaining kernel}
In the original eight-state construction, retain the actual
four columns of pair means and half differences
$E=(e_1,\ldots,e_4)$, $O=(o_1,\ldots,o_4)$, with
$e_j=(q_{j,+}+q_{j,-})/2$ and
$o_j=(q_{j,+}-q_{j,-})/2$. These are the complete original
source points over the literal quartet, including both signs,
as defined and proved in SE1--7 of \cite{Fable029}. Their determinant
is
$1792\chi\delta^2\gamma^2(2\delta^2\gamma^2-\delta^2+\gamma^2)$,
where $\chi=\pm1$. It is nonzero on the original admitted
domain $0<\delta<1/2$, $\gamma>2$. Thus $O^{-1}$ is the
exact original inverse on this domain, with no extra state
or sign omitted. Write a full signed vector as $(\alpha,\beta)$,
whose actual evaluation is $z=E\alpha+O\beta$.

Let $G_Y=Y^*\langle\, ,\,\rangle_{\Gamma,W}Y$ be the
original target label Gram. The complete target norm and
arithmetic form are
\begin{align}
\mathbb H_W&=
\begin{pmatrix}I&0\\E&O\end{pmatrix}^{*}
\diag(G_Y,G)
\begin{pmatrix}I&0\\E&O\end{pmatrix},\nonumber\\
\mathbb W_\tau&=[E\ O]^*B_\tau^*T_3(2+i\tau)B_\tau[E\ O],
\qquad
S_\tau=\begin{pmatrix}I&0\\-O^{-1}E&O^{-1}B_\tau^{-1}\end{pmatrix}.
\tag{CP25}
\end{align}
Direct multiplication proves
\[
\begin{pmatrix}I&0\\E&O\end{pmatrix}S_\tau
=\diag(I,B_\tau^{-1}),\qquad
[E\ O]S_\tau=[0\ B_\tau^{-1}].
\]
Consequently CP18 proves the full simultaneous comparison
\begin{equation}
S_\tau^*\mathbb W_\tau S_\tau
=\diag(0_4,T_3(2+i\tau))
\succeq\diag(0_4,\mu_*I_4),\qquad
S_\tau^*\mathbb H_W S_\tau=\diag(G_Y,H_\tau).
\tag{CP26}
\end{equation}
The inverse of $S_\tau$ is
$\begin{psmallmatrix}I&0\\B_\tau E&B_\tau O\end{psmallmatrix}$,
verified by multiplying either way. The complete kernel, rank,
and target quotient coercivity are therefore exactly
\begin{equation}
\ker\mathbb W_\tau
=\{(\alpha,-O^{-1}E\alpha):\alpha\in\C^4\},\qquad
\operatorname{rank}\mathbb W_\tau=4,\qquad
\mathbb W_\tau|_{\rm quotient}
\succeq\frac{\mu_*}{4g_0(\tau)}
                         \mathbb H_W|_{\rm quotient}.
\tag{CP27}
\end{equation}
To specify the quotient norm without an informal deletion,
the full coordinate pair is $(\alpha,z)$ and its squared
norm is $\alpha^*G_Y\alpha+z^*Gz$.
For fixed $z$ the unique minimum over the kernel coset is
at $\alpha=0$. Its section is
$(0,O^{-1}z)$. In rational coordinates $d=B_\tau z$ it is
$(0,O^{-1}B_\tau^{-1}d)$ and has squared norm $d^*H_\tau d$.
This proves exactly the quotient and the constant in CP27.
There are no additional arithmetic null directions in this
continuous pole range, because CP18 makes $T_3$ positive definite.

The source norm remains distinct. Let
$G_X=X^*\langle\, ,\,\rangle_{\Gamma,U}X$ and
$G_U=\Phi_{\rm FC}^*\langle\, ,\,\rangle_{\Gamma,U}\Phi_{\rm FC}$,
with the original source quotient norm, and put
$H_{U,\tau}=B_\tau^{-*}G_UB_\tau^{-1}$.
For the actual source pair
$(X\alpha,\Phi_{\rm FC}z)$ the same multiplication gives
\begin{equation}
S_\tau^*\mathbb H_U S_\tau=\diag(G_X,H_{U,\tau}),\qquad
\mathbb W_\tau|_{\rm source\ quotient}
\succeq
\frac{\mu_*}{\operatorname{tr}H_{U,\tau}}\,
                 \mathbb H_U|_{\rm quotient}.
\tag{CP28}
\end{equation}
The source quotient again minimizes at $\alpha=0$;
$H_{U,\tau}$ is its exact positive Gram, with every original
inverse coefficient specified by FC24. The actual conductor
maps this pair to $(Y\alpha,\Psi z)$ because
$T_AX=Y$ and $T_A\Phi_{\rm FC}=\Psi$. Thus CP25--28 preserve
both original metrics and their precise morphism.

There is also a uniform source constant with every original
inverse weight explicitly present. Write the retained conductor
symbol as
$g_A(t)=t^{-v}e^{-4w(t)}E_A(w(t))=\sum_{n\ge0}c_nt^n$,
where $w(t)=-i\arctan t$ and
$c_0=(-i)^v\mu_v/v!\ne0$ is its actual leading moment.
The four inverse coefficients are exactly
\[
\eta_0=c_0^{-1},\quad \eta_1=-c_1/c_0^2,\quad
\eta_2=(c_1^2-c_0c_2)/c_0^3,\quad
\eta_3=(-c_1^3+2c_0c_1c_2-c_0^2c_3)/c_0^4 .
\]
Their convolution with $c_0,c_1,c_2,c_3$ gives $(1,0,0,0)$.
With the original weights $\rho_n=\sqrt{n!/(s_0/2)_n}$,
the actual inverse matrix in the original orthonormal source
and target frames has entry
$\eta_{j-i}\rho_j/\rho_{v+i}$ for $0\le i\le j\le3$,
and zero below the diagonal, by FC24/WCF6.
Thus its complete squared Frobenius norm and uniform
source comparison are
\begin{equation}
J_A=\sum_{0\le i\le j\le3}
 |\eta_{j-i}|^2\frac{\rho_j^2}{\rho_{v+i}^2}>0,\qquad
H_{U,\tau}\preceq J_AH_\tau,\qquad
\mathbb W_\tau|_{\rm source\ quotient}
\succeq\frac{\mu_*}{4J_Ag_0(T_*)}
                  \mathbb H_U|_{\rm quotient}
\quad(|\tau|\le T_*).
\tag{CP29}
\end{equation}
Indeed a matrix has operator norm at most its Frobenius norm,
so the literal source inverse gives
$\|T_A^{-1}p\|_{\Gamma,U}^2\le J_A\|p\|_{\Gamma,W}^2$.
Substitute $p=C_{-\kappa}R_\tau d$ to obtain the matrix
inequality; CP23--24 prove the last bound. Orthogonal
coordinates are used only to calculate the norm of this
unchanged physical inverse: all masses and quotient weights
are those of the original source and target.

These results establish a continuous positive cubic test
range and its exact full eight-state kernel. The proof uses
the original full-Weil sum and the original cubic receiver
throughout. It makes no assertion about degrees above three
or about poles outside the explicit interval CP6.

The certificate
\texttt{verify\_continuous\_pole\_cubic\_positivity.py}
checks all rational constant comparisons by integer arithmetic,
the coefficient-norm and reflected-pair multiplicity factors,
the exact chord identity, and the native diagonal-moment formula.
Its output is retained separately. The human theorems CP3--4
are cited mathematical inputs, not assertions recertified by
that short algebra script.

\section{Complete original source and target coercivity}
The analytic inputs to this note are the proved full-zero-sum
inequalities WP14 and WP23, and the certified five-pole matrices
WP26, in \cite{WeilCertificates}. The receiving maps and their two distinct
original metrics are RW1--20 \cite{Fable029}; their exact native
matrices are NH1--42 \cite{Fable029}. We use those results at the
stated poles, retain every zero multiplicity in the Weil form,
and calculate the entire source and target coercivity on the
four-dimensional quotient of the full eight-state space.
No assertion about untested degrees or poles is used.

\section{The full signed space, its exact kernel and both minimum lifts}
Keep the four marked roots $r_j$ of the literal quartet polynomial
$h_\rho(r)=Ar^4+r^3+Br^2+Cr+D$, where
\[
 A=-\tfrac12,\quad B=\delta^2-\gamma^2-\tfrac34,\quad
 C=\gamma^2-\delta^2+\tfrac14,\quad
 D=-\tfrac1{32}-\tfrac{\gamma^2-\delta^2}{4}
                         -\tfrac{(\delta^2+\gamma^2)^2}{2},
 \quad0<\delta<\tfrac12,\quad\gamma>2.
\]
At each root retain both signs of $a_j^2h_\rho'(r_j)=1$,
put $d_j=h_\rho'(r_j)$, and use the actual mean and difference
columns of the inverse fibre:
\[
 e_j=\begin{pmatrix}0\\-r_j\\3id_j\\
 (B-17r_j+Ar_j^2)d_j-2Ad_j^2\end{pmatrix},\qquad
 o_j=a_j\begin{pmatrix}1\\-id_j\\Ad_j^2+2r_jd_j\\
 7ir_j^2d_j-13id_j^2\end{pmatrix},\quad E=(e_j),\ O=(o_j).
 \tag{NW1}
\]
These are SE1, proved from the original ES polynomial
\cite{ES488,Fable029}. The complete determinant proved in SE7 is
\[
 \det O=1792\chi\delta^2\gamma^2
 (2\delta^2\gamma^2-\delta^2+\gamma^2)\ne0,
 \qquad \chi=A^2\det(r_j^n)_{n,j}\prod_ja_j\in\{1,-1\}.
\]
Thus the inverse in every formula below is the explicit
$O^{-1}=\operatorname{adj}(O)/\det O$; no sign partner is
discarded.

Let $f_{j,+},f_{j,-}$ be the eight separate basis states.
Set $\iota_+c_j=(f_{j,+}+f_{j,-})/2$ and
$\iota_-c_j=(f_{j,+}-f_{j,-})/2$. Every state is uniquely
$v=\iota_+\alpha+\iota_-\beta$; define its complete evaluation
$z=E\alpha+O\beta$. The original source and target states
are, respectively,
\[
 (X\alpha,\Phi z)\in U\oplus U,\qquad
 (Y\alpha,\Psi z)\in W\oplus W,
 \quad T_AX=Y,\quad T_A\Phi=\Psi.
 \tag{NW2}
\]
All four maps are the existing exact conductor maps of
FC26--35, with the original periods and moments. Put
$G_X=X^*\langle\ ,\ \rangle_U X$,
$G_Y=Y^*\langle\ ,\ \rangle_W Y$,
$G_U=\Phi^*\langle\ ,\ \rangle_U\Phi$ and
$G=\Psi^*\langle\ ,\ \rangle_W\Psi$.
Each direct sum in NW2 has its original orthogonal direct-sum
norm, so the exact squared norms are
\[
 \|v\|_{8,U}^2=\alpha^*G_X\alpha+z^*G_Uz,
 \qquad
 \|v\|_{8,W}^2=\alpha^*G_Y\alpha+z^*Gz.
 \tag{NW3}
\]
No summand uses the norm from the other side of the conductor.

For the rational receiver retain
$\omega=2+i\tau$, $\tau\in\{0,14,21,100,1000\}$,
$h=3$, $d=h/2=3/2$, $c_*=a_{\rm amp}/2-4$ and
$\kappa=c_*-1/2$. Let $R_\omega$ be the complete coefficient
matrix of
$N_j(s)=\sqrt3(s+\bar\omega-1)^j(\omega-s)^{3-j}$,
$0\le j\le3$, and let $C_\kappa$ act by
$p(S')\mapsto p(s+\kappa)$. Its entries are
$(C_\kappa)_{r\ell}=\binom\ell r\kappa^{\ell-r}$ for
$r\le\ell$; its inverse is $C_{-\kappa}$. Define the exact
rational coefficient column and receiver by
\[
 d_v=B_\omega z,\qquad B_\omega=R_\omega^{-1}C_\kappa\Psi,
 \qquad
 F_v(s)=\sum_{j=0}^3(d_v)_j\frac{\sqrt3}{\omega-s}
                     \left(\frac{s+\bar\omega-1}{\omega-s}\right)^j.
 \tag{NW4}
\]
RW3 gives every coefficient of $R_\omega$ and its inverse;
$\det R_\omega=3^8\ne0$. Thus $B_\omega$ is an actual
isomorphism. The full Weil form is exactly
\[
 \mathbb W_\omega(v,v)=\W(F_v,F_v)=d_v^*T_3(\omega)d_v.
 \tag{NW5}
\]
This is the full zero sum of WP3 and RW12, including all
nontrivial zero multiplicities, not a four-zero truncation.

At all five poles $T_3(\omega)$ is strictly positive by
WP26, and at $\tau=0$ WP23 also supplies an analytic positive
lower bound. Therefore the full kernel in the eight-state
space is precisely
\[
 \mathcal N=\{\iota_+\alpha-\iota_-O^{-1}E\alpha:
                              \alpha\in\C^4\}.
 \tag{NW6}
\]
Its four explicit independent generators, in the original
eight marked basis states, are
\[
 n_j=\frac{f_{j,+}+f_{j,-}}2
       -\frac12\sum_{\ell=1}^4(O^{-1}E)_{\ell j}
                                      (f_{\ell,+}-f_{\ell,-}).
 \tag{NW7}
\]
Independence follows from their even components $c_j$.
Their evaluation is zero, so they are annihilated by NW5.
Conversely positivity forces $d_v=0$, and the invertible
$B_\omega,O$ then give exactly NW6. This proves both
inclusions and the exact nullity four.

For every coefficient column $d$ there is a unique vector
orthogonal to $\mathcal N$ in either of the two original
norms and having that column:
\[
 \ell_\omega(d)=\iota_-O^{-1}B_\omega^{-1}d.
 \tag{NW8}
\]
To prove both minimization assertions rather than just a
section identity, every vector with this column is
$\ell_\omega(d)+\iota_+\alpha-\iota_-O^{-1}E\alpha$.
In NW3 its evaluation is the fixed $B_\omega^{-1}d$,
and the remaining squared norm is $\alpha^*G_X\alpha$
or $\alpha^*G_Y\alpha$, strictly minimized at $\alpha=0$.
The cross term vanishes by NW2, because the first component
of the section and the second component of the null vector
are both zero. Consequently the exact quotient norms are
\[
 \|[v]\|_{Q,W}^2=d_v^*H_\omega d_v=z^*Gz,
 \qquad
 \|[v]\|_{Q,U}^2=d_v^*H_{U,\omega}d_v=z^*G_Uz.
 \tag{NW9}
\]
The actual target polynomial of NW8 is
$C_{-\kappa}R_\omega d$; its actual source is
$T_A^{-1}C_{-\kappa}R_\omega d$, with FC24 coefficients
evaluated below. Thus both minimum lifts are fully typed
through the original conductor.

\section{Evaluated original target and source constants}
Retain $b=s/2>0$, $M=(2\pi)^{s/2}$ and the original
measure
\[
 dm_s(y)=\frac{M2^{s/2}}{4\pi\Gamma(s/2)}
                 |\Gamma(s/4+iy/2)|^2dy.
 \tag{NW10}
\]
Its moments are $M$, $Mb$, $M(3b^2+2b)$ and
$M(15b^3+30b^2+16b)$ in degrees $0,2,4,6$, with
odd moments zero \cite{WCF,Fable029}. For the target, all diagonal
entries of $H_\omega$ are
$3\int((3/2)^2+(y-\tau)^2)^3dm_s(y)$. Expanding with
these unchanged moments gives the following exact trace:
\[
\begin{aligned}
 t_W(\tau):=\tr H_\omega=12M\big[&\tau^6+(15b+27/4)\tau^4
 +(45b^2+141b/2+243/16)\tau^2\\
 &+15b^3+201b^2/4+715b/16+729/64\big]>0.
\end{aligned}\tag{NW11}
\]
Its exact determinant is
\[
 D_W:=\det H_\omega=12\cdot3^{16}M^4b^3(b+1)^2(b+2)>0.
 \tag{NW12}
\]
No test-pole height is omitted from NW11; the height cancels
from NW12 by the exact coefficient determinant proved in
RW11 and NH10.

Here is an equally explicit source trace, including every
original inverse coefficient. Let $v$ be the actual order
of the original conductor and $\mu_n=E_A^{(n)}(0)$,
$\mu_v\ne0$. Retain
\[
\begin{gathered}
 \nu_{v+j}=\sum_{r=0}^j\binom{v+j}{v+r}(-4)^{j-r}\mu_{v+r},
 \quad0\le j\le3,\\
 g_0=(-i)^v\mu_v/v!,\quad
 g_1=(-i)^{v+1}\nu_{v+1}/(v+1)!,\\
 g_2=(-i)^{v+2}\nu_{v+2}/(v+2)!-(v/3)g_0,\quad
 g_3=(-i)^{v+3}\nu_{v+3}/(v+3)!-((v+1)/3)g_1,\\
 \xi_0=g_0^{-1},\quad \xi_1=-g_1g_0^{-2},\quad
 \xi_2=g_1^2g_0^{-3}-g_2g_0^{-2},\quad
 \xi_3=-g_1^3g_0^{-4}+2g_1g_2g_0^{-3}-g_3g_0^{-2},\\
 k_j=(b)_{v+j}/(v+j)!,\quad j=0,1,2,3.
\end{gathered}\tag{NW13}
\]
These are the finite FC24 inverse coefficients, including
the original center separation $4$ and the original
$w(t)=-i\arctan t$ phase. Their derivation and multiplication
check are NH26--32. Write the full matrix
\[
 F=\begin{pmatrix}
 \xi_0&\xi_1&b\xi_0+2\xi_2&(3b+2)\xi_1+6\xi_3\\
 0&\xi_0&2\xi_1&(3b+2)\xi_0+6\xi_2\\
 0&0&2\xi_0&6\xi_1\\0&0&0&6\xi_0
 \end{pmatrix}.
 \tag{NW14}
\]
In the original minimum quotient orthonormal frame
$[\phi_v],\ldots,[\phi_{v+3}]$ at center $c_0=c_*+4$,
the inverse images of $1,y,y^2,y^3$, where
$y=(S'-c_*)/i$, have matrix
$\sqrt M\diag(\sqrt{k_j})F$. The actual polynomials are
specified by
$\phi_n(S)=p_{n,s}((S-c_0)/i)/\sqrt{Mn!(b)_n}$ and
$p_{n+1,s}=yp_{n,s}-n(n-1+b)p_{n-1,s}$,
$p_{0,s}=1,p_{1,s}=y$. These representatives contain no
component in the removed kernel $\mathcal P_{v-1}$, so
their Gram $K_U=MF^*\diag(k_j)F$ is the original minimum
quotient Gram. Its entries and all leading minors are
evaluated in NH33--34.

For complete trace evaluation define the following explicit
real symmetric polynomial matrix, with $d=3/2$:
\[
 Z(\tau)=4\begin{pmatrix}
 (d^2+\tau^2)^3&-3\tau(d^2+\tau^2)^2&-(d^2-3\tau^2)(d^2+\tau^2)&\tau(d^2-\tau^2)\\
 -3\tau(d^2+\tau^2)^2&(d^2+\tau^2)(5d^2+9\tau^2)&-\tau(7d^2+9\tau^2)&-(d^2-3\tau^2)\\
 -(d^2-3\tau^2)(d^2+\tau^2)&-\tau(7d^2+9\tau^2)&5d^2+9\tau^2&-3\tau\\
 \tau(d^2-\tau^2)&-(d^2-3\tau^2)&-3\tau&1
 \end{pmatrix}.
 \tag{NW15}
\]
Every entry comes from $Z=Q(\tau)Q(\tau)^*$, where the
column $j$ of $Q$ is the full coefficient vector of
$(d+i(y-\tau))^j(d-i(y-\tau))^{3-j}$. NH6 gives all
sixteen entries of $Q$; expanding their pairwise products
proves NW15 directly. The exact source trace and determinant
are therefore the finite original-moment expressions
\[
 \boxed{\quad t_U(\tau)=3M\sum_{r=0}^3k_r
               \sum_{p,q=0}^3F_{rp}\overline{F_{rq}}Z_{pq}(\tau),
 \qquad
 D_U=144\cdot3^{16}M^4k_0k_1k_2k_3|\xi_0|^8.\quad}
 \tag{NW16}
\]
All symbols on the right are evaluated in NW13--15;
there is no unspecified matrix norm. Indeed
$H_{U,\omega}=3Q^*K_UQ$, so its trace is the first
expression by multiplication. Its determinant is the
squared modulus of the exact triangular source coefficient
matrix, times $|\det(\sqrt3Q)|^2=3^{16}$, giving the
second expression. Both are strictly positive. Formula
NW16 retains every cross term and every inverse moment;
it does not replace $K_U$ by its diagonal.

\section{Five explicit unconditional native coercivity bounds}
The provider's analytic full-form inequality WP23 is
\[
 T_3(2)\succeq a_*I,\qquad
 a_*=\frac1{2\cdot200^4\cdot30^{12}}>0.
 \tag{NW17}
\]
Its proof includes the unverified high zeros by the signed
tail estimate WP20--22, and depends on the stated theorems
of Platt--Trudgian and Hasanalizade--Shen--Wong through
WP4--5. It is unconditional. The five exact-ball matrices
in WP26 give stronger numerical coefficient bounds that
can be extracted without rerunning the zeta calculation.
The following are strict outward rational enclosures of
the provider's stored real balls:
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
 \tau&c_0<q_\tau&\det T_3(2+i\tau)>D_\tau&
       a_\tau=27D_\tau/(4q_\tau)^3\\\hline
 0&7/50&468/10^{17}&3159/4390400000000000\\
 14&38/25&967/10^8&26109/22475571200\\
 21&173/100&1299/10^5&175365/165686944\\
 100&251/100&9&3796875/15813251\\
 1000&127/25&556&58640625/32774128
\end{array}
\tag{NW18}
\]
All first four principal minors in each stored matrix were
proved positive by WP26, hence the four eigenvalues are
positive. Their sum is $4c_0<4q_\tau=:S_\tau$. If
$\lambda$ is the smallest, the product of the other three
is at most $(S_\tau/3)^3$ by the arithmetic--geometric
mean inequality. Thus
\[
 a_\tau I\preceq T_3(2+i\tau)\preceq S_\tau I.
 \tag{NW19}
\]
The strict inequalities from the balls imply these weak
matrix inequalities with the displayed exact rational
constants. At zero, both $a_*$ and $a_0$ are available;
$a_0>a_*$ is also an exact rational comparison.

Every positive four-by-four Hermitian matrix $H$ satisfies
\[
 H\preceq(\tr H)I,\qquad
 H\succeq\frac{27\det H}{(\tr H)^3}I.
 \tag{NW20}
\]
The first follows by summing its positive eigenvalues;
the second follows by the same product-of-three argument
just used. Apply NW20 separately to the actual $H_\omega$
and $H_{U,\omega}$, with their explicitly evaluated
traces and determinants. Combining with NW5, NW9 and
NW19 proves the complete two-sided quotient estimates:
\[
\boxed{\begin{aligned}
 \frac{a_\tau}{t_W(\tau)}\|[v]\|_{Q,W}^2
 &\le\mathbb W_{2+i\tau}(v,v)
 \le\frac{S_\tau t_W(\tau)^3}{27D_W}\|[v]\|_{Q,W}^2,\\
 \frac{a_\tau}{t_U(\tau)}\|[v]\|_{Q,U}^2
 &\le\mathbb W_{2+i\tau}(v,v)
 \le\frac{S_\tau t_U(\tau)^3}{27D_U}\|[v]\|_{Q,U}^2,
 \qquad\tau\in\{0,14,21,100,1000\}.
\end{aligned}}\tag{NW21}
\]
Every constant on the right and left is the finite expression
NW11--16 and NW18. Thus these are quantitative inequalities
in each original native norm, with no coefficient norm left
unconverted. At $\tau=0$ the entirely analytic lower
constants $a_*/t_W(0)$ and $a_*/t_U(0)$ follow from
NW17 by exactly the same proof, independently of the
matrix ball computation.

For completeness, a sharper direct native expression is
also exact at each certified pole:
\[
 \mathbb W_\omega(v,v)\ge
 \frac{\det T_3(\omega)}{\tr(\operatorname{adj}(T_3(\omega))H_J)}
                         \|[v]\|_{Q,J}^2,
 \qquad J=W\text{ or }U.
 \tag{NW22}
\]
The denominator is strictly positive. To prove it, let
$A=H_J^{1/2}T_3^{-1}H_J^{1/2}>0$. Then
$\lambda_{\max}(A)\le\tr A$
$=\tr(T_3^{-1}H_J)$; inversion gives NW22.
Its numerator and denominator are finite determinant and
cofactor expressions in the actual certified Toeplitz
entries and the full original matrices, so NW22 can be
enclosed directly without introducing a spectral norm
parameter. NW21 already supplies explicit rational and
original-moment bounds without requiring this refinement.

\section{The cubic Gaussian counterpart in both original norms}
The provider also proves the complete Gaussian inequality
WP14. To retain its exact meaning, put
\[
 \mathcal A(s)=\tfrac12s(s-1)\pi^{-s/2}\Gamma(s/2),\quad
 \phi_*(x)=(4\pi^2x^4-6\pi x^2)e^{-\pi x^2},\quad
 D_x=-x\,d/dx.
 \tag{NW23}
\]
For $r(s)=\sum_{j=0}^3r_js^j$, its actual test function
$r(D_x)\phi_*$ has Mellin transform $\mathcal A(s)r(s)$
on $\Re s>-2$, by WP1--2 including their boundary-term
proof. WP14 gives, for this full cubic source space,
\[
 \W(\mathcal A r,\mathcal A r)\ge e^{-300}\sum_j|r_j|^2.
 \tag{NW24}
\]
For our full eight-state evaluation, its exact polynomial
is $r(s)=(\Psi z)(s+\kappa)$ and its source is therefore
$[\Psi z](D_x+\kappa)\phi_*$. This retains the original
center translation and every entry of $\Psi$, as in WP15--16.

The coefficient conversion from $r(s)$ to its value on
$s=1/2+iy$ is exactly
\[
 D_G=\begin{pmatrix}
 1&1/2&1/4&1/8\\0&i&i&3i/4\\0&0&-1&-3/2\\0&0&0&-i
 \end{pmatrix},\qquad
 Z_G=D_GD_G^*=
 \begin{pmatrix}
 85/64&-27i/32&-7/16&i/8\\
 27i/32&41/16&-17i/8&-3/4\\
 -7/16&17i/8&13/4&-3i/2\\
 -i/8&-3/4&3i/2&1
 \end{pmatrix}.
 \tag{NW25}
\]
The original target monomial Gram in $y$ is
$K=(m_{p+q})_{0\le p,q\le3}$ with the moments NW10;
the source one is $K_U=MF^*\diag(k_j)F$ from NW14.
The Gaussian coefficient Grams in the two original
arithmetic norms are exactly $J_W=D_G^*KD_G$ and
$J_U=D_G^*K_UD_G$. Their evaluated traces are
\[
\boxed{\begin{aligned}
 t_{G,W}&=M(85/64+339b/16+141b^2/4+15b^3),\\
 t_{G,U}&=M\sum_{r=0}^3k_r\sum_{p,q=0}^3
                         F_{rp}\overline{F_{rq}}(Z_G)_{pq}.
\end{aligned}}\tag{NW26}
\]
For the first identity, integrate
$\sum_{j=0}^3|1/2+iy|^{2j}$ using the full moments NW10;
for the second, multiply the explicitly displayed matrices.
Both traces are strictly positive, and no Gaussian
$L^2$ norm has been substituted for the original conductor
norm. The two original centers have been transported
through the exact $s=S'-\kappa$ dictionary.

The Gaussian full eight-state form has exactly the same
four-dimensional kernel NW6: its coefficient receiver
$C_\kappa\Psi$ is invertible and NW24 is strictly positive.
For a Gaussian coefficient column $r$, the actual
orthogonal minimum lift in either original norm is
\[
 \ell_G(r)=\iota_-O^{-1}\Psi^{-1}C_{-\kappa}r.
 \tag{NW27}
\]
Its target is the polynomial $r(S'-\kappa)$ and its
source is $T_A^{-1}r(S'-\kappa)$, with inverse coefficients
NW13--14. The same minimization argument as NW8 proves
that its quotient squared norms are $r^*J_Wr$ and
$r^*J_Ur$. Combining NW24 with the trace upper bounds
therefore gives the two unconditional original-native
Gaussian inequalities on every full signed state:
\[
\boxed{\quad
 \W(\mathcal A r,\mathcal A r)
 \ge\frac{e^{-300}}{t_{G,W}}\|[v]\|_{Q,W}^2,
 \qquad
 \W(\mathcal A r,\mathcal A r)
 \ge\frac{e^{-300}}{t_{G,U}}\|[v]\|_{Q,U}^2.
 \quad}\tag{NW28}
\]
These apply to the same original quotient spaces as NW21,
through the displayed Gaussian coefficient receiver; the
rational and Gaussian coefficient columns are not
identified with one another.

\section{Exact source use and independent checks}

reads the existing provider certificate as immutable data.
Using exact rational arithmetic on each displayed ball's
decimal midpoint and radius, it proves the outward
enclosures NW18 and their rational $a_\tau$. It does not
rerun Arb, compute zeta zeros, or repeat the provider's
Taylor-series calculation. Separately, it symbolically
verifies every entry of NW15 and NW25, the fully evaluated
traces NW11 and NW26, and both source trace identities
from the complete FC24 matrix NW14. Its receipt is
\texttt{native\_weil\_coercivity\_exact.json}.
The provider's exact file identities are
\[
\begin{array}{ll}
 \texttt{WEIL\_POSITIVITY\_AND\_ARITHMETIC\_CERTIFICATES.tex}:&
 \texttt{ffe3f0cd195a3a2652f3d47540cd4ce9bfd8cb730cab29223d730b145a2ba73f},\\
 \texttt{ARITHMETIC\_BALL\_CERTIFICATES.json}:&
 \texttt{9c3ae36dc2f03fa42b2d54c8f5a30802fcc0f49b4a2bd9d99bc598b6151a12f7}.
\end{array}
\]
The four-zero proof uses Platt--Trudgian's verified height,

primary paper.

\section{Certified arithmetic eigenlines in the original target norm}
The five-pole calculation in \cite[WP24--27]{WeilCertificates}
encloses the complete Weil coefficient matrices. Here the receiving
calculation encloses all four generalized eigenvalues in the
unchanged original target norm, constructs their exact eigenlines
in the original eight signed coordinates, and proves that no
additional kernel is hidden at any of these poles.

Fix the original permitted Gamma parameter $s=1$, so
$b=1/2$, $M=\sqrt{2\pi}$, and retain the full original target
center $c_*$ through $S'=z+c_*-1/2$. At
\[
 \omega=2+i\tau,\qquad \tau\in\{0,14,21,100,1000\},\qquad
 h=3,\quad d=3/2,
 \tag{NV1}
\]
let $Q(\tau)$ be exactly NH6 and $K$ exactly NH7. The actual
positive target Gram and arithmetic Gram are
\[
 H_\omega=3Q(\tau)^*KQ(\tau),\quad
 T_\omega=(c_{j-i}(\omega))_{0\le i,j\le3},\quad
 \det H_\omega=12\cdot3^{16}M^4b^3(b+1)^2(b+2).
 \tag{NV2}
\]
Thus every original correlation and the original total mass remain.
The entries $c_j$ are the full zero sums RW12--13. Their
finite evaluation uses the proved exact generating identity
\[
 m_\omega(u)=\frac{\omega+(1-\bar\omega)u}{1+u},\qquad
 X_\omega(u)=\xi(m_\omega(u)),\qquad
 \frac{X_\omega'(u)}{X_\omega(u)m_\omega'(u)}
 =\frac{\xi'}{\xi}(m_\omega(u)).
 \tag{NV3}
\]
For $j=1,2,3$, $c_j$ is its coefficient of $u^j$; the
diagonal is twice the real part of its constant term.
The exact derivation, including the zero sum and all
Hadamard constants, is \cite[MP6--15]{MovableWeil}.
Both divisors in NV3 have nonzero constant term:
$\xi(\omega)\ne0$ on $\Re\omega>1$ and
$m_\omega'(0)=-3$. Formal series division is therefore
legitimate. The script retains eight coefficients before
differentiation, more than needed for every displayed entry.
No truncation of a zero, prime or Gamma sum is used to
replace these completed-function values.

Here is the complete real-root certification argument,
so a floating-point eigenvalue is not being used as a sign
test. For real $x$ set
\[
 A_\omega(x)=T_\omega-xH_\omega,\qquad
 \Delta_0(x)=1,\quad
 \Delta_j(x)=\det A_\omega(x)_{\{0,\ldots,j-1\}},
 \quad1\le j\le4.
 \tag{NV4}
\]
At each rational endpoint used below the certificate proves
all four $\Delta_j$ real and nonzero, with strict signs.
Gaussian elimination without a row exchange gives
$A=L\,\diag(p_1,\ldots,p_4)L^*$, where $L$ is invertible
unit lower triangular and $p_j=\Delta_j/\Delta_{j-1}$.
For completeness, at the first step $p_1=A_{00}\ne0$;
subtracting its column multiples yields its Schur complement.
Its first $r$ leading determinant is
$\Delta_{r+1}/p_1$, by the block determinant identity.
Induction proves every pivot and the factorization.
Hence the number of negative eigenvalues of $A$ equals
the number of sign changes in
$(1,\Delta_1,\ldots,\Delta_4)$, since multiplication by
$L^*$ is a bijection preserving the maximal dimensions
of positive and negative quadratic subspaces.

The positive matrix $H_\omega$ has an invertible positive
square root. Congruence by $H_\omega^{-1/2}$ identifies
$A_\omega(x)$ with $H_\omega^{-1/2}T_\omega H_\omega^{-1/2}-xI$.
Its negative inertia is therefore precisely the number
of original-native generalized eigenvalues less than $x$.
The certificate provides four disjoint rational intervals
$(l_j,u_j)$ with
\[
 0<l_1<u_1<l_2<u_2<l_3<u_3<l_4<u_4,\qquad
 \operatorname{neg}A_\omega(l_j)=j-1,\quad
 \operatorname{neg}A_\omega(u_j)=j.
 \tag{NV5}
\]
Every interval consequently contains exactly one eigenvalue
with multiplicity. There are four in total, so all have
been enclosed, are positive and are simple.
The exact rational endpoints, all forty endpoint inertias,
all $160$ leading-minor balls, and every original matrix
entry are retained in
\texttt{ORIGINAL\_NATIVE\_WEIL\_CERTIFICATES.json}.
Each interval has relative width less than $10^{-60}$.

The following rounded numbers only identify the certified
intervals; the rational endpoints and strict signs are
the evidence:
\[
\begin{array}{c|rrrr}
 \tau&\lambda_1&\lambda_2&\lambda_3&\lambda_4\\\hline
 0&8.14745\,10^{-11}&1.31906\,10^{-7}&1.90271\,10^{-5}&1.59784\,10^{-3}\\
 14&8.28289\,10^{-11}&3.32645\,10^{-7}&2.28517\,10^{-3}&1.07200\,10^{4}\\
 21&2.49471\,10^{-10}&8.59801\,10^{-7}&3.53539\,10^{-2}&1.19546\,10^{5}\\
 100&8.86137\,10^{-14}&5.05459\,10^{-7}&4.05208\,10^{1}&3.46066\,10^{8}\\
 1000&1.54757\,10^{-19}&3.26961\,10^{-8}&4.69894\,10^{3}&1.63176\,10^{15}
\end{array}.
\tag{NV6}
\]
The calculation uses python-flint 0.9.0, Arb at $768$ bits,
and one computational worker. The original interval-arithmetic
source is Johansson \cite{JohanssonArb}; exact algorithms,
script hash and complete enclosures are supplied. Every
determinant's imaginary ball contains zero, consistent
with the already proved exact Hermitian symmetry. A ball
containing zero in its real part never passes a sign test.

The eigenlines are also explicitly constructed, rather than
left as unnamed vectors. Let $\lambda_j$ be the unique
real root of $\det A_\omega(x)$ in the certified interval
NV5. For each one, the certificate selects an index
$k_j\in\{0,1,2,3\}$ and proves the principal cofactor
$(\operatorname{adj}A_\omega(x))_{k_jk_j}$ nonzero
throughout that entire interval. It evaluates the same
cofactor by interval arithmetic with an enclosure of
the whole rational interval. Consequently
\[
 d_j=\operatorname{adj}(T_\omega-\lambda_jH_\omega)e_{k_j}
 \ne0,\qquad
 T_\omega d_j=\lambda_jH_\omega d_j.
 \tag{NV7}
\]
Every coordinate of $d_j$ is the specified signed
three-by-three determinant, hence a polynomial of degree
at most three in the exactly defined $\lambda_j$.
The adjugate identity proves the eigenvector equation;
the certified diagonal cofactor proves nonvanishing.
Simplicity proves that its line is the complete eigenspace.

Retain the eight signed vectors and the exact factors $1/2$
in SE2. The original eight-state eigenvector and its two
physical components are therefore
\[
 v_j=\iota_-O^{-1}B_\omega^{-1}d_j
   =\frac12\sum_{\ell=1}^4
     (O^{-1}B_\omega^{-1}d_j)_\ell(f_{\ell,+}-f_{\ell,-}),
 \quad
 \mathcal J_Wv_j=(0,C_{-\kappa}R_\omega d_j),\quad
 \mathcal J_Uv_j=(0,T_A^{-1}C_{-\kappa}R_\omega d_j).
 \tag{NV8}
\]
No sign state is identified with another in this map.
RW15--20 show that $v_j$ is an eigenvector of the
actual target-native pencil
$\mathbb Wv=\lambda\mathbb H_Wv$.
The four remaining eigenvectors have eigenvalue zero and are
\[
 g_\ell=\iota_+e_\ell-\iota_-O^{-1}Ee_\ell,\qquad
 1\le\ell\le4.
 \tag{NV9}
\]
Indeed their evaluations vanish and their label coordinates
are independent; NV5 excludes any further kernel.
For $j\ne k$, Hermitian symmetry in NV7 gives
$(\lambda_j-\lambda_k)d_k^*H_\omega d_j=0$, proving
orthogonality in the original target norm. All $g_\ell$
are orthogonal to all $v_j$ in that same norm by RW16.
The four graph vectors retain their full label Gram $G_Y$,
so no Euclidean label norm is silently substituted.

The numerical eigenvalues in NV6 concern the actual target
metric with the stated permitted $s=1$. The original
source metric is separately and completely evaluated in
NH26--42 and the native coercivity calculation. Its
conductor moments are not assigned numerical values here.
The symbolic exact source vector in NV8 nevertheless exists
for every original conductor on its established domain,
using the actual FC24 inverse. Thus each certified
arithmetic eigenline has a fully constructed return
through the original Fable and conductor maps.


\section{The finite completion and the exact residue return}
This note proves the finite completion proposed in EA1--28 of
\cite{ExceptionalAtlas}, retaining the original reciprocal factor
$t=1/a$, both infinity signs and all derivative constants.
The original polynomial and marked states remain attributed
to \cite{ES488}; the ordinary-fibre calculations and existing
nonfinite factor completion are recorded in \cite{Fable029}.
The finite completion below is a different, explicitly related
object. All schemes and algebras in this note are over $\C$.

\section{The original map and two completed root charts}
The original source coordinates are $(a,y,z,w)$. Define
\begin{align}
b&=i+ay,&c&=-i+2ay+a^2z,\nonumber\\
d&=-iy-a(iz+2y^2)-a^2yz,&e&=2z-7iy^2+aw,\nonumber\\
f&=iw+3iy^3-4yz+a(6iy^2z+wy+4y^4)+2a^2y^3z,\nonumber\\
P&=(ac,ae+bd,af+be,bf)=(A,B,C,D).
\tag{FS1}
\end{align}
Direct multiplication gives $ad+bc=1$ and
$a^3f-a^2be+ab^2d-b^3c=1$. Thus
$(aU+bV)(cU^3+dU^2V+eUV^2+fV^3)=H_u(U,V)$, where
\[
H_u(U,V)=AU^4+U^3V+BU^2V^2+CUV^3+DV^4,\quad
h_u(r)=Ar^4+r^3+Br^2+Cr+D,\quad
k_u(v)=A+v+Bv^2+Cv^3+Dv^4 .
\]
The coefficient of $U^3V$ remains one throughout.
For $a\ne0$, division by the linear factor gives
$r=-b/a=-y-i/a$ and $a^2h_u'(r)=1$.
Keeping the reciprocal factor as the actual new coordinate,
define
\begin{equation}
X_f=\Spec\frac{\C[A,B,C,D,r,t]}{(h_u(r),\,t^2-h_u'(r))},
\qquad
X_\infty=\Spec\frac{\C[A,B,C,D,v,\theta]}
 {(k_u(v),\,\theta^2+k_u'(v))}.
\tag{FS2}
\end{equation}
On $r\ne0$ and $v\ne0$ the exact gluing maps and inverses are
\begin{equation}
v=r^{-1},\quad\theta=-t/r,\qquad
r=v^{-1},\quad t=-\theta/v .
\tag{FS3}
\end{equation}
At a root, differentiating $k(v)=v^4h(1/v)$ gives
$k'(v)=-v^2h'(1/v)$. Hence FS3 preserves both equations
and signs, and direct substitution proves the inverse
compositions. Glue these charts to form $X$ with projection
$\pi:X\to\mathbb A^4_u$. No coordinate $t$ or $\theta$ is
inverted in defining the completed charts.

The equations on the finite chart solve as
\begin{equation}
C=t^2-4Ar^3-3r^2-2Br,\qquad
D=3Ar^4+2r^3+Br^2-rt^2.
\tag{FS4}
\end{equation}
Substitution in both directions proves
$X_f=\mathbb A^4_{t,r,A,B}$, including $A=0$ and $t=0$.
At $v=0$, the other chart has exactly
\begin{equation}
A=0,\quad\theta=\pm i,\qquad
D_\pm=\{v=0,\ \theta=\mp i\}\simeq\mathbb A^3_{B,C,D}.
\tag{FS5}
\end{equation}
Thus $D_+$ is the included original sign $\theta=-i$ and
$D_-$ is the missing original sign $\theta=i$.
Eliminating $A$ on $X_\infty$ leaves the equation
$1+\theta^2+2Bv+3Cv^2+4Dv^3=0$.
At $v=0$ its derivative in $\theta$ is $2\theta\ne0$.
Therefore $X$ is smooth there, and it is smooth elsewhere
by FS3--4.

\section{Five explicit finite free descriptions over the target}
For $p\in\{0,1,-1,2\}$ write $c_p=H_u(p,1)=h_u(p)$.
The following identity is literal coefficient arithmetic:
\begin{equation}
1=\tfrac12h_u(0)-\tfrac12h_u(1)-\tfrac16h_u(-1)
                       +\tfrac16h_u(2)-2A .
\tag{FS6}
\end{equation}
It is verified by comparing each coefficient of $A,B,C,D$
and the fixed cubic term. Consequently the five principal
opens $D(A),D(c_0),D(c_1),D(c_{-1}),D(c_2)$ cover the
entire characteristic-zero target.

On $D(c_p)$ use the determinant-one substitution
$(U,V)=(pU'-V',U')$ and put
$h_p(R)=H_u(pR-1,R)$. Its leading coefficient is precisely
the unit $c_p$. The complete root/sign transitions are
\begin{align}
R&=(p-r)^{-1},&T&=t/(p-r)
                    &&\text{on the finite overlap},\nonumber\\
R&=-v/(1-pv),&T&=\theta/(1-pv)
                    &&\text{on the infinity overlap},\nonumber\\
r&=p-R^{-1},&t&=T/R&& (R\ne0),\nonumber\\
v&=R/(pR-1),&\theta&=T/(1-pR)&& (pR-1\ne0).
\tag{FS7}
\end{align}
Every denominator is an explicitly stated unit on the relevant
overlap. On $D(c_p)$ the marked root cannot equal $p$;
thus $p-r$ is a unit on its finite root scheme. More
algebraically $h_u(p)-h_u(r)$ is divisible by $p-r$,
and $h_u(r)=0$, so the unit $c_p$ proves this assertion.
Likewise the infinity denominator $1-pv$ cannot vanish
there. The inverse opens cover the new chart because $R$
and $pR-1$ generate the unit ideal.

The derivative identity is
$h_p'(R)=R^2h_u'(p-R^{-1})$ at a root, obtained by
differentiating $h_p(R)=R^4h_u(p-R^{-1})$.
Thus $T^2=h_p'(R)$ on both overlaps; at $v=0$, $R=0$
and $T=\theta$, so the infinity sign is preserved exactly.
FS7 and its inverse formulas give an isomorphism of the
whole preimage over $D(c_p)$ with
\begin{equation}
\Spec\,\mathcal O_{D(c_p)}[R,T]/
                 (h_p(R),\,T^2-h_p'(R)).
\tag{FS8}
\end{equation}
The same description on $D(A)$ is the original finite
chart FS2 with the additional unit $A$; all marked roots
there are finite. In either case, writing $\lambda$ for
the retained leading coefficient, division by the unit
$\lambda$ makes only the root relation monic.
The sign relation is still exactly $T^2=h_p'(R)$,
not the derivative of the divided polynomial.
The successive monic relations give the free module basis
\begin{equation}
1,R,R^2,R^3,\ T,TR,TR^2,TR^3 .
\tag{FS9}
\end{equation}
Indeed the root quotient is free on the first four monomials;
the monic quadratic relation in $T$ makes its extension
free on $1,T$. This proves on all five opens, without
any restriction on their discriminants, that
\begin{equation}
\pi:X\longrightarrow\mathbb A^4_u
\text{ is finite locally free of rank eight.}
\tag{FS10}
\end{equation}

The finite chart is irreducible. It is dense in $X$:
on the infinity chart the remaining equation is monic
in $\theta$, so its coordinate algebra is free over
$\C[v,B,C,D]$ and multiplication by $v$ is injective.
Thus no irreducible component is supported on $v=0$;
every such point is also smooth by FS5.
It follows that $X$ is irreducible and smooth, hence normal.
Since FS10 has rank eight and is dominant, its function
field is a degree-eight extension of $\C(A,B,C,D)$.
Finally, a finite normal integral scheme over an affine
base is the integral closure of that base in its function
field: any element integral over the base is also integral
over its finite coordinate ring, so normality includes it.
This proves that $X$ is the finite normalization in the
actual original degree-eight function-field extension.

\section{The original open immersion, including its infinity sheet}
The full resultant-one factor space is
$\mathcal F=\{ad+bc=1,\ a^3f-a^2be+ab^2d-b^3c=1\}$.
Its exact identification with the unramified part of $X$
is given on the finite chart by
\begin{align}
a&=t^{-1},&b&=-r/t,&c&=At,&d&=(1+Ar)t,\nonumber\\
e&=(B+r+Ar^2)t,&f&=(C+Br+r^2+Ar^3)t&& (t\ne0),
\tag{FS11}
\end{align}
and on the infinity chart by
\begin{align}
a&=-v/\theta,&b&=\theta^{-1},&
c&=\theta(1+Bv+Cv^2+Dv^3),\nonumber\\
d&=\theta(B+Cv+Dv^2),&
e&=\theta(C+Dv),&f&=\theta D
\qquad(\theta\ne0).
\tag{FS12}
\end{align}
Multiplication of the factors gives $H_u$ in either formula.
The resultant is $t^{-2}h'_u(r)=1$ in FS11 and
$-\theta^{-2}k'_u(v)=1$ in FS12.
Their inverse is $(r,t)=(-b/a,1/a)$ when $a\ne0$ and
$(v,\theta)=(-a/b,1/b)$ when $b\ne0$.
Since $ad+bc=1$, these inverse charts cover $\mathcal F$.
Direct substitution and FS3 prove every inverse composition.
The excluded locus is the reduced divisor
$R_0=\{t=0\}\simeq\mathbb A^3_{r,A,B}$; on the infinity
chart it is $\theta=0$, which never meets $v=0$.
Thus $\mathcal F\simeq X\setminus R_0$ exactly.

The original source maps to $X$ via FS1 and these inverse
factor charts. For $a\ne0$ its complete inverse is
\begin{equation}
q(t,r,A,B)=\left(t^{-1},-r-it,\,
At^3+2rt+3it^2,\,
7ir^2t+(B-17r+Ar^2)t^2-13it^3-2At^4\right).
\tag{FS13}
\end{equation}
All terms come from the successive original factor equations;
substitution verifies $Pq=\pi$.
At $a=0$ its factors have $b=i$, hence $\theta=-i$.
It has exactly the original state
$q_\infty=(0,B,i(7B^2-C)/2,11B^3-2BC-D)$ over
every $A=0$ target.

For an explicit inverse on an open neighborhood of that
entire infinity sheet, use $b(b+i)\ne0$ in $\mathcal F$
and set
\begin{align}
Y&=\frac{a^2f-abe+2b^2d}{b+i},\nonumber\\
Z&=\frac{af-be+7Y^2-10iaY^3-4a^2Y^4}{2b^3},\nonumber\\
W&=\frac{a^2eY^3+7ia^2Y^5+3iaeY^2-17aY^4
                       -2eY-f-11iY^3}{b^3}.
\tag{FS14}
\end{align}
No factor $a$ is divided out in FS14. The original two
factor equations give
$b^2+1=a(a^2f-abe+2b^2d)$, so $b-i=aY$.
Substitution of FS1 gives the three polynomial identities
\[
a^2f-abe+2b^2d=(b+i)y,\qquad
af-be+7y^2-10iay^3-4a^2y^4=2b^3z,
\]
\[
a^2ey^3+7ia^2y^5+3iaey^2-17ay^4-2ey-f-11iy^3=b^3w.
\]
These identities follow by expanding the displayed factor
polynomials and prove that FS14 is the inverse on the
dense subopen $a\ne0$. Both it and FS1 are regular on
the whole stated open set, which is reduced as an open
subset of the smooth irreducible $X$. Equality of the
compositions there follows from equality on that dense
open. This proves the inverse also at $a=0$.
The opens $a\ne0$ and $b(b+i)\ne0$ cover precisely the
original image in $\mathcal F$: at $a=0$ the resultant
forces $b=\pm i$, and the omitted case is $b=-i$.
We have therefore proved, with complete coordinate maps,
\begin{equation}
\mathbb A^4_q\simeq X\setminus(R_0\cup D_-),\qquad
R_0\cap D_-=\varnothing,\qquad
R_0\simeq D_-\simeq\mathbb A^3 .
\tag{FS15}
\end{equation}
It is an open immersion, rather than an assertion that
$t=1/a$ is an invertible change at its new boundary.

\section{The different, branch divisor and full trace discriminant}
In the original column order $(t,r,A,B)$ and target order
$(A,B,C,D)$, differentiating FS4 and FS13 gives
\begin{equation}
\det D\pi=-2t^3,\qquad
\det Dq=t^3\quad(t\ne0),\qquad
\det DP=-2.
\tag{FS16}
\end{equation}
For the first determinant, the remaining $(t,r)$ minor
after the $(A,B)$ rows is
$\begin{psmallmatrix}2t&-\kappa\\-2rt&r\kappa-t^2\end{psmallmatrix}$,
where $\kappa=12Ar^2+6r+2B$; it has determinant $-2t^3$.
For the second, the triangular pivots of the source
parameterization are $-t^{-2},-1,t^3,t^2$, whose product
is $t^3$. The chain rule proves the last identity where
$a\ne0$, and polynomial equality extends it to all original
source points.

The reduced ramification support is $R_0$; the actual
different (or ramification Cartier divisor) is
\begin{equation}
\operatorname{Fitt}_0\Omega_{X/\mathbb A^4}
=(t^3)\quad\hbox{on }X_f,\qquad
\operatorname{Ram}(\pi)=3R_0 .
\tag{FS17}
\end{equation}
Indeed both source and target are smooth of dimension four,
so their differential presentation has determinant FS16.
Alternatively the relations in FS2 have relative Jacobian
in variables $(r,t)$ equal to
$\begin{psmallmatrix}h'(r)&0\\-h''(r)&2t\end{psmallmatrix}$,
whose determinant is $2t^3$ on $X_f$.
At an infinity point, the relative derivative matrix of
$(k(v),\theta^2+k'(v))$ in $(v,\theta)$ has determinant
$2\theta k'(0)=2\theta\ne0$.
Thus no ramification component is hidden at infinity.
The changes FS7 transport the determinant by units and
give FS17 globally.

Define the exact binary quartic discriminant $\mathfrak D$
by its polynomial extension from
$\mathfrak D=A^6\prod_{i<j}(r_j-r_i)^2$ for $A\ne0$.
At $A=0$ its specialization is exactly
\begin{equation}
\mathfrak D(0,B,C,D)
=B^2C^2-4C^3-4B^3D+18BCD-27D^2.
\tag{FS18}
\end{equation}
One way to verify both the polynomial extension and this
constant is the determinant of the Sylvester matrix of
$h,h'$ divided by $A$ on $A\ne0$, then expansion and
evaluation at zero. FS18 is the ordinary discriminant of
the monic cubic; the projective infinity root is simple.
Thus the reduced branch locus is exactly
$\{\mathfrak D=0\}$, the image of $R_0$.
This also identifies the locus where every root chart of
the completed cover is etale; no $A=0$ component is added.

In a free root/sign chart with polynomial leading coefficient
$\lambda$ and basis FS9, let $M_R$ be multiplication by $R$
in the degree-four root algebra. Define
$T_{ij}=\Tr(M_R^{i+j})$ and
$T'_{ij}=\Tr(M_{h_p'}M_R^{i+j})$, $0\le i,j\le3$.
The full bilinear trace Gram in the unaltered basis is
\begin{equation}
\left(\Tr_{X/u}(e_ie_j)\right)_{i,j=1}^{8}
=\begin{pmatrix}2T&0\\0&2T'\end{pmatrix},\qquad
\det\left(\Tr_{X/u}(e_ie_j)\right)
=256\,\frac{\mathfrak D^3}{\lambda^{14}} .
\tag{FS19}
\end{equation}
The algebra is the direct sum of its root module and $T$
times that module. Multiplication by a root-algebra element
acts identically on these two summands, so its trace is twice
the root trace. Odd multiplication has trace zero (also by
conjugating with the sign involution), and the product of
two odd factors is exactly $h_p'$ times their root product.
This proves the block formula even on nonreduced fibres.

For distinct roots, write
$\Delta=\prod_{i<j}(R_j-R_i)$ and
$V=(R_j^i)_{0\le i\le3,1\le j\le4}$.
Then $T=VV^{\mathsf T}$ and
$T'=V\diag(h_p'(R_j))V^{\mathsf T}$, so
$\det T=\Delta^2$, $\det T'=\lambda^4\Delta^4$:
the six pair-sign reversals give
$\prod_j h_p'(R_j)=\lambda^4\Delta^2$.
The determinant of the full blocks is consequently
$2^8\lambda^4\Delta^6
=256(\lambda^6\Delta^2)^3/\lambda^{14}$, as asserted.
Both sides are regular coefficient functions on this free
chart; equality on the dense distinct-root locus proves
equality on every fibre.

The binary discriminant remains unchanged under the
determinant-one substitutions used above. Explicitly,
$c_p=A\prod_j(p-r_j)$ and
$R_j-R_i=(r_j-r_i)/[(p-r_j)(p-r_i)]$, so the factors
in $c_p^6\prod_{i<j}(R_j-R_i)^2$ cancel exactly to
$A^6\prod_{i<j}(r_j-r_i)^2$. The identity extends to all
coefficient points. Thus FS19 on $D(A)$ has denominator
$A^{14}$, and on $D(c_p)$ it has denominator $c_p^{14}$.
Since those denominators are units only on their explicitly
stated opens, the complete global discriminant ideal is
\begin{equation}
\operatorname{disc}(\pi)=(\mathfrak D^3).
\tag{FS20}
\end{equation}
In particular $A^{-14}$ in the first basis is not an
additional ramification divisor. At $A=0$ choose any
$p\in\{0,1,-1,2\}$ with $c_p\ne0$, which FS6 guarantees;
FS19 then evaluates the exact trace determinant with its
full constant $256$ and no singular denominator.

\section{Every nonreduced fibre in the original reciprocal coordinate}
Fix a target $u$. At a finite root $r_0$ of exact multiplicity
$m$ put $\epsilon=r-r_0$ and factor the actual polynomial as
$h_u(r_0+\epsilon)=\epsilon^m g(\epsilon)$ with $g(0)\ne0$.
The root algebra local at $r_0$ is exactly
$\C[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^m)$: all other root factors are
units there. In this algebra
$h'_u(r_0+\epsilon)=m g(0)\epsilon^{m-1}$.
Thus the completed local factor, with its original $t$,
is
\begin{equation}
\mathcal A_{m,c}
=\C[\epsilon,t]/(\epsilon^m,\ t^2-c\epsilon^{m-1}),
\qquad c=m g(0)\ne0 .
\tag{FS21}
\end{equation}
No leading coefficient has been absorbed into $t$.
The two successive monic relations give the exact basis
and multiplication
\begin{align}
\mathcal B_m&=(1,\epsilon,\ldots,\epsilon^{m-1},
              t,t\epsilon,\ldots,t\epsilon^{m-1}),\nonumber\\
\epsilon^i\epsilon^j&=\epsilon^{i+j},&
\epsilon^i(t\epsilon^j)&=t\epsilon^{i+j},&
(t\epsilon^i)(t\epsilon^j)&=c\epsilon^{m-1+i+j},
\tag{FS22}
\end{align}
with every exponent at least $m$ interpreted as zero.
These formulas specify every structure constant and prove
the length $2m$, including all nilpotents.
For $m=1$ the algebra is
$\C[t]/(t^2-c)=\C\oplus\C$, its two points being
$t=\pm\sqrt c$; these are the two original factor signs.
For $m=2$, the exact isomorphism without changing $t$ is
\begin{equation}
\mathcal A_{2,c}\simeq\C[t]/(t^4),\qquad
\epsilon\longmapsto t^2/c,\qquad
t\longmapsto t .
\tag{FS23}
\end{equation}
The inverse sends the class of $t$ to the actual reciprocal
factor. Its square and fourth power satisfy the two original
relations, proving both compositions.
For $m=3$ and $m=4$ the full original algebras remain
$\C[\epsilon,t]/(\epsilon^3,t^2-c\epsilon^2)$ and
$\C[\epsilon,t]/(\epsilon^4,t^2-c\epsilon^3)$.
If one wishes only to compare their abstract isomorphism
types, choose an actual square root $\lambda^2=c$ and send
the new symbol $s$ to $t/\lambda$; the inverse sends $t$
to $\lambda s$. This records the precise comparison to
the coefficient-one presentation and both choices of
$\lambda$; it does not replace the retained coordinate.

For completeness the local maximal-ideal filtrations are
also exact. For $m=2$ they are those of $(t)$ in
$\C[t]/t^4$, with dimensions $4,3,2,1,0$.
For $m=3,4$, put $\mathfrak n=(\epsilon,t)$. Direct
multiplication using FS22 gives
\begin{equation}
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
m&(\dim\mathcal A,\dim\mathfrak n,\dim\mathfrak n^2,\ldots)
 &\text{associated graded dimensions}&\operatorname{Soc}(\mathcal A)\\\hline
2&(4,3,2,1,0)&(1,1,1,1)&\C\,t\epsilon\\
3&(6,5,3,1,0)&(1,2,2,1)&\C\,t\epsilon^2\\
4&(8,7,5,3,1,0)&(1,2,2,2,1)&\C\,t\epsilon^3
\end{array}
\tag{FS24}
\end{equation}
For example at $m=4$ the bases of the successive nonzero
powers from the second onward are
$(\epsilon^2,\epsilon^3,t\epsilon,t\epsilon^2,t\epsilon^3)$,
$(\epsilon^3,t\epsilon^2,t\epsilon^3)$ and $(t\epsilon^3)$.
At $m=3$ they are $(\epsilon^2,t\epsilon,t\epsilon^2)$
and $(t\epsilon^2)$. The socle statements follow by
annihilating an arbitrary FS22 basis combination first by
$\epsilon$ and then by $t$.
Since multiplication by a nilpotent has trace zero,
for $m\ge2$ the local trace pairing is exactly
\begin{equation}
\Tr_{\mathcal A_{m,c}}(uv)=2m\,u(0,0)v(0,0),\qquad
\operatorname{rad}\Tr=\mathfrak n,\qquad
\operatorname{rank}\Tr=1 .
\tag{FS25}
\end{equation}
To see the trace identity directly, write a local element
as its constant plus a nilpotent in $\mathfrak n$; its
multiplication operator has that constant on all $2m$
diagonal entries after ordering any compatible filtration.
The reduced $m=1$ factor has its two nondegenerate scalar
trace pairings. Thus FS25 also specifies the complete
degenerate trace forms, not just their vanishing determinants.

The constants in FS21 have the following original-coordinate
expressions, with no unspecified unit:
\begin{equation}
c=\frac{h_u^{(m)}(r_0)}{(m-1)!},\qquad
c_2=12Ar_0^2+6r_0+2B,\quad
c_3=12Ar_0+3,\quad c_4=4A .
\tag{FS26}
\end{equation}
The expression for $m=1$ is just $c_1=h_u'(r_0)$.
Exact multiplicity means the stated constant is nonzero.
For example a cubic triple root has $c_3=3$.
The quadruple-root algebra is not $\C[t]/t^8$:
FS24 shows its embedding dimension is two, and FS22 gives
$t^4=c_4^2\epsilon^6=0$. Its length eight is retained by
the two generators and their full multiplication, not by
declaring the reciprocal factor to have nilpotency order eight.

The entire fibre is the product of these local factors,
by the Chinese remainder theorem in its root algebra.
When $A=0$ the finite-root part has total length six,
and the two infinity points FS5 contribute two reduced
copies of $\C$. The complete list is therefore
\begin{equation}
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c|c}
\text{degree}&\text{root multiplicities}&\text{full completed algebra}
 &\#\text{original points}&\operatorname{rank}\Tr\\\hline
4&1+1+1+1&\C^8&8&8\\
4&2+1+1&\mathcal A_{2,c}\times\C^4&4&5\\
4&2+2&\mathcal A_{2,c}\times\mathcal A_{2,c'}&0&2\\
4&3+1&\mathcal A_{3,c}\times\C^2&2&3\\
4&4&\mathcal A_{4,c}&0&1\\\hline
3&1+1+1&\C^6\times\C^2_\infty&7&8\\
3&2+1&\mathcal A_{2,c}\times\C^2\times\C^2_\infty&3&5\\
3&3&\mathcal A_{3,3}\times\C^2_\infty&1&3
\end{array}
\tag{FS27}
\end{equation}
Every algebra has length eight. Each displayed local
coefficient is the actual FS26 coefficient at that root;
in the $2+2$ case $c$ and $c'$ refer to its two different
roots and are both retained. The original open removes
the entire local factor of each multiple root, because
its $t$ is nilpotent and its principal open $D(t)$ is
empty. Of the two infinity factors it removes only
$\theta=i$. This proves every original count in FS27.
The trace ranks follow by summing FS25 and the simple
scalar trace ranks. Thus the table records both the
geometric points and their nonreduced local schemes.

\section{The omitted surface, ramification parametrization and deck map}
A quartic has no simple root precisely in the two
patterns $2+2$ and $4$. Its leading coefficient is
nonzero. With the unchanged cubic coefficient one,
being a scalar times a quadratic square is equivalent to
\begin{equation}
4AB-1-8A^2C=0,\qquad D-AC^2=0,\qquad
h_u(s)=A\left(s^2+\frac{s}{2A}+C\right)^2 .
\tag{FS28}
\end{equation}
Indeed write the monic quadratic as $s^2+ps+q$;
comparison of its cubic and linear coefficients gives
$p=1/(2A)$, $q=C$, and the other two give FS28.
The maps
$(A,C)\mapsto(A,(4A)^{-1}+2AC,C,AC^2)$ and
$(A,B,C,D)\mapsto(A,C)$ are inverse, with $A\ne0$.
This proves that the original omitted image is exactly
the smooth surface $\C^*\times\C$.
Its quadratic has a double root exactly when
$C=1/(16A^2)$. Thus its full quadruple curve is
\begin{equation}
B=\frac3{8A},\qquad C=\frac1{16A^2},\qquad
D=\frac1{256A^3},\qquad r_0=-\frac1{4A},\qquad A\ne0 .
\tag{FS29}
\end{equation}
Outside this curve FS28 has two length-four completed
points; on the curve it has the full two-generator
length-eight algebra in FS27.

On $R_0$ the projection is exactly FS4 at $t=0$.
Its differential in coordinates $(A,B,r)$ has its first
two rows equal to $(1,0,0)$ and $(0,1,0)$; the third
column in full target coordinates is
$(0,0,-\kappa,r\kappa)^{\mathsf T}$,
$\kappa=12Ar^2+6r+2B$.
It therefore has rank three exactly at roots of
multiplicity two, and rank two at roots of multiplicity
at least three. This proves the precise rank drop,
including cubic triples, without substituting the name
of the discriminant for its parametrization.

Both signs extend on the whole completed algebra as the
regular deck involution
\begin{equation}
(r,t)\mapsto(r,-t),\qquad
(v,\theta)\mapsto(v,-\theta).
\tag{FS30}
\end{equation}
FS3 makes the two formulas agree. Its fixed scheme is
exactly $R_0$, since characteristic zero makes $2$ a unit;
it exchanges $D_+$ and $D_-$. Its invariant algebra
in FS9 is precisely the degree-four root algebra, because
every element is uniquely $f(R)+Tg(R)$.
Thus its quotient is the universal projective root scheme
$\{H_u(U,V)=0\}\subset\mathbb A^4_u\times\mathbb P^1$,
including every nonreduced root scheme. The odd part
continues to multiply by the exact rule $T^2=h_p'(R)$;
it is not discarded at a fixed point.

Finally the exact nonproper-value locus of the original
affine map is
\begin{equation}
\mathcal N(P)=\pi(R_0\cup D_-)
            =\{\mathfrak D=0\}\cup\{A=0\}.
\tag{FS31}
\end{equation}
To prove the first containment, a finite morphism has
compact inverse images of compact complex sets: its
affine coordinate generators satisfy finitely many
monic relations, whose coefficient bounds bound every
root over a compact target. If a target lies outside
the images of the two removed closed divisors, a small
compact neighborhood has inverse image disjoint from
them. The regular inverse FS13--14 is bounded on that
compact preimage, excluding an escaping original
sequence there.

For the reverse containment, at any finite repeated root
fix $(r,A,B)$ and take $t=\varepsilon\ne0$ in FS4.
The target converges to the prescribed repeated-root
target, while FS13 has $a=1/\varepsilon\to\infty$.
For any $A=0$ target let
$q_\varepsilon=(\varepsilon,B,i(7B^2-C)/2,11B^3-2BC-D)$.
It tends to the included original point. The simultaneous
factor sign in the original coordinates for $a\ne0$ is
\[
\Sigma(a,y,z,w)=
(-a,\ y+2i/a,\ 6i/a^2-z,\
 w-14iy^2/a+28y/a^2+40i/a^3).
\]
Substitution into FS1 negates every factor and therefore
preserves $P$. The second coordinate of
$\Sigma q_\varepsilon$ escapes as $2i/\varepsilon$,
while its image tends to the stated $A=0$ target.
This proves every point of the second divisor image is
nonproper, including its discriminant intersections.

The exact verifier
\texttt{verify\_finite\_signed\_completion.py} checks the
five-open identity, all coordinate-transition signs,
both Jacobians, the original-chart inverse identities,
the trace-discriminant constants and every local
algebra filtration and trace pairing in FS21--27.
No numerical fibre sampling replaces those algebra identities.

The received exceptional atlas \cite{ExceptionalAtlas}, EA29--36, specifies
constant-term unfoldings and claims their original-metric singular
asymptotes. This note verifies those claims, derives the complete
forward and inverse compound limits with their complex phases,
evaluates every multiplicity-dependent constant, and determines
the original singular flags. The polynomial is the retained
four-dimensional ES construction \cite{ES488}; its existing complete
inverse is GF7. Every metric below is the fixed original Gamma
metric transported by the existing \(\Psi\), with no change to
the period or the conductor as the unfolding parameter varies.

\section{The entire original differential and its inverse}
Use the original source coordinates \((a,y,z,w)\), and output
order \((A,B,C,D)\). The polynomial is
\[
\begin{aligned}
P_0&=a^3z+2a^2y-ia,\\
P_2&=-a^3y^2z-2ia^2yz+a^2w-2a^2y^3-10iay^2+3az+y,\\
P_3&=2a^3y^3z+6ia^2y^2z+2a^2wy+4a^2y^4
       +2iaw-4iay^3-2ayz+2iz+7y^2,\\
P_4&=2a^3y^4z+8ia^2y^3z+a^2wy^2+4a^2y^5
       +2iawy+7iay^4-10ay^2z-4iyz-w-3y^3.
\end{aligned}\tag{FD1}
\]
For \(t\ne0\), put
\[
\begin{aligned}
q(t,r,A,B)=\bigl(&t^{-1},-r-it,At^3+2rt+3it^2,\\
 &7ir^2t+(B-17r+Ar^2)t^2-13it^3-2At^4\bigr)^T,\\
\pi(t,r,A,B)=\bigl(&A,B,t^2-4Ar^3-3r^2-2Br,\,
                      3Ar^4+2r^3+Br^2-rt^2\bigr)^T.
\end{aligned}\tag{FD2}
\]
Substitution into FD1 proves \(Pq=\pi\). This is the complete
finite simple-root inverse, with \(t=1/a\), rather than a
replacement source coordinate at \(t=0\). With
\[
 h_u(s)=As^4+s^3+Bs^2+Cs+D,\quad
 \ell_r=(r^4,r^2,r,1),\quad \ell'_r=(4r^3,2r,1,0),\quad
 \kappa=12Ar^2+6r+2B,
 \tag{FD3}
\]
its equations are \(h_u(r)=0\), \(t^2=h_u'(r)\).
Differentiating these two equations in all four output directions
gives exactly
\[
 dr=-\ell_r\,du/t^2,\qquad
 dt=\ell'_r\,du/(2t)-\kappa\ell_r\,du/(2t^3).
 \tag{FD4}
\]
Here and throughout, \(\ell_r\) is the complex-linear row
functional with the displayed coefficients, not their conjugates.
The differential of the original coordinate vector in FD2 is
\[
\begin{aligned}
 da&=-dt/t^2,&dy&=-dr-i\,dt,\\
 dz&=t^3dA+2t\,dr+(3At^2+2r+6it)dt,\\
 dw&=(r^2t^2-2t^4)dA+t^2dB
       +[14irt+(-17+2Ar)t^2]dr\\
   &\hspace{13mm}
       +[7ir^2+2(B-17r+Ar^2)t-39it^2-8At^3]dt.
\end{aligned}\tag{FD5}
\]
FD4 inserted into FD5 is the full \(4\)-by-\(4\) matrix
\(J^{-1}=DP(q)^{-1}\), in the original coordinate orders.
In particular it includes every \(dA,dB,dC,dD\) term.
An equivalent explicit outer-product formula is
\[
 J^{-1}=q_t\left(\frac{\ell'_r}{2t}
                    -\frac{\kappa\ell_r}{2t^3}\right)
          -\frac{q_r\ell_r}{t^2}+q_Ae_A^*+q_Be_B^*,
 \tag{FD6}
\]
where every column \(q_t,q_r,q_A,q_B\) is specified by FD5.
Thus FD6 has no unevaluated matrix inverse.

For completeness, the entire forward Jacobian also has a compact
column expression retaining all lower terms. Put
\[
\begin{aligned}
v(r)&=(1,-r^2,-2r^3,2r^4)^T,&
w(r)&=(0,1,-2r,r^2)^T,\\
R_0&=(4i,3ir^2,-22ir^3,15ir^4)^T,\\
R_1&=(3A,-Ar^2+2B-16r,
                  -10Ar^3-4Br+32r^2,8Ar^4+2Br^2-16r^3)^T,\\
R_2&=(0,-2iAr-14i,-8iAr^2-2iB+22ir,
                                10iAr^3+2iBr-8ir^2)^T,\\
R_3&=(0,-2A,4Ar-2,-2Ar^2+2r)^T,\\
S_0&=(0,2Ar-13,8Ar^2+2B+32r,
                              -10Ar^3-2Br-19r^2)^T.
\end{aligned}
\]
Writing \(e_4=(0,0,0,1)^T\), its four columns are exactly
\[
\begin{aligned}
J_{\bullet a}&=\frac{2r}{t}v+R_0+tR_1+t^2R_2+t^3R_3-it^4e_4,\\
J_{\bullet y}&=\frac2{t^2}v+\frac{14ir}{t}w+S_0+t^2e_4,\\
J_{\bullet z}&=\frac1{t^3}v+\frac2t w,\qquad
J_{\bullet w}=\frac1{t^2}w .
\end{aligned}\tag{FD7}
\]
These columns follow by differentiating FD1 before substituting
FD2. The independent certificate verifies both products of FD7
with FD4--5 to be the identity over
\(\mathbb Q(i)(t,r,A,B)\).

The two intermediate Jacobians have determinants
\[
 \det Dq=t^3,\qquad \det D\pi=-2t^3,\qquad \det J=-2.
 \tag{FD8}
\]
For the first formula, after the first two rows the diagonal
entries of the remaining triangular minor are \(t^3,t^2\);
the first two pivots are \(-t^{-2},-1\). For the second,
move the two coefficient columns \(A,B\) to the front, an
even permutation. The residual \(t,r\) block is
\(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}2t&-\kappa\\-2rt&r\kappa-t^2\end{smallmatrix}\right)\),
whose determinant is \(-2t^3\). The chain rule proves the
last equality for every \(t\ne0\). These parameter values
cover the original \(a\ne0\) chart by \(t=1/a\),
\(r=-y-i/a\), \(A=P_0\), \(B=P_2\). Polynomial continuation
therefore gives \(\det DP=-2\) everywhere in the original
affine source. No finite critical point is created here.

\section{The exact complex Puiseux branches}
Fix an actual target polynomial \(h_0(s)=As^4+s^3+Bs^2+Cs+D\)
with a finite root \(r_0\) of exact multiplicity \(m=2,3,4\).
For \(x=s-r_0\), its original coefficients give exactly
\[
 h_0(r_0+x)=c_2x^2+c_3x^3+c_4x^4,\qquad
 c_2=6Ar_0^2+3r_0+B,\quad c_3=4Ar_0+1,\quad c_4=A.
 \tag{FD9}
\]
The first nonzero coefficient is \(c_m\).
For \(m=4\), in particular, \(c_3=0\) forces
\(A=-1/(4r_0)\) and \(r_0\ne0\); these are consequences
of the retained cubic coefficient one, not free normalizations.
The specified unfolding is exactly
\[
 h_\varepsilon(s)=h_0(s)-\varepsilon,\qquad
 u_\varepsilon=u_0-\varepsilon e_D.
 \tag{FD10}
\]
Keep the actual complex \(c_m\ne0\). The analytic map
\[
 f(x)=x\left(\frac{h_0(r_0+x)}{c_mx^m}\right)^{1/m}
 \tag{FD11}
\]
uses the unique local root with value one at \(x=0\).
Its derivative is one there, so it has a local analytic inverse
\(g(w)=w+O(w^2)\). This follows, for example, by the complex
inverse-function theorem; its nonzero derivative is explicit.
Let \(\zeta_j=e^{2\pi ij/m}\), \(0\le j<m\), and use the
Puiseux parameter \(u\) with \(\varepsilon=c_mu^{2m}\).
Then all \(m\) local roots and both original factor signs are
given by the exact analytic branches
\[
\begin{aligned}
 r_j(u)&=r_0+g(\zeta_j u^2),\\
 t_{j,\pm}(u)&=\theta_{j,\pm}u^{m-1}a_j(u),\qquad
 \theta_{j,\pm}^2=mc_m\zeta_j^{m-1},\qquad
 \theta_{j,-}=-\theta_{j,+},
\end{aligned}\tag{FD12}
\]
where \(a_j(u)\) is the analytic square root with value one of
\[
 \frac{h_0'(r_j(u))}
                  {mc_m\zeta_j^{m-1}u^{2m-2}}.
\]
The quotient has analytic extension with value one. In particular
\[
\begin{aligned}
 r_j-r_0&=\zeta_ju^2+O(u^4),\\
 t_{j,\pm}&=\theta_{j,\pm}u^{m-1}(1+O(u^2)),\\
 \kappa_j=h_0''(r_j)&=K_j u^{2m-4}(1+O(u^2)),\qquad
 K_j=m(m-1)c_m\zeta_j^{m-2},\\
 t_{j,\pm}^2/\kappa_j&=\frac{\zeta_j}{m-1}u^2(1+O(u^2)).
\end{aligned}\tag{FD13}
\]
These formulas hold as complex \(u\to0\), on either sign
branch, with every phase in \(\theta_{j,\pm},K_j,\zeta_j\)
retained. For sufficiently small nonzero \(u\), both \(t\)
and \(\kappa\) are nonzero. All resulting
\(q(t_{j,\pm}(u),r_j(u),A,B)\) are actual original source
points, by FD2 and FD10--12. No claim about a different
target path is used.

\section{All phased forward and inverse exterior limits}
Use increasing coordinate subsets of \(\{1,2,3,4\}\), in
the order \(a,y,z,w\) in the domain and \(A,B,C,D\) in the
codomain. A superscript star on a coordinate wedge \(e_I^*\)
in the following matrices means its coordinate covector;
it is not a metric adjoint or a conjugation of the root.
Let \(v_0=v(r_0)\), \(w_0=w(r_0)\),
\(\ell_0=(r_0^4,r_0^2,r_0,1)\), and
\(\ell'_0=(4r_0^3,2r_0,1,0)\). Define
\[
\begin{aligned}
 b_3&=(1,-r_0,r_0^2,-r_0^4)^T
       &&\text{in the wedge order }123,124,134,234,\\
 \eta_3&=(r_0^4,r_0^3,-r_0^2/2,-1/2)
       &&\text{in that same wedge order}.
\end{aligned}
\]
Along each branch FD12 the complete matrix limits are
\[
\boxed{\begin{array}{c|c|c}
 r&\text{forward limit}&\text{inverse limit}\\\hline
 1&t^3J\longrightarrow v_0e_3^*
